===Chinese===
再引基佐的话：「社会属于*某一种排他的*权力，它容不下任何别的权力。凡属倾向不同的原则一概遭到禁绝。统治的原则绝不容许一个敌对原则在它身旁现身并施展影响。他们文明中这种单一性的印记，同样打在他们的文学与智识产品上。新近引入欧洲的那些印度文学巨著，仿佛全都出自同一副铸模。它们仿佛都是同一事实的结果、同一观念的表达。宗教与道德论说、历史传说、剧诗、史诗，全都带着同一副面目。在这些心智与想象的作品中闪耀的，正是我们在他们的生活与制度中发现的同一种单一与单调的品格。」就连希腊，纵有满座经典珍宝，也未能免于这种由排他而生的局限。

===Original===
But the course of empire moves one degree westward. Europe becomes the theater of the leading exponents of civilization, and here we have a *Race Problem*﻿—if, indeed, the confused jumble of races, the clash and conflict, the din and devastation of those stormy years can be referred to by so quiet and so dignified a term as “problem.” Complex and appalling it surely was. Goths and Huns, Vandals and Danes, Angles, Saxons, Jutes﻿—could any prophet foresee that a vestige of law and order, of civilization and refinement would remain after this clumsy horde of wild barbarians had swept over Europe?

===Chinese===
然而帝国的进程向西又移一度。欧洲成了文明主要代言者上演的剧场，于是在这里我们便有了一个*种族问题*——倘若那些风雷激荡的岁月里种族的混杂纠结、撞击与冲突、喧嚣与蹂躏，当真配得上「问题」这样一个安静而体面的字眼的话。它的确既复杂又骇人。哥特人与匈奴人、汪达尔人与丹麦人、盎格鲁人、撒克逊人、朱特人——这样一支笨拙的野蛮游群席卷欧洲之后，还能有哪位先知预见到，法律与秩序、文明与文雅竟会留得一脉残迹？

===Original===
“Where is somebody’ll give me some white for all this yellow?” cries one with his hands full of the gold from one of those magnificent monuments of antiquity which he and his tribe had just pillaged and demolished. Says the historian: “Their history is like a history of kites and crows.” Tacitus writes: “To shout, to drink, to caper about, to feel their veins heated and swollen with wine, to hear and see around them the riot of the orgy, this was the first need of the barbarians. The heavy human brute gluts himself with sensations and with noise.”

===Chinese===
「哪儿有人肯拿白的来，换我这一把黄的？」一个人嚷道，双手捧满了金子——那是刚被他和他部落的人洗劫、捣毁的某座古代宏伟纪念物里掏出来的金子。史家说：「他们的历史就像一部鸢与乌鸦的历史。」塔西佗（Tacitus）写道：「叫喊、狂饮、蹦跳，觉出自己的血管被酒烧得滚热胀起，耳闻目睹身边纵酒的喧嚣——这便是蛮人的第一需要。这沉重的人形兽只求在感官与喧嚣里饱餐一顿。」

===Original===
Taine describes them as follows:

===Chinese===
丹纳（Taine）把他们描写如下：

===Original===
“Huge white bodies, cool-blooded, with fierce blue eyes, reddish flaxen hair; ravenous stomachs, filled with meat and cheese, heated by strong drinks. Brutal drunken pirates and robbers, they dashed to sea in their two-sailed barks, landed anywhere, killed everything; and, having sacrificed in honor of their gods the tithe of all their prisoners, leaving behind the red light of their burning, went farther on to begin again.”

===Chinese===
「高大白皙的身体，冷凉的血液，凶悍的蓝眼睛，红黄的亚麻色头发；饕餮的肠胃，塞满了肉与干酪，又被烈酒烧得滚热。横暴的、醉醺醺的海盗与劫匪，驾着双帆的小船冲向大海，随处登岸，杀戮殆尽；把俘虏的什一拿来祭了他们的神之后，留下身后焚烧的红色火光，再往前行，从头来过。」

===Original===
A certain litany of the time reads: “From the fury of the Jutes, Good Lord deliver us.” “Elgiva, the wife of one of their kings,” says a chronicler of the time, “they hamstrung and subjected to the death she deserved;” and their heroes are frequently represented as tearing out the heart of their human victim and eating it while it still quivered with life.

===Chinese===
当时的一篇连祷文写道：「从朱特人的狂怒中，好主啊，求你拯救我们。」「埃尔吉娃（Elgiva），他们一位国王的妻子，」当时的编年史家说，「被他们砍断腿筋，受了她罪有应得的死刑」；而他们的英雄，还屡屡被描绘成从人牲胸膛里掏出心来，趁它犹自颤动着生命，便吞食下去。

===Original===
A historian of the time, quoted by Taine, says it was the custom to buy men and women in all parts of England and to carry them to Ireland for sale. The buyers usually made the women pregnant and took them to market in that condition to ensure a better price. “You might have seen,” continues the historian, “long files of young people of both sexes and of great beauty, bound with ropes and daily exposed for sale. They sold as slaves in this manner, their nearest relatives and even their own children.”

===Chinese===
一位为丹纳所引的当时史家说，在英格兰各处买进男女、贩往爱尔兰出售，是那时的风气。买主通常先使那些女人怀孕，再带她们上市，以确保卖得好价钱。「你本可以见到，」那位史家接着说，「一队队美貌出众的青年男女，被绳索捆着，天天摆出来售卖。他们就这样把自己的近亲、甚至自己的亲生儿女卖作奴隶。」

===Original===
What could civilization hope to do with such a swarm of sensuous, bloodthirsty vipers? Assimilation was horrible to contemplate. They will drag us to their level, quoth the culture of the times. Deportation was out of the question; and there was no need to talk of their emigrating. The fact is, the barbarians were in no hurry about moving. They didn’t even care to colonize. They had come to stay. And Europe had to grapple with her race problem till time and God should solve it.

===Chinese===
面对这样一大群耽于声色、嗜血成性的蝮蛇，文明还能指望什么？同化之事，思之可怖。当时的文教说道：他们要把我们拖到他们的水准上去。驱逐则无从谈起；更不必侈谈让他们自行迁走。事实是，蛮人根本无意挪窝。他们连殖民都懒得费心。他们是来久住的。于是欧洲只得同它的种族问题扭打周旋，直到时间与上帝把它解决为止。

===Original===
And how was it solved, and what kind of civilization resulted?

===Chinese===
那么，它后来是怎样解决的？结出的又是一种怎样的文明？

===Original===
Once more let us go to Guizot. “Take ever so rapid a glance,” says he, “at modern Europe and it strikes you at once as diversified, confused, and stormy. All the principles of social organization are found existing together within it; powers temporal, and powers spiritual, the theocratic, monarchic, aristocratic, and democratic elements, all classes of society *in a state of continual struggle* without any one having sufficient force to master the others and take sole possession of society.” Then as to the result of this conflict of forces: “Incomparably more rich and diversified than the ancient, European civilization has within it the promise of *perpetual progress*. It has now endured more than fifteen centuries and in all that time has been in a state of progression, not so rapidly as the Greek nor yet so ephemeral. While in other civilizations the exclusive domination of a principle (*or race*) led to tyranny, in Europe the diversity of social elements (*growing out of the contact of different races*) the incapability of any one to exclude the rest, gave birth to the **liberty** which now prevails. This inability of the various principles to exterminate one another compelled each to endure the others and made it necessary for them in order to live in common to enter into a sort of mutual understanding. Each consented to have only that part of civilization which equitably fell to its share. Thus, while everywhere else the predominance of one principle produced tyranny, the variety and warfare of the elements of European civilization gave birth to *reciprocity and liberty*.”

===Chinese===
让我们再一次求教于基佐。「哪怕以最匆匆的一瞥，」他说，「投向现代欧洲，它也立刻给你以一种多样、纷乱、风雷激荡的印象。社会组织的一切原则都在它内部并存：世俗之权与属灵之权，神权的、君主的、贵族的、民主的诸元素，社会的一切阶级，都*处于不断的斗争之中*，而没有任何一方拥有足够的力量制服其余、独占社会。」至于这场力量冲突的结果，他则说：「远比古代文明更丰富、更多样的欧洲文明，其内部含有*永久进步*的许诺。它至今已经历了十五个多世纪，而在此全部时间里一直处于进展之中——不及希腊那样迅疾，却也不那样短命。在别的文明里，一种原则（*或一个种族*）的排他统治导向专制；在欧洲，社会成分的多样（*由不同种族的接触而生*），以及任何一方都无力排除其余这一事实，则诞育了如今盛行的**自由**。各种原则既无力相互灭绝，便不得不彼此容忍，并且为了共同生活而不得不达成某种相互谅解。每一方都甘愿只取文明中公平地归于自己的那一部分。于是，在其余一切地方，一个原则的独尊都产生专制，而欧洲文明诸成分的多样与争战，却诞育了*互惠与自由*。」

===Original===
There is no need to quote further. This is enough to show that the law holds good in sociology as in the world of matter, *that equilibrium, not repression among conflicting forces is the condition of natural harmony, of permanent progress, and of universal freedom*. That exclusiveness and selfishness in a family, in a community, or in a nation is suicidal to progress. Caste and prejudice mean immobility. One race predominance means death. The community that closes its gates against foreign talent can never hope to advance beyond a certain point. Resolve to keep out foreigners and you keep out progress. Home talent develops its one idea and then dies. Like the century plant it produces its one flower, brilliant and beautiful it may be, but it lasts only for a night. Its forces have exhausted themselves in that one effort. Nothing remains but to wither and to rot.

===Chinese===
无须再引下去了。这已足以表明：这条法则在社会学中一如在物质世界中成立——*冲突力量之间的平衡、而非压制，才是自然谐和、永久进步与普遍自由的条件*。一个家庭、一个社群、一个民族，其排他与自私于进步是自戕之举。种姓与偏见意味着僵滞。一个种族的独尊意味着死亡。对外来人才闭门不纳的社群，永远休想越过某一点而再进。决意把外国人拒之门外，便是把进步拒之门外。本土的人才发育出它那一个观念，然后便死去。它像那百年一花的龙舌兰（century plant），纵然开出一朵花，辉煌美丽，也只灿然一夜。它的力量已在那一搏中用尽。剩下的唯有枯萎与腐烂。

===Original===
It was the Chinese wall that made China in 1800 AD the same as China in the days of Confucius. Its women have not even yet learned that they need not bandage their feet if they do not relish it. The world has rolled on, but within that wall the thoughts, the fashions, the art, the tradition, and the beliefs are those of a thousand years ago. Until very recently, the Chinese were wholly out of the current of human progress. They were like gray headed infants﻿—a man of eighty years with the concepts and imaginings of a babe of eight months. A civilization measured by thousands of years with a development that might be comprised within as many days﻿—arrested development due to exclusive living.

===Chinese===
正是那道万里长城，使公元 1800 年的中国同孔子时代的中国一模一样。中国的妇女甚至至今还不曾知道，倘若自己并不喜欢，本可以不缠足。世界滚滚向前，而那道墙之内，思想、风尚、艺术、传统与信仰，仍是一千年前的旧物。直到晚近，中国人还全然置身于人类进步的洪流之外。他们像一群白头的老婴——八十岁的人，怀抱着八个月婴儿的概念与想象。以数千年计的文明，其发展却可以收在同样的若干天之内——封闭自处所致的发育停滞。

===Original===
But European civilization, rich as it was compared to Asiatic types, was still not the consummation of the ideal of human possibilities. One more degree westward the hand on the dial points. In Europe there was conflict, but the elements crystallized out in isolated nodules, so to speak. Italy has her dominant principle, Spain hers, France hers, England hers, and so on. The proximity is close enough for interaction and mutual restraint, though the acting forces are at different points. To preserve the balance of power, which is nothing more than the equilibrium of warring elements, England can be trusted to keep an eye on her beloved step-relation-in-law, Russia﻿—and Germany no doubt can be relied on to look after France and some others. It is not, however, till the scene changes and America is made the theater of action, that the interplay of forces narrowed down to a single platform.

===Chinese===
然而欧洲文明虽比亚洲诸型丰饶，仍不是人类诸般可能性的理想的极致。表盘上的指针再向西移一度。在欧洲，冲突固然有之，但种种元素可以说是各自凝成了孤立的团块。意大利有她的主导原则，西班牙有她的，法国有她的，英国有她的，如此等等。各支力量虽作用于不同地点，彼此却近得足以相互作用、相互钳制。为维持均势——那不过是交战诸元素的平衡——尽可放心让英国盯住她那位亲爱的、又继又姻的亲戚俄国，而德国呢，毫无疑问也可以指望她照看法国和其他某几位。然而，直到场景一换、美洲成为行动的剧场，诸力量的交互作用才收拢到一个单一的台面上。

===Original===
Hither came Cavalier and Roundhead, Baptist and Papist, Quaker, Ritualist, Freethinker and Mormon, the conservative Tory, the liberal Whig, and the radical Independent﻿—the Spaniard, the Frenchman, the Englishman, the Italian, the Chinaman, the African, Swedes, Russians, Huns, Bohemians, Gypsies, Irish, Jews. Here surely was a seething cauldron of conflicting elements. Religious intolerance and political hatred, race prejudice and caste pride﻿—

===Chinese===
涌到这里来的，有保王党人与圆颅党人，浸信会徒与教皇派，贵格会徒、重仪文派、自由思想者与摩门教徒，保守的托利党人、开明的辉格党人与激进的独立派——有西班牙人、法兰西人、英格兰人、意大利人、中国人、非洲人，有瑞典人、俄罗斯人、匈奴人、波希米亚人、吉普赛人、爱尔兰人、犹太人。这里确乎是一口冲突元素翻腾鼎沸的大锅。宗教的不宽容与政治的仇恨，种族的偏见与种姓的骄矜——

===Original===
> “Double, double, toil and trouble;
 

 Fire burn and cauldron bubble.”

===Chinese===
> 「不惮辛劳不惮烦，
> 火烧釜滚泡沫翻。」（《麦克白》女巫谣）

===Original===
Conflict, Conflict, Conflict.

===Chinese===
冲突，冲突，冲突。

===Original===
America for Americans! This is the white man’s country! The Chinese must go, shrieks the exclusionist. Exclude the Italians! Colonize the blacks in Mexico or deport them to Africa. Lynch, suppress, drive out, kill out! America for Americans!

===Chinese===
美国属于美国人！这是白人的国家！排华者尖声叫嚷：中国人必须走开。把意大利人排除出去！把黑人殖民到墨西哥去，或者放逐到非洲去。私刑，压制，驱逐，杀绝！美国属于美国人！

===Original===
“*Who are Americans?*” comes rolling back from ten million throats. Who are to do the packing and delivering of the goods? Who are the home-folks and who are the strangers? Who are the absolute and original tenants in fee-simple?

===Chinese===
「*谁才是美国人？*」一千万条喉咙滚出回声。货物的打包与递送，该由谁来做？谁是坐地户，谁是外乡客？谁又是那绝对的、最初的完全产权（fee-simple）业主？

===Original===
The red men used to be owners of the soil﻿—but they are about to be pushed over into the Pacific Ocean. They, perhaps, have the best right to call themselves “Americans” by law of primogeniture. They are at least the oldest inhabitants of whom we can at present identify any traces. If early settlers from abroad merely are meant and it is only a question of squatters’ rights﻿—why, the Mayflower, a pretty venerable institution, landed in the year of Grace 1620, and the first delegation from Africa just one year ahead of that﻿—in 1619. The first settlers seem to have been almost as much mixed as we are on this point; and it does not seem at all easy to decide just what individuals we mean when we yell “America for the Americans.” At least the cleavage cannot be made by hues and noses, if we are to seek for the genuine F.F.V.’s as the inhabitants best entitled to the honor of that name.

===Chinese===
红种人本是这土地的主人——如今却快要被推进太平洋里去了。凭长子继承的法权，他们或许最有资格自称「美国人」。至少，他们是如今还能辨认得出痕迹的最古老的居民。倘若说的只是从海外来的早期移民，那不过是个垦居者权利的问题——那么好吧，「五月花号」这桩相当可敬的老古董，是在主历 1620 年登陆的，而从非洲来的第一个代表团，恰恰还比它早一年——1619 年。看来，最早的移民在这件事上几乎同我们一样是混了血的；当我们高喊「美国属于美国人」的时候，指的究竟是哪些个人，似乎也殊难断定。至少，这条界线不能凭肤色与鼻梁来划——倘若我们要寻找货真价实的「弗吉尼亚第一家族」（F.F.V.），把他们当作最配享有这个名号之荣的居民的话。

===Original===
The fact is this nation was foreordained to conflict from its incipiency. Its elements were predestined from their birth to an irrepressible clash followed by the stable equilibrium of opposition. Exclusive possession belongs to none. There never was a point in its history when it did. There was never a time since America became a nation when there were not more than one race, more than one party, more than one belief contending for supremacy. Hence no one is or can be supreme. All interests must be consulted, all claims conciliated. Where a hundred free forces are lustily clamoring for recognition and each wrestling mightily for the mastery, individual tyrannies must inevitably be chiselled down, individual bigotries worn smooth and malleable, individual prejudices either obliterated or concealed. America is not from choice more than of necessity republic in form and democratic in administration. The will of the majority must rule simply because no class, no family, no individual has ever been able to prove sufficient political legitimacy to impose their yoke on the country. All attempts at establishing oligarchy must be made by wheedling and cajoling, pretending that not supremacy but service is sought. The nearest approach to outspoken self-assertion is in the conciliatory tones of candid compromise. “I will let you enjoy that if you will not hinder me in the pursuit of this” has been the American sovereign’s home policy since his first Declaration of Independence was inscribed as his policy abroad. Compromise and concession, liberality and toleration were the conditions of the nation’s birth and are the sine qua non of its continued existence. A general amnesty and universal reciprocity are the only modus vivendi in a nation whose every citizen is his own king, his own priest and his own pope.

===Chinese===
事实是，这个国家从发轫之初便被预定要经历冲突。它的诸种成分生来便注定要有一场压抑不住的碰撞，随后归于对立力量的稳定平衡。排他的独占不属于任何人；历史上也从来不曾有过属于谁的时候。自美国立国以来，从来不曾有一个时刻不是多于一个种族、多于一个政党、多于一种信仰在争夺霸权。因此，没有谁现在是、也没有谁可能是至尊。一切利益都必须得到征询，一切诉求都必须得到安抚。在一个上百支自由力量都放声争着要承认、个个都奋力角着要霸权的地方，个人的专横必然要被凿平，个人的偏执必然要被磨得圆软顺服，个人的偏见则不是被磨灭，便是被藏起。美国之采共和之形、行民主之治，与其说出于选择，毋宁说出于必要。多数的意志之所以必须统治，只不过是因为无论哪个阶级、哪个家族、哪个人，都从未能证明自己有足够的政治合法性，把枷锁加于这个国家。一切建立寡头政治的图谋，都只能靠哄劝与甘言诱哄来进行，还得假装所求的不是统治而是服务。最接近坦率自我申张的，也已是坦诚妥协的委婉腔调。「倘若你不阻挠我追求这个，我便让你享用那个」——自第一篇《独立宣言》被铭刻为他的对外政策之日起，这便是美国这位君主的对内政策。妥协与让步、宽厚与宽容，是这个国家诞生的条件，也是它继续存在的必要条件（sine qua non）。在一个人人自为王、自为祭司、自为教皇的国度里，普遍的赦免与普遍的互惠，是唯一的相处之道（modus vivendi）。

===Original===
De Tocqueville, years ago, predicted that republicanism must fail in America. But if republicanism fails, America fails, and somehow I can not think this colossal stage was erected for a tragedy. I must confess to being an optimist on the subject of my country. It is true we are too busy making history, and have been for some years past, to be able to write history yet, or to understand and interpret it. Our range of vision is too short for us to focus and image our conflicts. Indeed Von Holtz, the clearest headed of calm spectators, says he doubts if the history of American conflict can be written yet even by a disinterested foreigner. The clashing of arms and the din of battle, the smoke of cannon and the heat of combat, are not yet cleared away sufficiently for us to have the judicial vision of historians. Our jottings are like newspaper reports written in the saddle, mid prancing steeds and roaring artillery.

===Chinese===
多年前，托克维尔（De Tocqueville）预言共和主义在美国必将失败。然而共和主义若败，美国便败；而我总不能相信，这座巨丽的舞台是为了上演一出悲剧而搭起来的。我必须承认，在有关我的国家的问题上，我是个乐观主义者。的确，我们忙于创造历史——这些年来一直如此——以致还腾不出手来书写历史，也还谈不上理解与阐释它。我们的视距太短，还无法把我们的冲突聚焦成像。的确，头脑最清醒的冷静旁观者冯·霍尔斯特（Von Holtz）就说过，他怀疑连一个置身事外的外国人，此刻也写不出美国冲突的历史。兵器的铿锵与战场的喧嚣，炮火的硝烟与鏖战的灼热，都还未散尽到足以让我们拥有史家的持平之明。我们的随手札记，就像骑在马背上写的新闻电讯——四周是腾跃的战马与轰鸣的炮队。

===Original===
But of one thing we may be sure: the God of battles is in the conflicts of history. The evolution of civilization is His care, eternal progress His delight. As the European was higher and grander than the Asiatic, so will American civilization be broader and deeper and closer to the purposes of the Eternal than any the world has yet seen. This the last page is to mark the climax of history, the bright consummate flower unfolding *charity toward all and malice toward none*﻿—the final triumph of universal reciprocity born of universal conflict with forces that cannot be exterminated. Here at last is an arena in which every agony has a voice and free speech. Not a spot where no wrong can exist, but where each feeblest interest can cry with Themistocles, “*Strike, but hear me!*” Here you will not see as in Germany women hitched to a cart with donkeys; not perhaps because men are more chivalrous here than there, but because woman can speak. Here labor will not be starved and ground to powder, because the laboring man can make himself heard. Here races that are weakest can, *if they so elect*, make themselves felt.

===Chinese===
但有一件事我们可以确信：万军之上帝就在历史的诸冲突之中。文明的演化是祂的眷顾，永恒的进步是祂的喜悦。正如欧洲文明高过、伟过亚洲文明，美国文明也必将比世上已见的任何文明更宽阔、更深沉、更切近永恒者的旨意。这最后一页将标出历史的极峰，那朵明亮完满之花将绽放出*对一切人仁爱，对任何人无恶意*——那是从与不可灭绝之力进行的普遍冲突中诞生的普遍互惠之最终胜利。这里终于有了一座竞技场：每一种苦难都有发言权，都有言论自由。这不是一块全无冤屈得以存在之地，而是一块连最微弱的利益也能同地米斯托克利（Themistocles）一道喊出「*打吧，但请听我说！*」的地方。在这里，你不会见到德国那样的情形——女人与驴子同驾一车；这或许不是因为这里的男人比那里的更有骑士风度，而是因为女人能够开口。在这里，劳动不会被饿毙、被碾成齑粉，因为劳动者能使自己的声音被人听见。在这里，最弱小的种族，*只要自己愿意*，也能使人感到它们的分量。

===Original===
The supremacy of one race﻿—the despotism of a class or the tyranny of an individual can not ultimately prevail on a continent held in equilibrium by such conflicting forces and by so many and such strong fibred races as there are struggling on this soil. Never in America shall one man dare to say as Germany’s somewhat bumptious emperor is fond of proclaiming: “There is only one master in the country and I am he. I shall suffer no other beside me. Only to God and my conscience am I accountable.” The strength of the opposition tones down and polishes off all such ugly excrescencies as that. “I am the State,” will never be proclaimed above a whisper on a platform where there is within arm’s length another just as strong, possibly stronger, who holds, or would like to hold that identical proposition with reference to himself. In this arena then is to be the last death struggle of political tyranny, of religions bigotry, and intellectual intolerance, of caste illiberality and class exclusiveness. And the last monster that shall be throttled forever methinks is race prejudice. Men will here learn that a race, as a family, may be true to itself without seeking to exterminate all others. That for the note of the feeblest there is room, nay a positive need, in the harmonies of God. That the principles of true democracy are founded in universal reciprocity, and that “A man’s a man” was written when God first stamped His own image and superscription on His child and breathed into his nostrils the breath of life. And I confess I can pray for no nobler destiny for my country than that it may be the stage, however far distant in the future, whereon these ideas and principles shall ultimately mature; and culminating here at whatever cost of production shall go forth hence to dominate the world.

===Chinese===
一个种族的至尊——一个阶级的专制或一个人的暴政——终究不能在这样一片大陆上得逞：这片大陆被如此相冲突的力量、被在这土壤上奋争的这么多、这么筋骨强韧的种族维持于平衡之中。在美国，永远不会有哪个人敢像德国那位颇为骄横的皇帝所喜欢宣称的那样说：「这个国家只有一个主人，那就是我。我不容任何人立于我侧。我只对上帝和我的良心负责。」反对力量的强度，会把诸如此类的丑陋赘疣磨钝、抛光。「朕即国家」，永远不会高出耳语半分——在一座舞台上一臂之遥的地方，还站着另一个同样强大、也许更强大的人，他也持有、或者也想把这同一条命题用到自己身上。因此，正是在这座竞技场上，将上演政治专制、宗教偏执、智识不宽容、种姓狭隘与阶级排他的最后殊死挣扎。而依我想，那最后一个被永远扼死的怪物，便是种族偏见。人们将在这里学到：一个种族，正如一个家族，尽可以忠于自身，而无须谋求灭绝其他一切；连最微弱的那个音符，在上帝的和声里也有位置——不但有位置，而且确乎必需；真民主的各项原则植根于普遍的互惠，而「人总是人」这几个字，早在上帝第一次把祂自己的形象与名号印在祂孩子身上、将生气吹进他鼻孔的时候，就已经写下了。我承认，对于我的国家，我祈求不出比这更高贵的命运了：愿它成为——哪怕远在渺茫的未来——这些观念与原则终将成熟的舞台；愿它们在此臻于极峰——无论付出何等代价——然后从这里出发，去宰制世界。

===Original===
Methought I saw a mighty conflagration, plunging and heaving, surging and seething, smoking and rolling over this American continent. Strong men and wise men stand helpless in mute consternation. Empty headed babblers add the din of their bray to the crashing and crackling of the flames. But the hungry flood rolls on. The air is black with smoke and cinders. The sky is red with lurid light. Forked tongues of fiery flame dart up and lick the pale stars, and seem to laugh at men’s feebleness and frenzy. As I look on I think of Schiller’s sublime characterization of fire: “Frightful becomes this God-power, when it snatches itself free from fetters and stalks majestically forth on its own career﻿—the free daughter of Nature.” Ingenuity is busy with newly patented snuffers all warranted to extinguish the flame. The street gamin with a hooked wire pulls out a few nuggets that chanced to be lying on the outskirts where they were cooked by the heat; and gleefully cries “What a nice fire to roast my chestnuts,” and like little Jack Horner, “what a nice boy am I!”

===Chinese===
我仿佛看见一场熊熊的大火，在这片美洲大陆上俯仰奔突、翻涌沸腾，卷着浓烟滚滚而过。强壮的人与睿智的人束手无策，怔立无言。脑中空空的聒噪者，又把他们的嘶鸣添进烈焰的爆裂与噼啪声中。但那饥饿的火流兀自滚滚向前。空气因烟与烬而乌黑，天空因惨红的光而赤亮。火舌分叉，蹿起舔着苍白的星，仿佛在嘲笑人的孱弱与狂乱。我看着看着，想起席勒（Schiller）对火的崇高的描摹：「这神一般的力量，一旦自行挣脱锁链，庄严阔步地走上自己的途程——这自然之自由的女儿——便成为可怖之物。」机巧之士正忙着用新领专利的烛剪，个个保准能剪灭这火焰。街头的顽童用带钩的铁丝，从火场边缘扒出几块恰巧落在那儿、被热气烘熟的宝块，欢天喜地地叫道：「多好的火呀，正好烤我的栗子！」又像小杰克·霍纳（Jack Horner）那样念叨：「我是多乖的孩子呀！」

===Original===
Meantime this expedient, that expedient, the other expedient is suggested by thinkers and theorizers hoping to stifle the angry, roaring, devouring demon and allay the mad destruction.

===Chinese===
与此同时，思想家与空论家们献上这个权宜之计、那个权宜之计、第三个权宜之计，指望闷死那愤怒咆哮、吞噬一切的恶魔，平息这场疯狂的毁灭。

===Original===
> “Wehe wenn sie losgelassen,
 

 Wachsend ohne Widerstand,
 

 Durch die volkbelebten Gassen
 

 Walzt den ungeheuren Brand!”

===Chinese===
> 「祸哉，一旦它挣脱羁绊，
> 无遮无拦地疯长，
> 滚过人烟鼎沸的街巷，
> 卷起滔天的火浪！」（席勒《钟之歌》）

===Original===
But the strength of the Omnipotent is in it. The hand of God is leading it on. It matters not whether you and I in mad desperation cast our quivering bodies into it as our funeral pyre; or whether, like the street urchins, we pull wires to secure the advantage of the passing moment. We can neither help it nor hinder; only

===Chinese===
然而全能者的力量就在这火中。上帝的手正引它前行。你我是在疯狂的绝望里把自己颤抖的身躯投进去、作自己的火葬柴堆，还是像街头小儿那样拉扯铁丝、捞取过眼一时的好处，都无关宏旨。我们既帮不了它，也拦不住它；我们只有——

===Original===
> “Let thy gold be cast in the furnace,
 

 Thy red gold, precious and bright.
 

 Do not fear the hungry fire
 

 With its caverns of burning light.”

===Chinese===
> 「把你的金子投进炉中，
> 你那赤红、贵重而明亮的金子。
> 不要惧怕那饥饿的火，
> 不要惧怕它一窟一窟燃烧的光。」

===Original===
If it takes the dearest idol, the pet theory or the darling ‘ism,’ the pride, the selfishness, the prejudices, the exclusiveness, the bigotry and intolerance, the conceit of self, of race, or of family superiority﻿—nay, if it singe from thee thy personal gratifications in thy distinction by birth, by blood, by sex﻿—everything﻿—and leave thee nothing but thy naked manhood, solitary and unadorned﻿—let them go﻿—let them go!

===Chinese===
倘若这火取走你最亲爱的偶像，你宠爱的理论或心肝的什么「主义」，你的骄傲、你的自私、你的偏见、你的排他、你的偏执与不宽容，你对于自己、对于种族、对于门第高人一等的自负——不但如此，倘若它燎去你因出身、血统、性别之别而得的私人沾沾自喜——一切的一切——只留给你一身赤裸的人格，孑然无饰——那就由它们去吧——由它们去吧！

===Original===
> “And thy gold shall return more precious,
 

 Free from every spot and stain,
 

 For gold must be tried by fire.”

===Chinese===
> 「而你的金子将更贵重地归来，
> 洗净一切斑点与污痕，
> 因为金子必经火炼。」

===Original===
And the heart of nations must be tried by pain; and their polish, their true culture must be wrought in through conflict.

===Chinese===
而诸民族的心也必经苦痛的试炼；它们的润泽、它们真正的文化，也必经由冲突才锤炼得进去。

===Original===
Has America a Race Problem?

===Chinese===
美国有种族问题吗？

===Original===
Yes.

===Chinese===
有。

===Original===
What are you going to do about it?

===Chinese===
那你打算怎么办？

===Original===
Let it alone and mind my own business. It is God’s problem and He will solve it in time. It is deeper than Gehenna. What can you or I do!

===Chinese===
随它去，我只管自家的本分事。这是上帝的问题，祂自会适时解决。它比地狱还深。你我又能做些什么呢！

===Original===
Are there then no duties and special lines of thought growing out of the present conditions of this problem?

===Chinese===
那么，从这一问题眼下的状况之中，便生不出任何本分与特殊的思路来了吗？

===Original===
Certainly there are. Imprimis; let every element of the conflict see that it represent a positive force so as to preserve a proper equipoise in the conflict. No shirking, no skulking, no masquerading in another’s uniform. Stand by your guns. And be ready for the charge. The day is coming, and now is, when America must ask each citizen not “who was your grandfather and what the color of his cuticle,” but “*What can you do?*” Be ready each individual element﻿—each race, each class, each family, each man to reply “*I engage to undertake an honest man’s share*.”

===Chinese===
当然是有的。第一（imprimis）；务求冲突中的每一种成分都确保自己代表一股正面的力量，好在这场冲突中维持恰当的均势。不逃避，不躲闪，不穿别人的号衣冒名顶替。守住你们的炮位。准备迎接冲锋。时候将到，如今就是了：美国必问每一位公民的，不是「你的祖父是谁、他的表皮是什么颜色」，而是「*你能做什么？*」愿每一种个体成分——每个种族、每个阶级、每个家族、每个人——都准备好回答：「*我保证尽一个诚实人应尽的一份。*」

===Original===
God and time will work the problem. You and I are only to stand for the quantities *at their best*, which he means us to represent.

===Chinese===
上帝与时间自会解开这道题。你我只需去代表祂要我们代表的那些量，而且要*处于最佳状态*（at their best）。

===Original===
Above all, for the love of humanity stop the mouth of those learned theorizers, the expedient mongers, who come out annually with their new and improved method of getting the answer and clearing the slate: amalgamation, deportation, colonization and all the other ations that were ever devised or dreamt of. If Alexander wants to be a god, let him; but don’t have Alexander hawking his patent plan for universal deification. If all could or would follow Alexander’s plan, just the niche in the divine cosmos meant for man would be vacant. And we think that men have a part to play in this great drama no less than gods, and so if a few are determined to be white﻿—amen, so be it; but don’t let them argue as if there were no part to be played in life by black men and black women, and as if to become white were the sole specific and panacea for all the ills that flesh is heir to﻿—the universal solvent for all America’s irritations. And again, if an American family of whatever condition or hue takes a notion to reside in Africa or in Mexico, or in the isles of the sea, it is most un-American for any power on this continent to seek to gainsay or obstruct their departure; but on the other hand, no power or element of power on this continent, least of all a self-constituted tribunal of “recent arrivals,” possesses the right to begin figuring beforehand to calculate what it would require *to send* ten millions of citizens, whose ancestors have wrought here from the planting of the nation, to the same places at so much per head﻿—at least till someone has consulted those heads.

===Chinese===
首要的一件事：看在人类的份上，堵住那些博学的理论家、那些贩卖权宜之计的商贩的嘴吧——他们年年都端出一套新改良的求答案、清账板的办法：混合（amalgamation）、驱逐（deportation）、殖民（colonization），还有历来被人设计过、梦想过的一切其他「什么ation」。倘若亚历山大想当神，由他去；可别让亚历山大沿街兜售他那套人人封神的专利方案。倘若人人都能、都肯照亚历山大的方案办，神圣宇宙中专为人类留出的那个位置就要空缺了。而我们认定，在这出大戏里，人所要扮演的角色并不亚于诸神；所以，倘若真有少数人铁了心要做白人——阿门，那就这样吧；可别让他们的立论显得仿佛黑皮肤的男人和女人在人生中没有戏可演，仿佛变成白人是肉身所承一切疾患的唯一特效药与万应灵丹——是消解美国一切创痒的万能溶剂。再者，倘若一个无论境况如何、无论何种肤色的美国家庭起意要定居非洲或墨西哥、或四海中的岛屿，这片大陆上的任何权力若想否认或阻挠他们的离开，那是最不合美国精神的；但反过来说，这片大陆上的任何权力或权力成分——尤其是一个由「新来乍到者」自行加冕的裁判所——也决不拥有这样的权利：预先开始盘算，要多少开销才能把一千万公民「送」到同样那些地方去、按人头若干计价——这些公民的祖先自这个国家奠基之日起便在此劳作——至少，也总得先有人问过那些脑袋。

===Original===
We would not deprecate the fact, then, that America has a Race Problem. It is guaranty of the perpetuity and progress of her institutions, and insures the breadth of her culture and the symmetry of her development. More than all, let us not disparage the factor which the Negro is appointed to contribute to that problem. America needs the Negro for ballast if for nothing else. His tropical warmth and spontaneous emotionalism may form no unseemly counterpart to the cold and calculating Anglo-Saxon. And then his instinct for law and order, his inborn respect for authority, his inaptitude for rioting and anarchy, his gentleness and cheerfulness as a laborer, and his deep-rooted faith in God will prove indispensable and invaluable elements in a nation menaced as America is by anarchy, socialism, communism, and skepticism poured in with all the jail birds from the continents of Europe and Asia. I believe with our own Dr. Crummell that “the Almighty does not preserve, rescue, and build up a lowly people merely for ignoble ends.” And the historian of American civilization will yet congratulate this country that she has had a Race Problem and that descendants of the black race furnished one of its largest factors.

===Chinese===
因此，我们不愿对美国有其种族问题这一事实妄加訾议。它是她的各项制度得以永续、得以进步的保证，也确保了她文化的广度与她发展的匀称。尤为要紧的是，切莫轻看黑人（Negro）受命为这道题贡献的那个因子。哪怕别无所取，美国也需要黑人充当压舱物。他那热带的暖热与发自天性的深情，与冷面精算的盎格鲁-撒克逊人正可构成一种不失体统的对应。何况他对法律与秩序的本能、他对权威与生俱来的敬重、他之于作乱与无政府状态的不谙其道、他作为劳动者的温良与乐观、他对上帝根深蒂固的信仰，在一个正像美国这样、被随同欧亚两洲成群囚鸟一并倾入的无政府主义、社会主义、共产主义与怀疑论所威胁的国家里，都将证明是不可或缺、无可估价的元素。我与我们自己的克拉梅尔博士（Dr. Crummell）同样相信：「全能者保存、拯救并扶起一个卑微的民族，绝不会仅仅为了卑贱的目的。」而美国文明的史家，终有一日要向这个国家道贺：庆幸她有过一个种族问题，庆幸黑人种族的后裔为这道题供出了其中最大的因子之一。

### One Phase of American Literature / 美国文学之一面

===Original===
For nations as for individuals, a product, to be worthy the term literature, must contain something characteristic and sui generis.

===Chinese===
对一个民族一如对个人，一部作品要配得上文学之名，必得含有某种独特的、自成一格（sui generis）的气质。

===Original===
So long as America remained a mere English colony, drawing all her life and inspiration from the mother country, it may well be questioned whether there was such a thing as American literature. “Who ever reads an American book?” it was scornfully asked in the eighteenth century. Imitation is the worst of suicides; it cuts the nerve of originality and condemns to mediocrity: and ’twas not till the pen of our writers was dipped in the lifeblood of their own nation and pictured out its own peculiar heartthrobs and agonies that the world cared to listen. The nightingale and the skylark had to give place to the mockingbird, the bobolink and the whippoorwill, the heather and the bluebells of Britain, to our own goldenrod and daisy; the insular and monarchic customs and habits of thought of old England must develop into the broader, looser, freer swing of democratic America, before her contributions to the world of thought could claim the distinction of individuality and gain an appreciative hearing.

===Chinese===
当美利坚还只是英格兰的一块殖民地、她全部的生命与灵感皆汲取自母国之时，世上究竟有没有美国文学这回事，原是大可怀疑的。「谁曾读过一本美国人写的书？」十八世纪里有人如是冷嘲热讽地发问。模仿是最厉害的自戕：它斩断独创力的筋络，判人终身平庸。直到我们的作家把笔尖蘸入自己民族的血脉，描画出它独有的心搏与创痛，世界才肯侧耳倾听。夜莺与云雀须让位给反舌鸟、食米鸟与三声夜鹰，不列颠的石楠与风铃草须让位给我们自己的一枝黄花与雏菊；旧英格兰那岛国的、君主制的习俗与思维定式，必须发展成民主美利坚更开阔、更宽松、更自由的气度，她献给思想世界的成果方能享有个性独具的尊荣，赢得识者的垂听。

===Original===
And so our writers have succeeded in becoming national and representative in proportion as they have from year to year entered more and more fully, and more and more sympathetically, into the distinctive life of their nation, and endeavored to reflect and picture its homeliest pulsations and its elemental components. And so in all the arts, as men have gradually come to realize that

===Chinese===
于是，我们的作家正是逐年愈加充分、愈加体贴地融入本民族独特的生活，努力映现并描画其最家常的脉动与最本元的成分，才在这一比例上成功地成为民族的作家、时代的代言。于是，在一切艺术之中，正如人们渐渐领悟到的那样——

===Original===
> Nothing useless is or low
 Each thing in its place is best,

===Chinese===
> 「万物无一是无用，无一是卑微，
>  各安其位即是至善，」

===Original===
and have wrought into their products, lovingly and impartially and reverently, every type, every tint, every tone that they felt or saw or heard, just to that degree have their expressions, whether by pen or brush or rhythmic cadence, adequately and simply given voice to the thought of Nature around them. No man can prophesy with another’s parable. For each of us truth means merely the representation of the sensations and experiences of our personal environment, colored and vivified﻿—fused into consistency and crystallized into individuality in the crucible of our own feelings and imaginations. The mind of genius is merely the brook, picturing back its own tree and bush and bit of sky and cloud ensparkled by individual salts and sands and rippling motion. And paradoxical as it may seem, instead of making us narrow and provincial, this trueness to one’s habitat, this appreciative eye and ear for the tints and voices of one’s own little wood serves but to usher us into the eternal galleries and choruses of God. It is only through the unclouded perception of our tiny “part” that we can come to harmonize with the “stupendous whole,” and in order to do this our sympathies must be finely attuned and quick to vibrate under the touch of the commonplace and vulgar no less than at the hand of the elegant and refined. Nothing natural can be wholly unworthy; and we do so at our peril, if, what God has cleansed we presume to call common or unclean. Nature’s language is not writ in cipher. Her notes are always simple and sensuous, and the very meanest recesses and commonest byways are fairly deafening with her sermons and songs. It is only when we ourselves are out of tune through our pretentiousness and self-sufficiency, or are blinded and rendered insensate by reason of our foreign and unnatural “cultivation” that we miss her meanings and inadequately construe her multiform lessons.

===Chinese===
——把每一种类型、每一重色泽、每一记声调，凡他们所感、所见、所闻的，都怀着挚爱、公允与敬畏织入自己的作品，恰恰到这个程度，他们的表现——无论凭借笔墨、画刷，还是节奏的抑扬——才恰切而单纯地道出了周遭自然的思绪。没有人能借他人的寓言作预言。对我们每个人而言，真理不过是身畔环境的感觉与经验的再现，经我们自身情感与想象的坩埚点染活化——熔铸成一致，凝结成个性。天才的心智不过是一道溪流，映照出自家的树与灌木、自家的那一角天空与云影，又被独有的盐砂与粼粼水波缀上闪光。而看似悖谬的是，这种对自身栖居之所的忠实，这种对自己小小林莽的色泽与天籁那赏识的眼与倾听的耳，非但不会使我们偏狭乡土，反而恰似仪仗，把我们引进上帝的永恒画廊与永恒合唱。唯有透过对自家微小「部分」的澄明观照，我们方能与那「浩瀚整体」谐和相应；而要做到这一点，我们的同情必须调校得极为精微，无论抚过它的是平凡俚俗之手，还是典雅精致之手，都能同样迅捷地颤动。凡属自然之物，没有一样全然卑不足道；上帝所洁净的，我们若僭称之为凡俗不洁，便是自招危殆。自然的语言并非以密码写成。她的音符永远朴素而诉诸感官，连最卑陋的隅角与最寻常的僻径，也震响着她的布道与歌唱，声势几乎震聋人的耳朵。唯有当我们自己因自负与自足而走了调，或因那外来的、不自然的「教养」而目盲心顽，我们才会错失她的意旨，不能充分解读她形形色色的功课。

===Original===
For two hundred and fifty years there was in the American commonwealth a great *silent* factor. Though in themselves simple and unique their offices were those of the barest utility. Imported merely to be hewers of wood and drawers of water, no artist for many a generation thought them worthy the sympathetic study of a model. No Shakespeare arose to distil from their unmatched personality and unparalleled situations the exalted poesy and crude grandeur of an immortal Caliban. Distinct in color, original in temperament, simple and unconventionalized in thought and action their spiritual development and impressionability under their novel environment would have furnished, it might seem, as interesting a study in psychology for the poetic pen, as would the gorges of the Yosemite to the inspired pencil. Full of vitality and natural elasticity, the severest persecution and oppression could not kill them out or even sour their temper. With massive brawn and indefatigable endurance they wrought under burning suns and chilling blasts, in swamps and marshes﻿—they cleared the forests, tunneled mountains, threaded the land with railroads, planted, picked and ginned the cotton, produced the rice and the sugar for the markets of the world. Without money and without price they poured their hearts’ best blood into the enriching and developing of this country. *They wrought but were silent.*

===Chinese===
二百五十年来，美利坚的国体之中始终存在一个巨大的*沉默的*因子。他们本身单纯而独特，其职分却是最赤裸的实用。他们被掳入这个国度，本只做劈柴挑水的人，多少代人里没有一个艺术家认为他们配得上充当模特、值得同情地加以研究。没有一位莎士比亚起来，从他们无与伦比的个性与史无前例的境遇中，蒸馏出一个不朽的凯列班（Caliban）那高华的诗情与粗粝的壮美。肤色迥异，性情原创，思想与行动质朴而不落俗套，他们在新奇环境之下的精神发展与感受力，本可以为诗人的笔端提供一门不逊于约塞米蒂的峡谷之于灵感画笔的有趣心理学课题。他们生命力充沛，天性柔韧，最严酷的迫害与压迫既不能把他们斩尽杀绝，甚至连他们的性情也未能使之酸败。他们以伟岸的筋骨与不倦的耐力，在烈日与寒风之下、在沼泽与泥淖之中劳作——他们清除了森林，凿穿了山岭，用铁路贯穿大地，种植、采摘、轧棉，为世界市场生产稻米与食糖。他们不计金钱、不索代价，把心中最赤诚的鲜血倾注于这个国家的富庶与开发。*他们劳作着，却沉默着。*

===Original===
The most talked about of all the forces in this diversified civilization, they seemed the great American fact, the one objective reality, on which scholars sharpened their wits, at which orators and statesmen fired their eloquence, and from which, after so long a time, authors, with varied success and truthfulness have begun at last to draw subjects and models. Full of imagination and emotion, their sensuous pictures of the “New Jerusalem,” “the golden slippers,” “the long white robe,” “the pearly gates,” etc., etc., seem fairly to steam with tropical luxuriance and naive abandon. The paroxysms of religious fervor into which this simple-minded, childlike race were thrown by the contemplation of Heaven and rest and freedom, would have melted into sympathy and tender pity if not into love, a race less cold and unresponsive than the one with which they were thrown in closest contact. There was something truly poetic in their weird moanings, their fitful gleams of hope and trust, flickering amidst the darkness of their wailing helplessness, their strange sad songs, the half coherent ebullitions of souls in pain, which become, the more they are studied, at once the wonder and the despair of musical critics and imitators. And if one had the insight and the simplicity to gather together, to digest and assimilate these original lispings of an unsophisticated people while they were yet close﻿—so close﻿—to nature and to nature’s God, there is material here, one might almost believe, as rich, as unhackneyed, as original and distinctive as ever inspired a Homer, or a Caedmon or other simple genius of a people’s infancy and lisping childhood.

===Chinese===
在这个多元的文明里，他们是众口谈论最多的力量，俨然是美国的大事实、唯一的客观实在：学者们在他们身上磨砺才思，演说家与政治家对着他们纵骋辞令，而历经如此漫长的岁月之后，作家们也终于开始从他们身上取材取样，成败不一，虚实互见。他们富于想象与情感，他们对「新耶路撒冷」「金色的鞋」「白长袍」「珍珠门」等等的感官画境，简直蒸腾着热带的繁茂与天真的恣肆。这个头脑单纯、孩子气的种族，一冥想到天堂、安息与自由，便会被抛入宗教狂热的阵发之中；倘若与之朝夕相处的那个种族不是那么冷硬麻木，这些阵发本可以融化出同情与温柔的怜惜——倘若不是爱的话。他们怪异的呜咽、他们忽明忽灭的希望与信赖的微光——在孤苦无告的哀号的黑暗中闪烁——他们奇异而忧伤的歌谣，那些痛苦灵魂半连贯的迸涌，其中自有一种真正诗意的东西；这些歌谣越是被人研究，便越是令音乐批评家与摹仿者既叹为奇观、又绝望无从。倘若有人具备那样的眼力与那份单纯，能趁这个未经雕琢的民族还贴近——如此贴近——自然与自然之上帝的时候，把这些原初的牙牙之语收集起来，加以消化汲取，那么这里的材料，人们几乎可以相信，其丰饶、其未经斧凿、其原创与独特，足以激发一位荷马（Homer）、一位凯德蒙（Caedmon），或任何一个民族的襁褓期与牙牙学语的童年所诞生的质朴天才。

===Original===
In the days of their bitterest persecution, their patient endurance and Christian manliness inspired *Uncle Tom’s Cabin*, which revolutionized the thought of the world on the subject of slavery and at once placed its author in the front rank of the writers of her country and age. Here at last was a work which England could not parallel. Here was a work indigenous to American soil and characteristic of the country﻿—a work which American forces alone could have produced. The subject was at once seen to be fresh and interesting to the world as well as national and peculiar to America; and so it has since been eagerly cultivated by later writers with widely varying degrees of fitness and success.

===Chinese===
在他们受迫害最惨烈的日子里，他们坚忍的承受与基督徒的丈夫气概激发了《汤姆叔叔的小屋》（*Uncle Tom's Cabin*），那本书扭转了全世界关于奴隶制问题的思想，并使作者一跃而居其国度与时代作家之首列。这终于是一部英格兰无法比肩的作品。这是一部土生土长于美利坚土壤、带有这个国度之特征的作品——一部唯有美国的力量才能产生的作品。这个题材一举被世人看出既新鲜有趣，又是美国所特有、具有民族性的；自那以后，后来的作家们便热切地耕耘于斯，造诣与成就参差不一。

===Original===
By a rough classification, authors may be separated into two groups: first, those in whom the artistic or poetic instinct is uppermost﻿—those who write to please﻿—or rather who write because *they* please; who simply paint what they see, as naturally, as instinctively, and as irresistibly as the bird sings﻿—with no thought of an audience﻿—singing because it loves to sing﻿—singing because God, nature, truth sings through it. For such writers, to be true to themselves and true to Nature is the only canon. They cannot warp a character or distort a fact in order to prove a point. They have nothing to prove. All who care to, may listen while they make the woods resound with their glad sweet carolling; and the listeners may draw their own conclusions as to the meaning of the cadences of this minor strain, or that hushed and almost awful note of rage or despair. And the myriad-minded multitude attribute their myriad-fold impressions to the myriad-minded soul by which they have severally been enchanted, each in his own way according to what he brings to the witching auditorium. But the singer sings on with his hat before his face, unmindful, it may be unconscious, of the varied strains reproduced from him in the multitudinous echoes of the crowd. Such was Shakespeare, such was George Eliot, such was Robert Browning. Such, in America, was Poe, was Bryant, was Longfellow; and such, in his own degree perhaps, is Mr. Howells.

===Chinese===
粗略分类，作家可分为两类：第一类是艺术本能或诗性本能占上风的人——为愉悦而写的人——或者说，因为*他们自己*乐于写而写的人；他们只是描画所见，像鸟儿歌唱一样自然、一样本能、一样不可遏止——全不想着有无听众——歌唱只因它爱唱——歌唱因为上帝、自然、真理借它而唱。对这样的作家，忠于自己、忠于自然是唯一的法度。他们不能为证明一个论点而歪曲一个人物或扭曲一个事实。他们没有什么要证明的。谁愿意听，谁便听着他们让林间回荡起欢畅甜美的啭鸣；听者尽可自行断定，这小调的抑扬、那压低的、近乎可畏的愤怒或绝望之音，各有什么含义。而心思万千的众生，把千姿百态的印象归诸那令他们各自着迷的万千心魂，每人依自己带进这座迷人剧场的一切，各得其乐。但歌者帽檐遮着脸继续唱下去，浑然不觉——也许是全然不知——人群层层的回声从他身上复录出的种种声调。莎士比亚（Shakespeare）是这样，乔治·艾略特（George Eliot）是这样，罗伯特·勃朗宁（Robert Browning）也是这样。在美利坚，坡（Poe）是这样，布赖恩特（Bryant）是这样，朗费罗（Longfellow）是这样；而豪威尔斯（Howells）先生，或许在他自己的分际上，也是这样。

===Original===
In the second group belong the preachers﻿—whether of righteousness or unrighteousness﻿—all who have an idea to propagate, no matter in what form their talent enables them to clothe it, whether poem, novel, or sermon﻿—all those writers with a purpose or a lesson, who catch you by the buttonhole and pommel you over the shoulder till you are forced to give assent in order to escape their vociferations; or they may lure you into listening with the soft music of the siren’s tongue﻿—no matter what the expedient to catch and hold your attention, they mean to fetter you with their one idea, whatever it is, and make you, if possible, ride their hobby. In this group I would place Milton in much of his writing, Carlyle in all of his, often our own Whittier, the great reformer-poet, and Lowell; together with such novelists as E. P. Roe, Bellamy, Tourgée and some others.

===Chinese===
第二类是布道者——无论布的是义还是不义——一切有观念要传播的人，无论其才具使他们能以何种形式——诗歌、小说或讲章——来负载它；一切有宗旨或有教训的作家，他们揪住你的衣扣，捶打你的肩头，直到你为了躲开他们的聒噪不得不点头称是；他们也可能用塞壬之舌的柔音曼语诱你倾听——无论用什么权宜之计抓住并攥住你的注意力，他们都要用他们那唯一的观念把你锁住，并且如有可能，让你也骑上他们的癖好。这一类里，我要放进弥尔顿（Milton）的大部分作品、卡莱尔（Carlyle）的全部、常见于我们的伟大改革诗人惠蒂埃（Whittier）以及洛威尔（Lowell）；还有诸如 E·P·罗（E. P. Roe）、贝拉姆（Bellamy）、图尔吉（Tourgée）等小说家和另外几位。

===Original===
Now in my judgment writings of the first class will be the ones to withstand the ravages of time. “Isms” have their day and pass away. New necessities arise with new conditions and the emphasis has to be shifted to suit the times. No finite mind can grasp and give out the whole circle of truth. We do well if we can illuminate just the tiny arc which we occupy and should be glad that the next generation will not need the lessons we try so assiduously to hammer into this. In the evolution of society, as the great soul of humanity builds it “more lofty chambers,” the old shell and slough of didactic teaching must be left behind and forgotten. The world for instance has outgrown, I suspect, those passages of *Paradise Lost* in which Milton makes the Almighty Father propound the theology of a seventeenth century Presbyterian. But a passage like the one in which Eve with guileless innocence describes her first sensations on awaking into the world is as perennial as man.

===Chinese===
依我的判断，第一类作品才是经得起时间侵蚀的。「主义」们各领风骚一时，随即消逝。新的境况催生新的需要，重心必须随时移易以合时宜。没有一颗有限的心智能把握并输出真理的全圆。我们若能照亮自己所据的那一小段弧光，便已尽责，并且应当庆幸：下一代将不再需要我们如此勤勉地敲进这一代的那些功课。在社会的演进之中，当人类伟大的灵魂为它「筑起更崇高的居室」时，说教的老壳与旧蜕必须被抛下、被遗忘。譬如说，我怀疑世人已经长过了《失乐园》（*Paradise Lost*）中弥尔顿让全能天父宣讲十七世纪长老会神学的那些段落。但像下面这一段——夏娃以毫无心机的天真，描述自己初醒入世时的最初感受——却与人一样长青：

===Original===
> “That day I oft remember, when from sleep
 I first awaked and found myself reposed
 Under a shade on flowers, much wondering where
 And what I was, whence thither brought and how.
 Not distant far from thence a murmuring sound
 Of waters issued from a cave, and spread
 Into a liquid plain, then stood unmoved
 Pure as the expanse of Heaven;
 I thither went
 With unexperienced thought and laid me down
 On the green bank, to look into the clear
 Smooth lake that to me seemed another sky.
 As I bent down to look, just opposite
 A shape within the watery gleam appeared,
 Bending to look on me; I started back,
 It started back; but pleased I soon returned,
 Pleased it returned as soon with answering looks
 Of sympathy and love; there I had fixed
 Mine eyes till now﻿—and pined with vain desire,
 Had not a voice thus warned me.

===Chinese===
> 「那一天我时常记起：当我初从
>  睡梦中醒来，发觉自己安卧在
>  浓荫下的花丛之上，惊疑自己身在何处、
>  是什么，从何而来，又如何被带至此地。
>  不远处有一道潺潺水声
>  自岩洞涌出，漫流成
>  一泓明湖，静停不动，
>  清纯如同天宇的浩瀚；
>  我便循声而去，
>  怀着未经世事的心思，躺卧在
>  青草岸边，俯望那清澈
>  平滑的湖水——在我看来恍若另一重天空。
>  当我俯身下望，恰在对面，
>  水光潋滟中现出一个形影，
>  也正俯身望我；我惊退，
>  它也惊退；但我旋即欢喜地回身，
>  它也欢喜地随即回望，眼中
>  流露着怜爱与柔情；我的目光
>  便在那里停驻——为虚妄的渴慕而憔悴，
>  若不是有一个声音这样警告我：

===Original===
 ‘What thou seest,
 What there thou seest, fair creature, is thyself;
 With thee it came and goes; but follow me,
 And I will bring thee where no shadow stays
 Thy coming and thy soft embraces.’

===Chinese===
>  『你所见的，
>  你所见的那个，美丽的人儿，正是你自己；
>  它随你来，也随你去；但请跟随我，
>  我要领你去一个没有阴影滞留、
>  不碍你前来、不碍你温柔相拥的地方。』

===Original===
 What could I do but follow straight
 Invisibly thus led?
 Till I espied thee, fair indeed and tall,
 Under a plantain; yet methought less fair,
 Less winning soft, less amiably mild
 Than that smooth watery image; back I turned
 Thou following criedst aloud, ‘Return, fair Eve,
 Whom fliest thou? whom thou fliest, of him thou art.
 Part of my soul, I seek thee, and thee claim
 My other half.’ ”

===Chinese===
>  我还能怎样？只得跟随这
>  无形的引领，径直向前，
>  直到我望见了你——你确乎俊美而挺拔，
>  立在一株芭蕉树下；然而我想，你却不及
>  那水中柔美的形影秀丽，
>  不及它温婉可亲、和婉动人，
>  于是我转身回去，
>  你在后追随，大声喊道：『回来吧，美丽的夏娃，
>  你逃避的是谁？你所逃避的，正是他的骨血。
>  你是我灵魂的一半，我寻找你，
>  也认领你做我的另一半。』」

===Original===
This will never cease to throb and thrill as long as man is man and woman is woman.

===Chinese===
只要人还是人、女人还是女人，这一段就永远不会停止搏动与震颤。

===Original===
Now owing to the problematical position at present occupied by descendants of Africans in the American social polity﻿—growing, I presume, out of the continued indecision in the mind of the more powerful descendants of the Saxons as to whether it is expedient to apply the maxims of their religion to their civil and political relationships﻿—most of the writers who have hitherto attempted a portrayal of life and customs among the darker race have belonged to our class II: they have all, more or less, had a point to prove or a mission to accomplish, and thus their art has been almost uniformly perverted to serve their ends; and, to add to their disadvantage, most, if not all the writers on this line have been but partially acquainted with the life they wished to delineate and through sheer ignorance ofttimes, as well as from design occasionally, have not been able to put themselves in the darker man’s place. The art of “thinking one’s self imaginatively into the experiences of others” is not given to all, and it is impossible to acquire it without a background and a substratum of sympathetic knowledge. Without this power our portraits are but death’s heads or caricatures and no amount of cudgeling can put into them the movement and reality of life. Not many have had Mrs. Stowe’s power because not many have studied with Mrs. Stowe’s humility and love. They forget that underneath the black man’s form and behavior there is the great bedrock of humanity, the key to which is the same that unlocks every tribe and kindred of the nations of earth. Some have taken up the subject with a view to establishing evidences of ready formulated theories and preconceptions; and, blinded by their prejudices and antipathies, have altogether abjured all candid and careful study. Others with flippant indifference have performed a few psychological experiments on their cooks and coachmen, and with astounding egotism, and powers of generalization positively bewildering, forthwith aspire to enlighten the world with dissertations on racial traits of the Negro. A few with really kind intentions and a sincere desire for information have approached the subject as a clumsy microscopist, not quite at home with his instrument, might study a new order of beetle or bug. Not having focused closely enough to obtain a clear-cut view, they begin by telling you that all colored people look exactly alike and end by noting down every chance contortion or idiosyncrasy as a race characteristic. Some of their conclusions remind one of the enterprising German on a tour of research and self-improvement through Great Britain, who recommended his favorite sauerkraut both to an Irishman, whom he found sick with fever, and to a Scotchman, who had a cold. On going that way subsequently and finding the Scotchman well and the Irishman dead, he writes: *Mem.﻿—Sauerkraut good for the Scotch but death to the Irish*.

===Chinese===
如今，由于非洲人的后裔在美国社会政体中所处的悬而未决的位置——依我推测，这是由于撒克逊人更强大的后裔们心中始终犹豫不决，不知把他们的宗教准则施于自己的民政与政治关系是否合宜——迄今为止大多数试图描画较暗肤色种族的生活与习俗的作家，都属于我们的第二类：他们全都在或多或少的程度上有一个要证明的论点或要完成的使命，因而他们的艺术几乎一律被滥作达成目的的工具；更添一层不利的是，这一路作家中的大多数——如果不是全部——对想要刻画的生活只有一知半解的了解，于是往往出于全然的无知，偶尔也出于有意的设计，不能把自己放进那较暗肤色之人的处境。「把自己想象地代入他人的经验」这门艺术并非人人可得，而没有一层同情知识作底子与基底，它也无从习得。没有这种能力，我们的肖像便不过是骷髅或漫画，再怎么捶打也捶不进生活的动感与实感。没有几个人有过斯托夫人（Mrs. Stowe）那样的笔力，因为没有几个人以斯托夫人那样的谦卑与爱心研究过。他们忘记了，在黑人的形貌与举止之下，横亘着人性的巨大基岩，开启它的钥匙，与开启世上一切部落与宗族的并无二致。有些人带着预设的理论与成见来处理这个题目，为的是坐实证据；他们被偏见与憎恶蒙住了眼，索性弃绝一切坦诚而细心的研究。另一些人以轻率的冷漠，在自家厨娘与车夫身上做了几桩心理实验，便以骇人的自负和简直令人目眩的概括功力，扬言要以论述黑人种族特质的专著来启迪世界。还有少数几位，怀着真正的善意与求知的诚恳来接近这个题目，情状宛如一位不甚熟练的显微镜学家——对自己的仪器还未能运用自如——去研究一个新目地的甲虫或臭虫。焦距对得不够近，看不清眉目，他们便先告诉你所有有色人长相一模一样，末了又把偶然撞见的每一处扭曲或怪癖都记作一条种族特征。他们的某些结论令人想起那位在大不列颠作研究之旅兼自我进修、进取心切的德国人：他向一位染热病卧床的爱尔兰人和一位患感冒的苏格兰人，都推荐了他心爱的德国酸菜（sauerkraut）。其后他又路过那里，发现苏格兰人痊愈而爱尔兰人已死，遂记道：*备忘——酸菜宜于苏格兰人，而致爱尔兰人于死命。*

===Original===
This criticism is not altered by our grateful remembrance of those who have heroically taken their pens to champion the black man’s cause. But even here we may remark that a painter may be irreproachable in motive and as benevolent as an angel in intention, nevertheless we have a right to compare his copy with the original and point out in what respects it falls short or is overdrawn; and he should thank us for doing so.

===Chinese===
这一批评并不因我们感激地记念那些毅然提笔为黑人事业辩护的人而有所更改。但即便对他们，我们也可以说：一个画家尽可以在动机上无可指摘、在用意上仁厚如天使，我们仍有权把他的摹本与原本两相对照，指出它在哪些方面有所不及或言过其实；他还该为我们的指正而感谢我们。

===Original===
It is in no captious spirit, therefore, that we note a few contributions to this phase of American literature which have been made during the present decade; we shall try to estimate their weight, their tendency, their truthfulness and their lessons, if any, for ourselves.

===Chinese===
因此，我们并非出于吹毛求疵之心，来检视本十年间对美国文学这一侧面作出的若干贡献；我们将试着估量它们的分量、它们的倾向、它们的真实性，以及——倘若有的话——它们给予我们自己的教益。

===Original===
Foremost among the champions of the black man’s cause through the medium of fiction must be mentioned Albion W. Tourgée. No man deserves more the esteem and appreciation of the colored people of this country for his brave words. For ten years he has stood almost alone as the enthusiastic advocate, not of charity and dole to the Negro, but of justice. The volumes he has written upon the subject have probably been read by from five to ten millions of the American people. Look over his list consecrated to one phase or another of the subject: *A Fool’s Errand*, *A Royal Gentleman*, *Bricks Without Straw*, *An Appeal to Caesar*, *Hot Ploughshares*, *Pactolus Prime*﻿—over three thousand pages﻿—enough almost for a life work, besides an almost interminable quantity published in periodicals.

===Chinese===
以小说为媒介为黑人事业奋斗的斗士之中，首屈一指的当推阿尔比恩·W·图尔吉（Albion W. Tourgée）。以他勇敢的言论而论，没有谁比他更当得起这个国家的有色人的敬意与感念。十年来他几乎孤身一人，热情充当的不是对黑人慈善施舍的倡导者，而是正义的倡导者。他就这个题目所写的诸卷，大约已被五百万到一千万美国人读过。且看他奉献于这一题目这一面或那一面的书单：《愚人的差事》（*A Fool's Errand*）、《一位王室绅士》（*A Royal Gentleman*）、《无砖之炊》（*Bricks Without Straw*）、《向凯撒申诉》（*An Appeal to Caesar*）、《炽热的犁铧》（*Hot Ploughshares*）、《帕克托卢斯·普赖姆》（*Pactolus Prime*）——逾三千页——几乎够得上毕生的著作，此外还有发表于期刊上几乎不计其数的文字。

===Original===
Mr. Tourgée essays to paint life with the coloring of fiction, and yet, we must say, we do not think him a novelist primarily; that is, novel making with him seems to be a mere incident, a convenient vehicle through which to convey those burning thoughts which he is constantly trying to impress upon the people of America, whether in lecture, stump speech, newspaper column or magazine article. His power is not that already referred to of thinking himself imaginatively into the experiences of others. He does not create many men of many minds. All his offspring are little Tourgées﻿—they preach his sermons and pray his prayers.

===Chinese===
图尔吉先生试着用小说的彩料描绘生活；然而我们必须说，我们并不认为他首先是一位小说家；就是说，写小说于他似乎只是余事，是一辆顺手的载具，用来输送那些他不断试图铭刻于美利坚人民心头的炽热思想——无论借讲台、竞选演说、报纸专栏还是杂志文章。他的力量不在于前面说过的那种把自己想象地代入他人经验的能力。他不创造千百种心性的千百个人物。他笔下的儿女都是小小的图尔吉——讲他的道，做他的祷告。

===Original===
In *Pactolus Prime*, for example, one of his latest, his hero, a colored bootblack in a large hotel, is none other than the powerful, impassioned, convinced and convincing lecturer, Judge Tourgée himself, done over in ebony. His caustic wit, his sledgehammer logic, his incisive criticism, his righteous indignation, all reflect the irresistible arguments of the great pleader for the Negro; and all the incidents are arranged to enable this bootblack to impress on senators and judges, lawyers, and divines, his plea for justice to the Negro, along with the blacking and shine which he skillfully puts on their aristocratic toes. And so with all the types which Mr. Tourgée presents﻿—worthy or pitiful ones always﻿—they uniformly preach or teach, convict or convert. Artistic criticism aside, it is mainly as a contribution to polemic literature in favor of the colored man that most of Tourgée’s works will be judged; and we know of no one who can more nearly put himself in the Negro’s place in resenting his wrongs and pleading for his rights. In presenting truth from the colored American’s standpoint Mr. Tourgée excels, we think, in fervency and frequency of utterance any living writer, white or colored. Mr. Cable is brave and just. He wishes to see justice done in the Freedman’s case in equity, and we honor and revere him for his earnest manly efforts towards that end. But Mr. Cable does not forget (I see no reason why he should, of course,) that he is a white man, a Southerner and an ex-soldier in the Confederate army. To use his own words, he writes, “with an admiration and affection for the South, that for justice and sincerity yield to none; in a spirit of faithful sonship to a Southern state.” Of course this but proves his sincerity, illustrates his candor, and adds weight to the axiomatic justice of a cause which demands such support from a thoroughly disinterested party, or rather a party whose interest and sympathy and affection must be all on the side he criticises and condemns. The passion of the partisan and the bias of the aggrieved can never be charged against him. Mr. Cable’s is the impartiality of the judge who condemns his own son or cuts off his own arm. His attitude is judicial, convincing, irreproachable throughout.

===Chinese===
例如在他较晚近的作品《帕克托卢斯·普赖姆》里，主人公——一家大旅馆里的黑人擦鞋匠——不是别人，正是那位雄辩滔滔、热烈笃诚、既能说服自己也能说服人的演讲家图尔吉法官本人，只不过用乌木重雕了一遍。他刻薄的机智、他大锤般的逻辑、他犀利的批评、他义正词严的愤慨，处处映现出这位黑人的伟大辩护士那不可抗拒的论证；而书中的一切情节，也都是为着让这位擦鞋匠把他对黑人应得正义的吁求，连同他熟练擦上参议员与法官、律师与神学家们贵族脚趾的鞋油与光泽，一起铭进他们心里。图尔吉先生呈现的一切类型莫不如此——或可敬或可怜——一律地布道或施教，定罪或劝化。撇开艺术批评不谈，图尔吉的著作将主要被当作有色人论战文学的贡献而受评判；而我们不知道还有谁，能在愤懑黑人之所冤、申辩黑人之所权上，比他更贴近地把自己放进黑人的位置。在从美国有色人的立场呈现真理这一点上，我们认为图尔吉先生在言辞的恳切与频仍上，超过当世任何作家，无论白人还是有色人。凯布尔（Cable）先生勇敢而公正。他愿看到获释奴隶的案卷在衡平法上得到公正审理，我们敬重并尊崇他为此目标所作的恳挚丈夫气的努力。但凯布尔先生不曾忘记（我看不出他为什么应该忘记，当然）他是白人、是南方人、是南部邦联军队里的一名退伍军人。用他自己的话说，他写作「怀着对南方的一种敬慕与热爱，其为正义与真诚不让于人；怀着对南方一州的赤子之诚」。这当然只证明他的真诚，印证他的坦荡，并为他所主张的那一事业自明之公道增添分量——这一事业竟能得到这样一位支持者，一位彻底置身利害之外者、毋宁说一位其利益、同情与爱恋全在他所批评、所谴责那一边的人。党同的激愤与受害者的偏袒，永远加不到他的头上。凯布尔先生的公正，是判自己儿子有罪的法官的公正，是自断一臂者的公正。他的态度是法官式的，令人信服，自始至终无可指摘。

===Original===
Not only the Christian conscience of the South, but also its enlightened self-interest is unquestionably on the side of justice and manly dealing toward the black man; and one can not help feeling that a cause which thus enlists the support and advocacy of the “better self” of a nation must ultimately be invincible: and Mr. Cable, in my judgment, embodies and represents that Christian conscience and enlightened self-interest of the hitherto silent South; he vocalizes and inspires its better self. To him the dishonesty and inhumanity there practiced against the black race is a blot on the scutcheon of that fair land and doomed to bring in its wake untold confusion, disaster, and disgrace. From his calm elevation he sees the impending evil, and with loving solicitude urges his countrymen to flee the wrath to come. Mr. Tourgée, on the other hand, speaks with all the eloquence and passion of the aggrieved party himself. With his whip of fine cords he pitilessly scourges the inconsistencies, the weaknesses and pettiness of the black man’s persecutors. The fire is burning within him, he cannot but speak. He has said himself that he deserves no credit for speaking and writing on this subject, for it has taken hold of him and possesses him to the exclusion of almost everything else. Necessity is laid upon him. Not more bound was Saul of Tarsus to consecrate his fiery eloquence to the cause of the persecuted Nazarene than is this white man to throw all the weight of his powerful soul into the plea for justice and Christianity in this American anomaly and huge inconsistency. Not many colored men would have attempted Tourgée’s brave defense of Reconstruction and the alleged corruption of Negro supremacy, more properly termed the period of white sullenness and desertion of duty. Not many would have dared, fearlessly as he did, to arraign this country for an enormous pecuniary debt to the colored man for the two hundred and forty-seven years of unpaid labor of his ancestors. Not many could so determinedly have held up the glass of the real Christianity before these believers in a white Christ and these preachers of the gospel, “Suffer the little *white* children to come unto me.” We all see the glaring inconsistency and feel the burning shame. We appreciate the incongruity and the indignity of having to stand forever hat in hand as beggars, or be shoved aside as intruders in a country whose resources have been opened up by the unrequited toil of our forefathers. We know that our bill is a true one﻿—that the debt is as real as to any pensioners of our government. But the principles of patience and forbearance, of meekness and charity, have become so ingrained in the Negro character that there is hardly enough self-assertion left to ask as our right that a part of the country’s surplus wealth be *loaned* for the education of our children; even though we know that our present poverty is due to the fact that the toil of the last quarter century enriched these coffers, but left us the heirs of crippled, deformed, frostbitten, horny-handed and empty handed mothers and fathers. Oh, the shame of it!

===Chinese===
不仅南方的基督徒良知，就连它开明的自利，也毫无疑问站在对黑人公正相待、以男子汉之道相待的一边；人们不由得感到，一个这样赢得民族「更好的自我」之支持与辩护的事业，终究必是不可战胜的：而凯布尔先生，依我的判断，正体现并代表着那个迄今沉默的南方的基督徒良知与开明自利；他使它发声，唤起它更好的自我。在他看来，那里施于黑种人的欺诈与非人之行，是那片美丽土地纹章上的污点，注定要牵带来数说不尽的混乱、灾祸与耻辱。从他沉静的高处，他望见迫近的祸患，怀着挚爱的忧惧敦劝同胞逃避将临的义怒。图尔吉先生则不然，他怀着受害当事人本人的全部雄辩与激愤说话。他用细绳编成的鞭子，无情抽打黑人迫害者们的言行相悖、弱点与琐屑。火在他里面烧着，他不能不说。他自己说过，他就这个题目发言撰文，不应得任何称誉，因为这题目攫住了他、占有了他，几乎排除其他一切。责无旁贷的重负压在他身上。当年大数人扫罗（Saul of Tarsus）之不得不把他的火烈辩才奉献给受迫害的拿撒勒人之事业，也不过如这位白人之不得不把他强大灵魂的全部分量，投进为这美国的乖异巨谬吁求正义与基督教精神的恳请之中。没有几个有色人会像图尔吉那样，勇敢地去为重建时期、并为所谓黑人至上之腐败辩护——后者更确切的名字，应是白人愠怼而弃职的时期。没有几个敢像他那样无所畏惧地控告这个国家，说它欠着有色人一笔巨大的金钱债，以偿还其先人二百四十七年无偿的劳役。没有几个能那样坚决地把真基督教的明镜举到这些信奉白人基督的人、这些福音传道人面前：「让小*白*孩子到我这里来吧。」我们都看得见那刺眼的不一致，都感到那灼烧的耻辱。我们懂得那种进退失据的屈辱：在一块被我们先人无偿苦役开出了资源的国土上，要么永远帽不离手地站在那里当乞丐，要么被当作闯入者推到一旁。我们知道我们的账单是真的——这笔债，同这个国家欠任何领抚恤金者的债一样实在。可是忍耐与克制、温顺与宽厚的诸般原则，在黑人品格中已浸淫得如此之深，以致连足够的自我申张都所剩无几，不能要求把国家盈余财富的一部分*借*来教育我们的儿女——哪怕我们明知，我们今日的贫困，正因为过去四分之一个世纪的辛劳肥了那些金库，留给我们的继承人却是残跛、佝偻、冻伤、掌心如茧而两手空空的母亲和父亲。啊，这是何等的耻辱！

===Original===
A coward during the war gets a few scratches and bruises﻿—often in *fleeing from the enemy*﻿—and his heirs are handsomely pensioned by his *grateful* country! But these poor wretches stood every man to his post for two hundred and fifty years, digging trenches, building roads, tunneling mountains, clearing away forests, cultivating the soil in the cotton fields and rice swamps till fingers dropped off, toes were frozen, knees twisted, arms stiff and useless﻿—and when their sons and heirs, with the burdens of helpless parents to support, wish to secure enough education to enable them to make a start in life, *their* grateful country sagely deliberates as to the feasibility of sending them to another undeveloped jungle to show off their talent for unlimited pioneer work in strange climes! The Indian, during the entire occupancy of this country by white men, has stood proudly aloof from all their efforts at development, and presented an unbroken front of hostility to the introduction and spread of civilization. The Negro, though brought into the country by force and compelled under the lash to lend his brawn and sturdy sinews to promote its material growth and prosperity, nevertheless with perfect amiability of temper and adaptability of mental structure has quietly and unhesitatingly accepted its standards and fallen in line with its creeds. He adjusts himself just as readily and as appreciatively, it would seem, to the higher and stricter requirements of freedom and citizenship; and although from beginning to end, nettled and goaded under unprecedented provocation, he has never once shown any general disposition to arise in his might and deluge this country with blood or desolate it with burning, as he might have done. It is no argument to charge weakness as the cause of his peaceful submission and to sneer at the “inferiority” of a race who would allow themselves to be made slaves﻿—unrevenged. It *may* be nobler to perish red-handed, to kill as many as your battle-axe holds out to hack and then fall with an exultant yell and savage grin of fiendish delight on the huge pile of bloody corpses﻿—expiring with the solace and unction of having ten thousand wounds all in front. I don’t know. I sometimes think it depends on where you plant your standard and who wears the white plume which your eye inadvertently seeks. If Napoleon is the ideal of mankind, I suppose ’tis only noble to be strong; and true greatness may consist in an adamantine determination never to serve. The greatest race with which I am even partially acquainted, proudly boasts that it has never met another race save as either enemy or victim. They seem to set great store by this fact and I judge it must be immensely noble according to their ideals. But somehow it seems to me that those nations and races who choose the Nazarene for their plumed knight would find some little jarring and variance between such notions and His ideals. There could not be at all times perfect unanimity between Leader and host. A good many of his sayings, it seems to me, would have to be explained away; not a few of his injunctions quietly ignored, and I am not sure but the great hulk of his principles and precepts must after all lie like leaden lumps, an undigested and unassimilable mass on an uneasy overburdened stomach. I find it rather hard to understand these things, and somehow I feel at times as if I have taken hold of the wrong ideal. But then, I suppose, it must be because I have not enough of the spirit that comes with the blood of those grand old *sea kings* (I believe you call them) who shot out in their trusty barks speeding over unknown seas and, like a death-dealing genius, with the piercing eye and bloodthirsty heart of hawk or vulture killed and harried, burned and caroused. This is doubtless all very glorious and noble, and the seed of it must be an excellent thing to have in one’s blood. But I haven’t it. I frankly admit my limitations. I am hardly capable of appreciating to the full such grand intrepidity﻿—due of course to the fact that the stock from which I am sprung did not attain that royal kink in its blood ages ago. My tribe has to own kinship with a very tame and unsanguinary individual who, a long time ago when blue blood was a distilling in the stirring fiery world outside, had no more heroic and daring a thing to do than help a pale sorrow-marked man as he was toiling up a certain hill at Jerusalem bearing his own cross whereon he was soon to be ignominiously nailed. This Cyrenian fellow was used to bearing burdens and he didn’t mind giving a lift over a hard place now and then, with no idea of doing anything grand or memorable, or that even so much as his name would be known thereby. And then, too, by a rather strange coincidence this unwarlike and insignificant kinsman of ours had his home in a country (the fatherland of all the family) which had afforded kindly shelter to that same mysterious Stranger, when, a babe and persecuted by bloody power and heartless jealousy, He had to flee the land of his birth. And somehow this same country has in its day done so much fostering and sheltering of that kind﻿—has watched and hovered over the cradles of religions and given refuge and comfort to the persecuted, the world weary, the storm tossed benefactors of mankind so often that she has come to represent nothing stronger or more imposing than the “eternal womanly” among the nations, and to accept as her mission and ideal, *loving service* to mankind.

===Chinese===
战争中的一个懦夫，擦破几处皮、碰青几块肉——往往还是在*逃离敌人*的时候——他的继承人便由他*感恩的*国家发给丰厚的抚恤金！而这些可怜的人们，人人坚守岗位二百五十年，挖堑壕、筑道路、凿山岭、清森林，在棉田与稻沼中耕作，直到手指脱落、脚趾冻掉、膝盖扭曲、手臂僵废——而当他们的儿女与继承人，背负着无力谋生的双亲，想受足够的教育以便在人生的起点迈出一步时，他们*感恩的*国家却在煞有介事地斟酌：把他们送往另一片未开发的丛林，去异域展示他们无限量的拓荒才能，究竟是否可行！印第安人在白人占据这片国土的全部岁月里，傲然置身于他们的一切开发努力之外，对文明的引入与传播摆出一副从未破裂的敌意阵线。黑人虽然被强掳到这个国家、在皮鞭下被迫献出筋骨与顽腱以促成它的物质增长与繁荣，却以天性的纯良温厚与心智结构的善于适应，安静而毫不迟疑地接受了它的标准，归入了它的信条。他同样欣然而识趣地调适自己，去适应自由与公民身份那更高、更严的要求；而且，尽管自始至终在前所未有的挑衅之下被刺痛、被驱迫，他却从不曾显露任何普遍的意向，要奋起凭他的膂力血洗这个国家，或纵火使它沦为焦土——这本是他做得到的。把他和平的顺服归因于孱弱，讥嘲一个甘心为奴、*含冤不报*的种族之「低劣」，算不得什么论证。双手沾血地毁灭、战斧抡得动便尽量多杀，然后带着狂喜的呼啸与狰狞的狞笑，扑倒在层层血尸之上——带着身前万创的安慰与荣光咽气——这*或许*更高贵。我不知道。我有时想，这取决于你把军旗插在哪里，取决于你的目光不觉间去追寻的那支白翎戴在谁的盔上。倘若拿破仑是人类的理想，我想便唯有强大才算高贵，真正的伟大也许就在于一种宁死不事的、坚如磐石的决心。我所略识的这一最伟大的种族，便骄傲地自夸：它与别的种族相逢，从来非敌即虏。他们似乎极珍视这一事实，我料想按他们的理想，这必定高贵之至。可是不知怎的，在我看来，那些选择拿撒勒人作他们插翎骑士的民族与种族，会发现这样的观念与祂的理想之间，颇有些龃龉难合。领袖与信众之间不可能时时完全一致。依我看，祂的不少话将不得不曲为解说，祂的若干训诫只好悄悄搁置，而且我说不准，祂那大堆的原则与箴规，到头来是否必定像铅块一样，沉甸甸地积在一个惶惶不安、不胜重负的胃里，无从消化、也无从吸收。我觉得这些事情颇难索解，有时竟觉得自己抱错了理想。可是转念一想，我想这必是因为我的血里缺少几分那种古老伟大的*海上王者*（sea kings，我想你们是这么称呼他们的）的精神——他们驾着结实的小船冲出，疾驰过未知的海洋，像一位司掌死亡的天才，带着鹰隼或秃鹫般锐利的眼睛与嗜血的心，杀戮与劫掠，焚烧与欢宴。这无疑全都极其光荣、极其高贵，这种种子掺在血液里必定是极好的东西。可惜我没有。我坦然承认我的局限。我几乎无从充分欣赏这等雄豪的无畏——这当然是由于我所从出的族系，在远古不曾把那王者之血的鬈曲注进它的血管。我的部族不得不攀认一位极驯良、极不嗜血的亲族：很久以前，当外面那个沸腾火烈的世界正在蒸馏贵血的时候，他所能做的最英勇、最大胆的事，不过是帮扶一位苍白而愁苦的人——那人正背负着自己不久将受钉的十字架，艰难地走上耶路撒冷的某座山坡。这位古利奈人（Cyrenian）惯于负重，偶尔帮人渡过一段难走的坡路，他并不介意，也全没有想到自己在做什么了不起、值得纪念的事，甚至不曾想到自己的名字会因此为人所知。而说来也巧，我们这位不好战的、微不足道的亲眷，他的家恰恰在一个国家（那是全家族的故土）——当那位神秘的圣婴遭嗜血的权势与狠心的嫉妒迫害、不得不逃离降生之地时，正是这个国家给了他仁慈的庇护。而也不知怎的，这个国家在它的时代里竟做了那么多这等抚育庇护之事——那么多次守护过各宗教的摇篮，给受迫害者、厌世者、饱经风霜的人类恩主以避难与慰藉——以致她在诸民族中间所代表的，无非是「永恒之女性」，再没有更强、更威严的东西，而她奉为使命与理想的，是对人类的*爱的服务*。

===Original===
With such antecedents then the black race in America should not be upbraided for having no taste for blood and carnage. It is the fault of their constitution that they prefer the judicial awards of peace and have an eternal patience to abide the bloodless triumph of right. It is no argument, therefore, when I point to the record of their physical supremacy﻿—when the homes and helpless ones of this country were absolutely at the black man’s mercy and not a town laid waste, not a building burned, and *not a woman insulted*﻿—it is no argument, I say, for you to retort: “*He was a coward; he didn’t dare!*” The facts simply do not show this to have been the case.

===Chinese===
有这样的前因，美利坚的黑种人便不该因不嗜血腥、不喜杀戮而受责难。他们禀性偏好和平的司法裁决，有永恒的耐心静候正义不流血的凯旋。因此，当我指给他们看那段膂力称雄的记录——当这个国家的家园与无力自卫者全然任凭黑人摆布之时，没有一座城镇遭蹂躏，没有一栋建筑被焚毁，*没有一名妇女被凌辱*——我说，你们若反唇相讥：「*他是懦夫；他不敢！*」——那算不得论证。事实根本表明，情形并非如此。

===Original===
Now the tardy conscience of the nation wakes up one bright morning and is overwhelmed with blushes and stammering confusion because convicted of dishonorable and unkind treatment of *the Indian*; and there is a wonderful scurrying around among the keepers of the keys to get out more blankets and send out a few primers for the “*wards*.” While the black man, a faithful son and indefeasible heir﻿—who can truthfully say, “Lo, these many years do I serve thee, neither transgressed I at any time thy commandment, and yet thou never gavest me a kid that I might make merry with my friends,”﻿—is snubbed and chilled and made unwelcome at every merrymaking of the family. And when appropriations for education are talked of, the section for which he has wrought and suffered most, actually defeats the needed and desired assistance for fear they may not be able to prevent his getting a fair and equitable share in the distribution.

===Chinese===
如今，这个国家迟到的良知在一个晴朗的早晨醒来，因被判定亏待、苛待了*印第安人*而羞愧满面、讷讷失措；于是掌管钥匙的人们手忙脚乱地四处奔走，要多取出些毯子，给这些「*被监护人*」送去几册启蒙课本。而黑人——这忠诚的儿子、这不可褫夺的继承人——他尽可以如实说：「看哪，我服事你这多年，从来没有违背过你的命，你并没有给我一只山羊羔，叫我和朋友一同快乐」——却在家庭的每一次欢宴上受冷遇、遭白眼、不被欢迎。而每当谈及教育拨款，那个他为之出力最多、受苦最深的地区，居然否决了这笔急需而众望所归的援助，唯恐分配时不能阻止他得到公平合理的一份。

===Original===
Oh, the shame of it!

===Chinese===
啊，这是何等的耻辱！

===Original===
In *Pactolus Prime* Mr. Tourgée has succeeded incomparably, we think, in photographing and vocalizing the feelings of the colored American in regard to the Christian profession and the pagan practice of the dominant forces in the American government. And as an impassioned denunciation of the heartless and godless spirit of caste founded on color, as a scathing rebuke to weak-eyed Christians who cannot read the golden rule across the color line, as an unanswerable arraignment of unparalleled ingratitude and limping justice in the policy of this country towards the weaker of its two children, that served it so long and so faithfully, the book is destined to live and to furnish an invaluable contribution to this already plethoric department of American literature.

===Chinese===
在《帕克托卢斯·普赖姆》中，图尔吉先生，我们认为，无可比拟地摄下并道出了有色美国人对于美国政府中主导势力的基督教表白与异教行径的感受。作为对建立在肤色之上那种冷酷无神之种姓精神的激愤控诉，作为对隔着肤色线便读不出金律的弱视基督徒的痛切训责，作为对这个国家对待它两个儿女中较弱者——那长期而忠实地服事过它的一个——的政策之中空前忘恩与跛行公义的无从置辩的弹劾，这本书注定要流传下去，为本已人满为患的这一门类贡献一份无法估价的文献。

===Original===
Mr. Cable and Mr. Tourgée represent possibly the most eminent as well as the most prolific among the writers on this subject belonging to the didactic or polemic class. A host of others there are﻿—lesser lights, or of more intermittent coruscations﻿—who have contributed on either side the debate single treatises, numerous magazine articles or newspaper editorials, advocating some one theory some another on the so-called *race problem*. In this group belongs the author of *An Appeal to Pharaoh*, advocating the deportation absurdity; also the writings of H. W. Grady; “In Plain Black and White,” *The Brother in Black*, *The South Investigated*, *A Defense of the Negro Race*, *The Prosperity of the South Dependent on the Elevation of the Negro*, *The Old South and the New*, *Black and White*, etc., etc., among which are included articles from the pen of colored men themselves, such as Mr. Douglass, Dr. Crummell, Dr. Arnett, Dr. Blyden, Dr. Scarborough, Dr. Price, Mr. Fortune, and others. These are champions of the forces on either side. They stand ever at the forefront dealing desperate blows right and left, now fist and skull, now broadsword and battle-axe, now with the flash and boom of artillery; while the little fellows run out ever and anon from the ranks and deliver a telling blow between the eyes of an antagonist. All are wrought up to a high tension, some are blinded with passion, others appalled with dread﻿—all sincerely feel the reality of their own vision and earnestly hope to compel their world to see with their eyes. Such works, full of the fever and heat of debate belong to the turmoil and turbulence of the time. A hundred years from now they may be interesting history, throwing light on a feature of these days which, let us hope, will then be hardly intelligible to an American citizen not over fifty years old.

===Chinese===
凯布尔先生与图尔吉先生，大概是这一题目上训诫类或论战类作家中最杰出、也最多产的两位。此外还有一大群——些较小的光亮，或时断时续的闪烁——在辩论双方各自贡献过单本的论著、众多的杂志文章或报纸社论，对这个所谓的*种族问题*（race problem）各鼓吹一种理论。归于这一类的，有《向法老申诉》（*An Appeal to Pharaoh*）的作者，鼓吹的是遣返的谬说；还有 H·W·格雷迪（H. W. Grady）的文字；《明明白白的黑与白》（"In Plain Black and White"）、《黑皮肤兄弟》（*The Brother in Black*）、《南方调查记》（*The South Investigated*）、《为黑人种族辩护》（*A Defense of the Negro Race*）、《南方繁荣系于黑人地位之提升》（*The Prosperity of the South Dependent on the Elevation of the Negro*）、《旧南方与新南方》（*The Old South and the New*）、《黑与白》（*Black and White*）等等，等等；其中还包括有色人自己手笔的文章，出自道格拉斯（Douglass）先生、克拉梅尔（Crummell）博士、阿内特（Arnett）博士、布莱登（Blyden）博士、斯卡伯勒（Scarborough）博士、普赖斯（Price）博士、富钦（Fortune）先生诸人。这些人是双方阵营的斗士。他们永远站在最前哨，左右开弓、奋不顾身地厮杀，时而拳脚交加，时而大剑与战斧齐飞，时而在炮火的闪光与轰鸣中冲锋；而小喽啰们也不时从队列里窜出，照着论敌的眉心给上一记致命的重击。人人绷紧到极点，有的被激愤灼瞎了眼，有的被恐惧吓破了胆——个个都真诚地感到自己眼见的真实，个个都恳切地希望迫令他们的世界用他们的眼睛去看。这类满蓄着辩论的热与火的作品，属于时代的喧嚣与动荡。百年之后，它们或许成为有趣的历史，照亮今日的一个侧面——让我们祝愿，到那时，凡不满五十岁的美国公民，已几乎无从理解这个侧面。

===Original===
Among our artists for art’s sweet sake, Mr. Howells has recently tried his hand also at painting the Negro, attempting merely a side light in half tones, on his life and manners; and I think the unanimous verdict of the subject is that, in this single department at least, Mr. Howells does not know what he is talking about. And yet I do not think we should quarrel with *An Imperative Duty* because it lacks the earnestness and bias of a special pleader. Mr. Howells merely meant to press the button and give one picture from American life involving racial complications. The kodak does no more; it cannot preach sermons or solve problems.

===Chinese===
在为艺术之甜美而艺术的艺术家中间，豪威尔斯先生新近也试笔画了画黑人，试图只给他的人生与习俗打一道半调的侧光；而我想，画中人的公论是一致的：至少在这个孤立的部门里，豪威尔斯先生不知所云。然而我并不认为我们该同《义不容辞的义务》（*An Imperative Duty*）过不去，只因为它缺少一位请命辩护士的恳切与偏袒。豪威尔斯先生不过是想按下快门，从美利坚生活中给出一张牵涉种族纠葛的照片。柯达相机做到的也不过如此；它不会讲道，也不能解题。

===Original===
Besides, the portrayal of Negro characteristics was by no means the main object of the story, which was rather meant, I judge, to be a thumbnail sketch containing a psychological study of a morbidly sensitive conscience hectoring over a weak and vacillating will and fevered into increased despotism by reading into its own life and consciousness the analyses and terrible retributions of fiction﻿—a product of the Puritan’s uncompromising sense of “*right though the heavens fall*,” irritated and kept sore by being unequally yoked with indecision and cowardice. Of such strokes Mr. Howells is undoubtedly master. It is true there is little point and no force of character about the beautiful and irresponsible young heroine; but as that is an attainment of so many of Mr. Howells’ models, it is perhaps not to be considered as illustrating any racial characteristics. I cannot help sharing, however, the indignation of those who resent the picture in the colored church﻿—“evidently,” Mr. Howells assures us, “representing *the best colored society*”; where the horrified young prig, Rhoda Aldgate, meets nothing but the frog-like countenances and catfish mouths, the musky exhalations and the “bress de Lawd, Honey,” of an uncultivated people. It is just here that Mr. Howells fails﻿—and fails because he gives only a half truth, and that a partisan half truth. One feels that he had no business to attempt a subject of which he knew so little, or for which he cared so little. There is one thing I would like to say to my white fellow countrymen, and especially to those who dabble in ink and affect to discuss the Negro; and yet I hesitate because I feel it is a fact which persons of the finer sensibilities and more delicate perceptions must know instinctively: namely, that it is an insult to humanity and a sin against God to publish any such sweeping generalizations of a race on such meager and superficial information. We meet it at every turn﻿—this obtrusive and offensive vulgarity, this gratuitous sizing up of the Negro and conclusively writing down his equation, sometimes even among his ardent friends and bravest defenders. Were I not afraid of falling myself into the same error that I am condemning, I would say it seems an *Anglo-Saxon characteristic* to have such overweening confidence in his own power of induction that there is no equation which he would acknowledge to be indeterminate, however many unknown quantities it may possess.

===Chinese===
况且，描画黑人特性本不是那部小说的主旨；据我判断，它的用意毋宁是一幅缩影，内含一份病态敏感的良知的心理研究——这良知威凌着一个软弱摇摆的意志，又因把小说里的剖析与骇人的报应读进了自己的生命与意识，而愈发燥热、愈发专横——这是清教徒那种「*哪怕天塌下来也要行正义*」的不妥协感的产物，因与犹疑和怯懦同轭不匹而时时发炎、久久作痛。这样的笔触，豪威尔斯先生无疑是圣手。诚然，那位美丽而不负责任的女主人公缺乏锋芒与性格的力量；但那原是豪威尔斯先生笔下许多模特儿的共同造诣，或许算不得任何种族特征的例证。然而，我禁不住与那些愤懑于书中有色教会一幕的人们同其愤懑——豪威尔斯先生向我们保证，那一幕「显然代表*上等的有色人社会*」；在那里，惊恐的年轻娇客罗达·奥尔德盖特（Rhoda Aldgate）所见，无非蛙似的面孔与鲇鱼似的嘴、麝香般的气息与「感谢主哩，亲爱的」（bress de Lawd, Honey，方言）——一个未经教化的人群的众生相。豪威尔斯先生恰在此处失手——失手在于他只给出半截真理，而且是有党见的半截。人们觉得，他本不该染指一个他知之如此之少、或者措意如此之少的题目。有一句话我想对我的白人同胞们说——尤其对那些舞文弄墨、喜好议论黑人的人；然而我欲言又止，因为我感到，凡感觉细腻、见识精微的人必然本能地知道它：那就是，凭如此贫乏而浮表的见闻，便对一个种族发表这等一网打尽的概括，是对人类的侮辱，是对上帝的犯罪。我们处处遇到它——这种咄咄逼人的无礼粗鄙，这种对黑人无缘由的品头论足、一锤定音地写下他的方程——有时竟出自他热忱的朋友、最勇敢的辩护者之口。倘若不是怕自己也犯下所谴责的这同一种错误，我真要说，对自己归纳力怀有这等过分自信，似乎是一种*盎格鲁-撒克逊特性*：天下没有一个方程他肯承认是无定的，无论它含着多少个未知量。

===Original===
Here is an extract from Dr. Mayo, a thoroughly earnest man and sincerely friendly, as I believe, to the colored people.

===Chinese===
下面摘录一段梅奥（Mayo）博士的话——据我所信，这是一位极其恳挚的人，对有色人也怀有真诚的友爱。

===Original===
> “Among these women are as many grades of native, intellectual, moral and executive force as among the white people. The plantations of the Gulf, the Atlantic coast and the Mississippi bottoms swarm with negro women who seem hardly lifted above the brutes. I know a group of young colored women, many of them accomplished teachers, who bear themselves as gently and with as varied womanly charms as any score of ladies in the land. The one abyss of perdition *to this class* is the slough of unchastity in which, *as a race* they still flounder, half conscious that it is a slough﻿—the double inheritance of savage Africa and slavery.”

===Chinese===
> 「在这些妇女中间，本地智力、道德与办事能力的等第之繁，一如白人之中。墨西哥湾沿岸、大西洋海滨与密西西比河低地的种植园里，到处都是似乎几乎不曾高出兽类的黑人妇女。我也认识一群年轻的有色妇女，其中许多人是才艺出众的教师，她们举止温婉，女性风韵之多姿，不逊于国内任何一批名媛。*对这一阶层而言*，唯一的毁灭深渊是不贞的泥沼——*作为种族*，她们仍深陷其中，浑然半觉那是一个泥沼——那是野蛮非洲与奴隶制的双重遗产。」

===Original===
Now there may be one side of a truth here, yet who but a self-confident Anglo-Saxon would dare make such a broad unblushing statement about a people *as a race*? Some developments brought to light recently through the scientific Christianity and investigating curiosity of Dr. Parkhurst may lead one to suspect the need of missionary teaching to “elevate” the white race; and yet I have too much respect for the autonomy of races, too much reverence for the collective view of God’s handiwork to speak of any such condition, however general, as characterizing *the race*. The colored people do not object to the adequate and truthful portrayal of types of their race in whatever degree of the scale of civilization, or of social and moral development, is consonant with actual facts or possibilities. As Mr. Howells himself says, “A man can be anything along the vast range from angel to devil, and without living either the good thing or the bad thing in which his fancy dramatizes him, he can perceive it”﻿—and I would add, can appreciate and even enjoy its delineation by the artist. The average Englishman takes no exception to the humorous caricatures of Dickens or to the satires and cynicisms of Thackeray. The Quilps and the Bernsteins are but strongly developed negatives of our universal human nature on the dark side.
