We recognize them as genre sketches﻿—and with the Agneses and Esthers and Aunt Lamberts as foils and correctives, we can appreciate them accordingly: while we do not believe ourselves to be the original of the portrait, there is enough sympathy and fellow feeling for the character to prevent our human relationship from being outraged and insulted. But were Dickens to introduce an average scion of his countrymen to a whole congregation of *Quilps*, at the same time sagely informing him that these represented *the best there was* of English life and morals, I strongly suspect the charming author would be lifted out on the toe of said average Englishman’s boot, in case there shouldn’t happen to be a good horsewhip handy.

===Chinese===
这话里或许有真理的一面；然而，除了一个自信满满的盎格鲁-撒克逊人，谁敢对一个民族*作为种族*发表这样露骨的大言？近来借帕克赫斯特（Parkhurst）博士的科学基督教精神与追根究底的好奇心而曝光的某些事态，倒让人怀疑需要传教士来「提携」白人种族；然而我对诸种族的自主性、对上帝手工的整全图景怀有太多的敬意，绝不肯把这样一种情形——无论多么普遍——说成*这个种族*的特征。有色人并不反对对其种族类型充分而真实的描画，无论那类型处在文明、社会与道德发展标尺的哪一度，只要与实际的事实或可能性相合。正如豪威尔斯先生自己说的：「在从天使到魔鬼的辽阔谱系上，一个人可以是任何东西；不必亲身活过他的幻想所戏剧化的那桩善事或恶事，他也能感知它」——我还要添一句：还能欣赏、甚至享受艺术家对它的刻画。普通的英国人并不见怪狄更斯（Dickens）的谐趣漫画，也不见怪萨克雷（Thackeray）的讽刺与冷嘲。奎尔普们与伯恩斯坦们，不过是我们普遍人性在幽暗一面被强烈显影的底片。我们认它们作世情小品——有艾格妮丝们、埃丝特们与兰伯特姑母们作映衬与矫正，我们自能相应地欣赏它们：只要不把我们自己认作画中人，只要对人物还存着足够的同情与共感，不致使我们人类的亲缘受辱、被冒渎。可是，倘使狄更斯把他本国同胞的一个寻常子弟引进整整一座教堂的*奎尔普*中间，还煞有介事地告诉他：这些人代表英国生活与道德的*菁华*——我强烈怀疑，若碰巧手边没有一条趁手的马鞭，这位可爱的作家多半会被那位普通英国人的靴尖送上天空。

===Original===
Our grievance then is not that we are not painted as angels of light or as goody-goody Sunday-school developments; but we do claim that a man whose acquaintanceship is so slight that he cannot even discern diversities of individuality, has no right or authority to hawk “the only true and authentic” pictures of a race of human beings. Mr. Howells’ point of view is precisely that of a white man who sees colored people at long range or only in certain capacities. His conclusions about the colored man are identical with the impressions that will be received and carried abroad by foreigners from all parts of the globe, who shall attend our Columbian Exposition for instance, and who, through the impartiality and generosity of our white countrymen, will see colored persons only as bootblacks and hotel waiters, grinning from ear to ear and bowing and courtesying for the extra tips. In the same way Mr. Howells has met colored persons in hotels or on the commons promenading and sparking, or else acting as menials and lazzaroni. He has not seen, and therefore cannot be convinced that there exists a quiet, self-respecting, dignified class of easy life and manners (save only where it crosses the roughness of their white fellow countrymen’s barbarity) of cultivated tastes and habits, and with no more in common with the class of his acquaintance than the accident of complexion﻿—beyond a sympathy with their wrongs, or a resentment at being socially and morally classified with them, according as the principle of altruism or of self love is dominant in the individual.

===Chinese===
我们的不平，并不是怨人家没把我们画成光明的天使或主日学校式的乖乖好人；我们要求的是：一个交谊浅陋到辨不出个性差异的人，没有权利也没有资格沿街叫卖一个人类种族的「唯一真实可信」的画像。豪威尔斯先生的视点，恰是一个远远望见有色人、或只在特定身份里看见有色人的白人的视点。他对有色人的结论，同世界各地前来参观我们的哥伦布博览会（Columbian Exposition）的外国人将带走回去的印象一模一样——承我们白人同胞的一视同仁与慷慨大度，他们将只看见有色人做擦鞋匠与旅馆侍者，龇着满口牙、点头哈腰地多讨几个赏钱。豪威尔斯先生也正是这样，只在旅馆里，或在公共草坪上遛达谈情时见过有色人，再不然就是他们充当仆役与拉扎罗尼（lazzaroni，那不勒斯式游民）的时候。他没有见过，因此也无法相信，还存在一个安静、自尊、有尊严的阶层，生活优游、举止娴雅（唯有碰上他们白人同胞的粗暴蛮横时才起波澜），趣味与习惯皆有教养，与他相熟的那个阶级除肤色这一偶然之外毫无共同之处——除了对这个阶级所受冤屈的同情，或因在社会与道德上被与之归为一类而生的愤懑，且视乎利他原则还是自爱原则在个人心中占上风。

===Original===
I respectfully submit that there is hardly a colored church in any considerable city in this country, which could be said in any sense to represent *the best colored society*, in which Rhoda Aldgate could not have seen, when she opened her eyes, persons as quietly and as becomingly dressed, as cultivated in tone and as refined in manner, as herself; persons, too, as sensitive to rough contact and as horribly alive as she could be (though they had known it from childhood) to the galling distinctions in this country which insist on *levelling down* all individuals more or less related to the Africans. So far from the cringing deference which Mr. Howells paints as exhibited to “the young white lady,” in nine cases out of ten the congregation would have supposed intuitively that she was a quadroon, so far from the unusual was her appearance and complexion. In not a few such colored churches would she have found young women of aspiration and intellectual activity with whom she could affiliate without nausea and from whom she could learn a good many lessons﻿—and, sadly I say it, even more outside the churches whom bitterness at racial inconsistency of white Christians had soured into a silent disbelief of all religion. In either class she would have found no trouble in reaching a heart which could enter into all the agony of her own trial and bitter grief. Nor am I so sure, if she had followed her first gushing impulse to go South and “elevate” the race with whom she had discovered her relationship, that she would have found even them so ready to receive her condescending patronage.

===Chinese===
我谨此陈明：在这个国家任何一座像样的城市里，几乎找不到一座可以任凭怎么说都算代表*上等有色人社会*的有色教会，罗达·奥尔德盖特在那里只要睁开眼睛，本可以见到衣着与她一样安然得体、风度与她一样有教养、举止与她一样文雅的人们；也本可以见到和她一样敏于粗鲁的接触、和她一样（尽管自幼便熟知此事）痛苦地感知这个国家那些磨人之区分的人们——这些区分执意要把一切与非洲人多少沾亲的个体*向下拉平*。绝无豪威尔斯先生所描绘的那种对「年轻白人女士」卑躬屈膝的恭顺；十之八九，会众凭直觉只会当她是个有四分之一黑人血统的混血女子（quadroon）——她的相貌与肤色实在算不得如何出众。在不少这样的有色教会里，她还可见到有志气、有智识活动的青年女子，与之交往不致作呕，且能从她们身上学到许多功课——而我痛心地说，教会之外这样的人更多，白人基督徒种族上的言行相悖，已把她们的心酸成了对一切宗教默默的不信。无论在哪一类人中，她要触到一颗心都不难——那颗心能全然体会她自己那份煎熬与锥心的悲恸。而且，倘若她当真顺从了最初那股热切的冲动，要南下到那个她新发现与自己血脉相连的种族中间去「提携」他们，我也不敢断定，连那些人是否就乐于领受她居高临下的眷顾。

===Original===
There are numerous other inadvertent misrepresentations in the book﻿—such as supposing that colored people voluntarily and deliberately prefer to keep to themselves in all public places and that from choice “they have their own neighborhoods, their own churches, their own amusements, their own resorts,”﻿—the intimation that there is a “*black* voice,” a black character, easy, irresponsible and fond of what is soft and pleasant, a black ideal of art and a black barbaric taste in color, a black affinity﻿—so that in some occult and dreadful way one, only one-sixteenth related and totally foreign by education and environment, can still feel that one-sixteenth race calling her more loudly than the fifteen-sixteenths. I wish to do Mr. Howells the justice to admit, however, that one feels his blunders to be wholly unintentional and due to the fact that he has studied his subject merely from the outside. With all his matchless powers as a novelist, not even he can yet “think himself imaginatively” into the colored man’s place.

===Chinese===
书中还有许多别的不经意的失实——比如说，以为有色人在一切公共场所都是自愿而刻意地闭守自处，以为出于自择「他们有自己的街区、自己的教会、自己的娱乐、自己的去处」——又暗示存在一种「*黑*人的嗓音」、一种黑人的性格：闲散、不负责任、贪恋柔软适意，一种黑人的艺术理想、一种黑人的野蛮用色趣味、一种黑人的亲和力——以致于凭某种玄奥而可怖的方式，一个只有十六分之一血统关联、因教育与环境而全然异质的人，仍能感到那十六分之一的种族在体内召唤她，比那十六分之十五的声音更为响亮。不过，我愿为豪威尔斯先生说句公道话：人们感到，他的种种失误全然出于无意，只在于他仅仅从外面研究了这个题目。纵有他作为小说家那无可匹敌的功力，连他也还不能把自己「想象地」放进有色人的位置。

===Original===
To my mind the quaintest and truest little bit of portraiture from lowlife that I have read in a long time is the little story that appeared last winter in the *Harper’s*, of the “Widder Johnsing and How She Caught the Preacher.” It is told with naive impersonality and appreciative humor, and is quite equal, I think, both in subject and treatment to the best of Mrs. Stowe’s New England dialect stories. It is idyllic in its charming simplicity and naturalness, and delightfully fresh in its sparkling wit and delicious humor. We do not resent such pictures as this of our lowly folk﻿—such a homely and honest

===Chinese===
依我看，许久以来我读到的最精怪、也最真切的一幅下层生活小像，是去年冬天发表在《哈泼斯》（*Harper's*）上的那篇小故事：《约翰逊寡妇怎样逮住传道人》（"Widder Johnsing and How She Caught the Preacher"，"Widder Johnsing" 为 Widow Johnson 的方言拼法）。它以天真的客观笔致与会心的幽默讲述，无论题材还是手法，我认为都足以媲美斯托夫人新英格兰方言故事中的上乘之作。它有一种迷人的质朴与自然，堪称田园诗；它那闪烁的机智与隽永的谐趣又清新可喜。我们对这类描绘我们卑微民众的图画并无怨言——这样一颗家常而诚实的——

===Original===
> “Pomegranate, which, if cut deep down the middle,
 Shows a heart within blood tinctured of a *veined humanity*,”

===Chinese===
> 「石榴啊，若从正中深深剖开，
>  便见一颗心，因*脉络纵横的人性*而染成殷红，」

===Original===
is always sweet to the taste and dear to the heart, however plain and humble the setting.

===Chinese===
——无论托座的样式多么朴素寒微，总是入口甘甜、动人心怀。

===Original===
A longer and more elaborate work, *Harold*, published anonymously, comes properly in our group second, the didactic novel. It gives the picture of a black Englishman cultured and refined, brought in painful contact with American﻿—or rather *un-American*, color prejudice. The point of the book seems to be to show that education for the black man is a curse, since it increases his sensitiveness to the indignities he must suffer in consequence of white barbarity. The author makes Harold, after a futile struggle against American inequalities, disappear into the jungles of Africa, “there to wed a dusky savage,” at the last cursing the day he had ever suspected a broader light or known a higher aspiration; a conclusion which, to my mind, is a most illogical one. If the cultivated black man cannot endure the white man’s barbarity﻿—the cure, it seems to me, would be to cultivate the white man. Civilize both, then each will know what is due from man to man, and that reduces at once to a minimum the friction of their contact.

===Chinese===
一部更长、更铺陈的作品《哈罗德》（*Harold*）以匿名出版，恰当地归入我们的第二类，训诫小说。它画的是一个有教养、有风度的英国黑人，在同美利坚的——毋宁说*非美利坚的*——肤色偏见的痛苦接触中的遭遇。这本书的用意似乎是要表明：对黑人而言，教育是一重诅咒，因为它加深了他对因白人的野蛮而必受的侮辱的敏感。作者让哈罗德在同美国的种种不平等作了一场徒劳的抗争之后，隐没进非洲的丛林，「去娶一位黝黑的蛮女」，临了诅咒自己竟疑心过更阔的光明、怀有过更高的志向的那些日子。这样一个结论，在我看来最不合逻辑。倘若受了教养的黑人受不住白人的野蛮——依我看，药方便是去教养白人。把双方都文明化，那么每一方都会懂得人对人应尽的本分，这就立刻把他们接触的磨擦减到最小。

===Original===
In the same rank as *Harold* belongs that improbability of improbabilities, *Doctor Huguet*, by the arch-sensationalist, Ignatius Donelly. As its purpose is evidently good, I shall not undertake to review the book. Suffice it to say the plot hinges on the exchange of soul between the body of a black chicken thief and that of a cultivated white gentleman, and sets forth the indignities and wrongs to which the cultured soul, with all its past of refinement and learning, has to submit in consequence of its change of cuticle. The book is an able protest against that snobbishness which elevates complexion into a touchstone of aristocracy and makes the pigment cells of a man’s skin his badge of nobility regardless of the foulness or purity of the soul within; the only adverse criticism from the colored man’s point of view being the selection of a chicken thief as his typical black man; but on the principle of antitheses this may have been artistically necessary.

===Chinese===
与《哈罗德》同列的，是那桩不可能中之不可能——《于格特医生》（*Doctor Huguet*），出自那位煽情圣手伊格内修斯·唐纳利（Ignatius Donelly）之手。既然其用意显然是好的，我就不去评它了。只须说：情节的枢机，在于一个偷鸡黑人的身体与一位有教养的白人绅士的身体互换了灵魂，写的是那颗有教养的灵魂，带着它全部的教养与学识的过往，因换了一层表皮而不得不忍受的种种侮辱与冤屈。这本书是对那种势利作风的有力抗议——它把肤色抬高为贵族的试金石，把人皮肤里的色素细胞当作高贵的徽章，全不问皮囊之内灵魂的污洁；从有色人的观点看，唯一可议之处是选了一个偷鸡贼作他的典型黑人；不过依对照的原理，这在艺术上或许自有其必要。

===Original===
I shall pass next to what I consider the most significant contribution to this subject for the last ten years﻿—a poem by Maurice Thompson in the *New York Independent* for January 21, 1892, entitled “A Voodoo Prophecy.” From beginning to end it is full of ghoulish imagery and fine poetic madness. Here are a few stanzas of it:

===Chinese===
接下来我要谈的，是我认为十年来就这个题目所作的最重要的一项贡献——莫里斯·汤普森（Maurice Thompson）刊于 1892 年 1 月 21 日《纽约独立报》（*New York Independent*）的一首诗，题名《伏都预言》（"A Voodoo Prophecy"）。它从头到尾满纸食尸鬼般的意象与精微的诗性癫狂。兹录数节如下：

===Original===
> “I am the prophet of the dusky race,
 The poet of wild Africa. Behold,
 The midnight vision brooding in my face!
 Come near me,
 And hear me,
 While from my lips the words of Fate are told.

===Chinese===
> 「我是这黝黑种族的先知，
>  是狂野非洲的诗人。看哪，
>  午夜的幻象正在我脸上盘旋！
>  靠近我，
>  倾听我，
>  命运的话语正从我唇间道出。

===Original===
> A black and terrible memory masters me,
 The shadow and the substance of deep wrong;
 You know the past, hear now what is to be:
 From the midnight land,
 Over sea and sand,
 From the green jungle, hear my Voodoo-song;

===Chinese===
>  一段黑色而可怖的记忆攫住了我，
>  那深重冤屈的影与实体；
>  你们既已知道过去，且听将来如何：
>  从这午夜之地，
>  越过海洋与沙滩，
>  从绿色丛林，听我的伏都之歌；

===Original===
> A tropic heat is in my bubbling veins,
 Quintessence of all savagery is mine,
 The lust of ages ripens in my reins,
 And burns
 And yearns,
 Like venom-sap within a noxious vine.

===Chinese===
>  我沸腾的血管里奔涌着热带的酷热，
>  一切野性的精华尽归于我，
>  亘古的贪欲在我的腰肾间成熟，
>  灼烧着，
>  渴望着，
>  像毒藤里流着的毒液。

===Original===
> Was I a heathen? Ay, I was﻿—am still
 A fetish worshipper; but I was free
 To loiter or to wander at my will,
 To leap and dance,
 To hurl my lance,
 And breathe the air of savage liberty.

===Chinese===
>  我曾是个异教徒么？是，我曾是——如今仍是
>  拜物神的人；但我曾自由，
>  可以随意闲荡或漫游，
>  可以腾跃舞蹈，
>  投掷长矛，
>  呼吸野蛮自由的空气。

===Original===
> You drew me to a higher life, you say;
 Ah, drove me, with the lash of slavery!
 Am I unmindful? Every cursed day
 Of pain
 And chain
 Roars like a torrent in my memory.

===Chinese===
>  你说，你把我引向了更高的生活；
>  啊，是用奴隶制的皮鞭驱赶！
>  我岂能忘怀？每一个该诅咒的日子，
>  那些痛苦，
>  那些锁链，
>  都像激流在我记忆里咆哮。

===Original===
> You make my manhood whole with ‘equal rights!’
 Poor empty words! Dream you I honor them?﻿—
 I who have stood on Freedom’s wildest hights?
 My Africa,
 I see the day
 When none dare touch thy garment’s lowest hem.

===Chinese===
>  你用「平等权利！」补全了我的男儿之身！
>  好一串空洞的字眼！你道我敬重它们？——
>  我，可是站上过自由最狂野高峰的人？
>  我的非洲啊，
>  我看见那一天，
>  那时无人敢触碰你衣裾最低的一角。

===Original===
> You cannot make me love you with your whine
 Of fine repentance. Veil your pallid face
 In presence of the shame that mantles mine;
 Stand
 At command
 Of the black prophet of the Negro race!

===Chinese===
>  你休想用你那精致悔罪的
>  呜咽叫我爱你。把你苍白的脸蒙起来吧，
>  在罩上我双颊的羞惭面前；
>  站住，
>  听令
>  于这黑人种族的黑先知！

===Original===
> I hate you, and I live to nurse my hate,
 Remembering when you plied the slaver’s trade
 In my dear land﻿ ﻿… How patiently I wait
 The day,
 Not far away,
 When all your pride shall shrivel up and fade.

===Chinese===
>  我恨你们，我活着就是为了哺育我的恨，
>  记着你们在我亲爱的国土上
>  贩卖奴隶的年月……我何等耐心地等待
>  那一天，
>  它已不远，
>  那时你们一切的骄傲都将枯萎凋零。

===Original===
> Yea, all your whiteness darken under me!
 Darken and be jaundiced, and your blood
 Take in dread humors from my savagery,
 Until
 Your will
 Lapse into mine and seal my masterhood.

===Chinese===
>  是啊，你们周身的白，都要在我之下变黑！
>  变黑，染上黄疸，你们的血
>  要从我的野性里吸进可怕的汁液，
>  直到
>  你们的意志
>  沉没入我的意志，成全我的主宰。

===Original===
> You, seed of Abel, proud of your descent,
 And arrogant, because your cheeks are fair,
 Within my loins an inky curse is pent,
 To flood
 Your blood
 And stain your skin and crisp your golden hair.

===Chinese===
>  你们这亚伯的苗裔，以血统自傲，
>  又因两颊白皙而倨慢，
>  在我的腰腹里，一道墨黑的诅咒正郁积，
>  要漫过
>  你们的血，
>  玷污你们的皮肤，蜷曲你们金色的头发。

===Original===
> As you have done by me, so will I do
 By all the generations of your race;
 Your snowy limbs, your blood’s patrician blue
 Shall be
 Tainted by me,
 And I will set my seal upon your face!

===Chinese===
>  你们怎样待了我，我也要怎样
>  待你们种族的世世代代；
>  你们雪白的肢体，你们血统里贵族的蓝，
>  都要
>  被我玷染，
>  我要在你们脸上打下我的烙印！

===Original===
> Yea, I will dash my blackness down your veins,
 And through your nerves my sensuousness I’ll fling;
 Your lips, your eyes, shall bear the musty stains
 Of Congo kisses,
 While shrieks and hisses
 Shall blend into the savage songs I sing!

===Chinese===
>  是啊，我要把我的黑泼进你们的血管，
>  把我的肉欲甩上你们的神经；
>  你们的唇、你们的眼，都要带着霉暗的斑渍——
>  刚果之吻的斑渍，
>  而尖啸与嘶声
>  将融进我唱的野性之歌！

===Original===
> Your temples will I break, your fountains fill,
 Your cities raze, your fields to deserts turn;
 My heathen fires shall shine on every hill,
 And wild beasts roam,
 Where stands your home;﻿—
 Even the wind your hated dust shall spurn.

===Chinese===
>  我要砸碎你们的殿宇，淤塞你们的泉源，
>  夷平你们的城市，把你们的田亩变成荒漠；
>  我的异教之火要在每一座山头闪耀，
>  野兽要出没在
>  你们家园的旧址上；——
>  连风也要唾弃你们可憎的尘土。

===Original===
> I will absorb your very life in me,
 And mold you to the shape of my desire;
 Back through the cycles of all cruelty
 I will swing you,
 And wring you,
 And roast you in my passions’ hottest fire.

===Chinese===
>  我要把你们的生命吸进我的生命，
>  把你们捏成我心所欲的形状；
>  沿着一切残忍的轮回，
>  我要甩荡你们，
>  拧绞你们，
>  把你们放在我情欲最炽的火上炙烤。

===Original===
> You, North and South, you, East and West,
 Shall drink the cup your fathers gave to me;
 My back still burns, I bare my bleeding breast,
 I set my face,
 My limbs I brace,
 To make the long, strong fight for mastery.

===Chinese===
>  你们，北方与南方，你们，东方与西方，
>  都要喝下你们先人递给我的那杯酒；
>  我的背仍在灼痛，我袒露我流血的胸膛，
>  我板起面孔，
>  我绷紧四肢，
>  要为争霸打一场漫长而坚强的战斗。

===Original===
> My serpent fetish lolls its withered lip
 And bares its shining fangs at thought of this:
 I scarce can hold the monster in my grip.
 So strong is he,
 So eagerly
 He leaps to meet my precious prophecies.

===Chinese===
>  我的蛇神慵懒地垂下它干瘪的唇，
>  一想到这个便亮出闪光的毒牙：
>  我几乎按捺不住这头怪物。
>  它如此强壮，
>  如此急切，
>  巴不得扑向我这珍贵的预言。

===Original===
> Hark for the coming of my countless host,
 Watch for my banner over land and sea.
 The ancient power of vengeance is not lost!
 Lo! on the sky
 The fire-clouds fly,
 And strangely moans the windy, weltering sea.”

===Chinese===
>  且听我无数的大军压境而来，
>  且看我的旌旗席卷陆地与海洋。
>  复仇的太古威力并未消亡！
>  看哪！天上
>  火云翻飞，
>  风声飒飒、波涛翻腾的大海也在异样悲号。」

===Original===
Now this would be poetry if it were only truthful. Simple and sensuous it surely is, but it lacks the third requisite﻿—truth. The Negro is utterly incapable of such vindictiveness. Such concentrated venom might be distilled in the cold Saxon, writhing and chafing under oppression and repression such as the Negro in America has suffered and is suffering. But the black man is in real life only too glad to accept the olive branch of reconciliation. He merely asks to be let alone. To be allowed to pursue his destiny as a free man and an American citizen, to rear and educate his children in peace, to engage in art, science, trades or industries according to his ability﻿—and *to go to the wall if he fail*. He is willing, if I understand him, to let bygones be bygones. He does not even demand satisfaction for the centuries of his ancestors’ unpaid labor. He asks neither pension, nor dole nor back salaries; but is willing to start from the bottom, all helpless and unprovided for as he is, with absolutely nothing as his stock in trade, with no capital, in a country developed, enriched, and made to blossom through his father’s “sweat and toil,”﻿—with none of the accumulations of ancestors’ labors, with no education or moral training for the duties and responsibilities of freedom; nay, with every power, mental, moral, and physical, emasculated by a debasing slavery﻿—he is willing, even glad to take his place in the lists alongside his oppressors, who have had every advantage, to be tried with them by their own standards, and to ask no quarter from them or high Heaven to palliate or excuse the ignominy of a defeat.

===Chinese===
这首诗倘若真实，它便是诗。它确实朴素而诉诸感官，可惜缺了第三要件——真实。黑人绝然怀不出这种睚眦必报的怨毒。这样浓缩的毒液，兴许可以在冷血的撒克逊人身上蒸馏出来——在那辗转于美国黑人已曾遭受、且仍在遭受的压迫与镇压之下的撒克逊人身上。可是现实生活中的黑人，只巴不得接过和解的橄榄枝。他只求人家让他安生。求准许他作为一个自由人和美国公民，去奔赴自己的命运，在平安里养育、教育自己的儿女，依自己的能力去从事艺术、科学、行业或实业——*倘若败下阵来，也甘心认输*。据我对他的了解，他愿意让既往不咎。他甚至不要求为祖先几个世纪的无偿劳役讨一分赔偿。他既不求抚恤，不求施舍，也不求补发欠薪；他甘愿从最底层起步——尽管他这样孤弱无援、一无准备，本钱上绝对一无所有，身无分文，在一个靠他父辈的「血汗与劳苦」才开发、富庶、开花结果的国度里——没有先人劳积的储蓄，没有为自由的义务与责任受过的教育或道德训练；不宁唯是，心智、道德与体力的每一种能力，都被贬抑人格的奴隶制阉割了——他甘愿、甚至乐于在竞技场上与他的压迫者们并肩就位，同他们受同一标准的检验，既不求他们发半点慈悲，也不求上苍开恩，来宽宥或开脱一场败绩的耻辱。

===Original===
The “Voodoo Prophecy” has no interest then as a picture of the black, but merely as a revelation of the white man. Maurice Thompson in penning this portrait of the Negro, has, unconsciously it may be, laid bare his own soul﻿—its secret dread and horrible fear. And this, it seems to me, is the key to the Southern situation, the explanation of the apparent heartlessness and cruelty of some, and the stolid indifference to atrocity on the part of others, before which so many of us have stood paralyzed in dumb dismay. The Southerner is not a cold-blooded villain. Those of us who have studied the genus in its native habitat can testify that his impulses are generous and kindly, and that while the South presents a solid phalanx of iron resistance to the Negro’s advancement, still as individuals to individuals they are warmhearted and often even tender. And just here is the difference between the Southerner and his more philosophical, less sentimental Northern brother. The latter in an abstract metaphysical way rather wants you to have all the rights that belong to you. He thinks it better for the country, better for him that justice, universal justice be done. But he doesn’t care to have the blacks, in the concrete, too near him. He doesn’t know them and doesn’t want to know them. He really can’t understand how the Southerner could have let those little cubs get so close to him as they did in the old days﻿—nursing from the same bottle and feeding at the same breast.

===Chinese===
这样看来，《伏都预言》作为一幅黑人的写照并无趣味，它只是作为白人的一帧透视才有意味。莫里斯·汤普森在落笔描绘这幅黑人肖像时，或许是无意识地，剖开了自己的灵魂——剖开了它隐秘的恐惧与骇人的惶怖。而这，依我看，正是南方局势的钥匙，是某些人表面上的狠心与残忍、另一些人对暴行的麻木不仁的注脚——我们许多人曾在这麻木之前瞠目结舌、束手无策。南方人并不是冷血的恶棍。我们这些在其原生栖息地研究过这一族类的人可以作证：他的冲动是慷慨而和善的；尽管南方对黑人的上进摆出一整列铁壁般的抗拒阵形，一旦以个人对个人，他们仍是热心肠的，往往甚至是温柔的。而恰恰在此，显出南方人同他那位更哲学、较少感伤的北方兄弟的分别。后者以一种抽象的、形而上的方式，倒也愿意你享有属于你的一切权利。他以为正义——普遍的正义——得到伸张，于国家更好，于他自己也更好。但他不愿意具体的有色人离他太近。他不认识他们，也不想去认识。他实在无法理解，南方人当年怎能让那些小崽子们挨得那么近——同吃一只奶瓶，同就一个胸脯吃奶。

===Original===
To the Southerner, on the other hand, race antipathy and color-phobia *as such* does not exist. Personally, there is hardly a man of them but knows, and has known from childhood, some black fellow whom he loves as dearly as if he were white, whom he regards as indispensable to his own pleasures, and for whom he would break every commandment in the decalogue to save him from any general disaster. But our Bourbon seems utterly incapable of generalizing his few ideas. He would die for A or B, but suddenly becomes utterly impervious to every principle of logic when you ask for the simple golden rule to be applied to the class of which A or B is one. Another fact strikes me as curious. A Southern white man’s regard for his black friend varies in inverse ratio to the real distance between them in education and refinement. Puck expresses it﻿—“I can get on a great deal better with a nigger than I can with a Negro.” And Mr. Douglass puts it: “Let a colored man be out at elbows and toes and halfway into the gutter and there is no prejudice against him; but let him respect himself and be a man and Southern whites can’t abide to ride in the same car with him.”

===Chinese===
另一方面，对南方人来说，种族憎恶与恐色症*本身*并不存在。就私人交谊而言，他们几乎人人从小就认识、并且一向认识某位他钟爱的黑人，爱得像对白人一样深，视之为他自己种种乐趣所不可或缺，并且为了使他免于任何一场大难，十诫里的每一条他都肯破。可是我们的这位波旁先生——南方那种老派的顽固保守派——似乎全然无能把自己的点滴观念稍加概括。他肯为张三或李四去死，可一旦你要求把那条简单的金律施用于张三或李四所属的整个阶级，他顿时对一切逻辑原理绝缘。还有一桩事实令我觉得蹊跷：南方白人对他黑人朋友的看重，同两人在教育与教养上的真实距离恰成反比。《帕克》（Puck）杂志有妙语道此——「同『黑鬼』打交道，可比同『黑人』打交道顺心多了。」而道格拉斯先生的说法是：「让一个有色人衣衫褴褛、半截身子跌进阴沟，那就什么偏见也没有；可他一旦自尊自重、立身为人，南方白人便连同一节车厢也坐不得了。」

===Original===
Why this anomaly? Is it pride? Ordinarily, congeniality increases with similarity in taste and manners. Is it antipathy to color? It does not exist. The explanation is the white man’s dread dimly shadowed out in this “Voodoo Prophecy” of Maurice Thompson, and fed and inspired by such books as Minden Armais and a few wild theorizers who have nothing better to do with their time than spend it advocating the fusion of races as a plausible and expedient policy. Now I believe there are two ideas which master the Southern white man and incense him against the black race. On this point he is a monomaniac. In the face of this feeling he would not admit he was convinced of the axioms of Geometry. The one is personal and present, the fear of Negro political domination. The other is for his posterity﻿—the future horror of being lost as a race in this virile and vigorous black race. Relieve him of this nightmare and he becomes “as gentle as the sucking dove.” With that dread delusion maddening him he would drive his sword to the hilt in the tender breast of his darling child, did he fancy that through her the curse would come.

===Chinese===
何以有此乖异？是骄傲么？寻常而言，情投意合本随趣味与举止的相似而俱增。是对颜色的憎恶么？它并不存在。解释就在白人的那份恐惧里——它在这首汤普森的《伏都预言》里投下昏暗的影子，又被诸如《明登·阿尔迈斯》（Minden Armais）之类的书和几位游谈无根的理论家喂养、煽动着——这些人别无更好的事可做，便把光阴花在鼓吹种族融合上，奉之为貌似有理、相机行事的政策。如今我相信，有两种观念主宰着南方白人，使他向黑人种族燃起怒火。在这一点上，他是个偏执狂。在这股情绪面前，他连几何学的公理也不肯认账。一种是个人的、当下的：对黑人政治主宰的恐惧。另一种是为他的子孙的：对作为一个种族消融在这雄强旺盛的黑种族之中的未来恐怖。替他解除了这场噩梦，他便「温驯得像一只乳鸽」。倘若那可怕的妄念使他发狂，那么哪怕是他心爱的女儿，只要他疑心诅咒将由她而来，他也肯把剑直没至柄地捅进她温柔的胸膛。

===Original===
Now argument is almost supersensible with a monomaniac. What is most needed is a sedative for the excited nerves, and then a mental tonic to stimulate the power of clear perception and truthful cerebration. The Southern patient needs to be brought to see, by the careful and cautious injection of cold facts and by the presentation of well selected object lessons that so far as concerns his first named horror of black supremacy politically, the usual safeguards of democracy are in the hands of intelligence and wealth in the South as elsewhere. The weapons of fair argument and persuasion, the precautionary bulwark of education and justice, the unimpeachable supremacy and insuperable advantage of intelligence and discipline over mere numbers﻿—are all in his reach. It is to his interest to help make the black peasant an intelligent and self-respecting citizen. No section can thrive under the incubus of an illiterate, impoverished, cheerless and hopeless peasantry. Let the South once address herself in good faith to the improvement of the condition of her laboring classes, let her give but a tithe of the care and attention which are bestowed in the North on its mercurial and inflammable importations, let her show but the disposition in her relative poverty merely to utter the benediction, *Be ye warmed and fed and educated*, even while she herself has not the wherewithal to emulate the Pullman villages and the Carnegie munificence, let her but give him a fair wage and an honest reckoning and a kindly Godspeed﻿—and she will find herself in possession of the most tractable laborer, the most faithful and reliable henchman, the most invaluable cooperator and friendly vassal of which this or any country can boast.

===Chinese===
同偏执狂讲道理，几乎是超乎感官的事。最需要的是一剂镇静药，先安顿他亢奋的神经，然后是一服心智的补剂，去激励清明知觉与如实思考的能力。这位南方的病人需要被引导着看到——通过小心翼翼地注射冷冰冰的事实、通过陈列精心挑选的实物教材——就他指名在先的那场对黑人政治主宰的恐惧而言，民主政治的寻常保障，同在别处一样，握在南方的心智与财富的手中。公平说理与劝服的兵器，教育加正义这道防波大堤，心智与纪律对单纯人数那无可置疑的优势——都伸手可及。帮助把黑人农民造就成有智识、有自尊的公民，正是他的利益所在。没有一个地区，能在文盲遍地、贫困潦倒、愁苦无望的农民阶级的重负下兴旺。只要南方真心实意致力于改善其劳动阶级的境况，只要她拿出北方倾注于那些喜怒无常、一点即着的进口移民的关怀与照拂的十分之一，只要她在相对的贫困中稍稍表示出这份心意，仅仅道一声祝福——*愿你们穿得暖、吃得饱、受得了教育*——哪怕她一时拿不出可供效法普尔曼新村与卡内基式慷慨的资财；只要给他公道的工资、诚实的结账与一声亲切的祝一路平安——她将发现自己坐拥这个国家或任何国家所能夸耀的最驯良的劳工、最忠实可靠的心腹、最无价的合作者与友好的附庸。

===Original===
So far as regards the really less sane idea that amicable relations subsisting between the races may promote their ultimate blending and loss of identity, it hardly seems necessary to refute it. Blending of races in the aggregate is simply an unthinkable thought, and the union of individuals can never fall out by accident or haphazard. There must be the deliberate wish and intention on each side; and the average black man in this country is as anxious to preserve his identity and transmit his type as is the average white man. In any case, hybridity is in no sense dependent on sectional or national amity. Oppression and outrage are not the means to chain the affections. Cupid, who knows no bolt or bars, is more wont to be stimulated with romantic sympathy towards a forbidden object unjustly persecuted. The sensible course is to remove those silly and unjust barriers which protect nothing and merely call attention to the possibilities of law-breaking, and depend instead on religion and common sense to guide, control and direct in the paths of purity and right reason.

===Chinese===
至于那个实在更欠清明的观念——以为两族之间和睦相处的友好关系，会促成他们最终的混血与特质的消融——似乎根本无须驳斥。种族在总体上的混合，根本是一个不可想象的想法；而个体之间的结合，永远不可能出于偶然或侥幸。双方必须各有郑重的心愿与意图；而我国的普通黑人，同普通白人一样，急切于保持自己的特质、传续自己的类型。何况，混血绝不依赖地区间或国家间的和睦。压迫与暴行不是锁住爱情的锁链。丘比特本不识门闩与铁栅，倒更容易被浪漫的同情心所激发——当禁果遭到不义的迫害之时。明智的办法，是撤除那些既护不住什么、又唯恐人不知犯禁之可能的愚蠢而不义的壁垒，转而仰赖宗教与常识，在纯洁与正理的路上引导人、约束人、指引人。

===Original===
The froth and foam, the sticks and debris at the water-top may have an uncertain movement, but as deep calleth unto deep the mighty ocean swell is always true to the tides; and whatever the fluctuations along the ragged edge between the races, the home instinct is sufficiently strong with each to hold the great mass true to its attractions. If Maurice Thompson’s nightmare vision is sincere on his part, then, it has no objective reality; ’tis merely a hideous phantasm bred of his own fevered and jaundiced senses; if he does not believe in it himself, it was most unkind and uncalled for to publish abroad such inflaming and irritating fabrications.

===Chinese===
水面上的泡沫与浮渣、断枝与碎屑，或许飘移无定；但深渊与深渊响应，大洋的巨涌永远忠于潮汐；无论种族交界那犬牙交错的水线如何涨落，家的本能在两族都足够强大，足以把大群体牢牢吸定在自己的引力之上。倘若莫里斯·汤普森的噩梦之象出于他的真诚，那么它也没有客观的真实；它不过是他自己发热的、黄疸的感官孵化出的一具狞恶幻影；倘若连他自己也不信，那么把这样煽风点火、刺激性情的虚构之词散布于世，便未免刻毒而无谓了。

===Original===
After this cursory glance at a few contributions which have peculiarly emphasized one phase of our literature during the last decade or two, I am brought to the conclusion that an authentic portrait, at once aesthetic and true to life, presenting the black man as a free American citizen, not the humble slave of *Uncle Tom’s Cabin*﻿—but the *man*, divinely struggling and aspiring yet tragically warped and distorted by the adverse winds of circumstance, has not yet been painted. It is my opinion that the canvas awaits the brush of the colored man himself. It is a pathetic﻿—a fearful arraignment of America’s conditions of life, that instead of that enrichment from the years and days, the summers and springs under which, as Browning says,

===Chinese===
在对过去一二十年里特别标举了我们文学这一侧面的几项贡献作了这番匆匆一瞥之后，我得出的结论是：一幅既具审美价值、又忠实于生活的本真肖像——呈现黑人作为自由的美国公民，不是《汤姆叔叔的小屋》里那卑顺的奴隶，而是那个*人*：神圣地奋斗着、憧憬着，却被命运的逆风悲惨地扭曲、压弯——至今还没有人画出来。依我之见，画布正在等待黑人自己的画笔。这是对美利坚生活境况的一纸令人恻然的——可怕的弹劾：本该随着岁岁月月、暑往春来的滋养——在那滋养之下，如勃朗宁所说——

===Original===
> “The flowers turn double and the leaves turn flowers,”﻿—

===Chinese===
> 「花朵开成重瓣，绿叶也变作花朵」——

===Original===
the black man’s native and original flowers have in this country been all hardened and sharpened into thorns and spurs. In literature we have no artists for art’s sake. Albery A. Whitman in *Twasinta’s Seminoles* and *Not a Man and Yet a Man* is almost the only poet who has attempted a more sustained note than the lyrics of Mrs. Harper, and even that note is almost a wail.

===Chinese===
——黑人与生俱来的、原创的花朵，在这个国家里却尽数硬化、锐化成了荆棘与距刺。在我们的文学里，还没有为艺术而艺术的艺术家。阿尔伯里·A·惠特曼（Albery A. Whitman）的《特瓦辛塔的塞米诺尔人》（*Twasinta's Seminoles*）与《虽非男儿，亦是男儿》（*Not a Man and Yet a Man*），几乎是仅有的在哈珀夫人（Mrs. Harper）抒情短章之外尝试更绵长之音的诗作，而连那音调也近乎一缕恸哭。

===Original===
The fact is, a sense of freedom in mind as well as in body is necessary to the appreciative and inspiring pursuit of the beautiful. A bird cannot warble out his fullest and most joyous notes while the wires of his cage are pricking and cramping him at every heart beat. His tones become only the shrill and poignant protest of rage and despair. And so the black man’s vexations and chafing environment, even since his physical emancipation has given him speech, has goaded him into the eloquence and fire of oratory rather than the genial warmth and cheery glow of either poetry or romance. And pity ’tis, ’tis true. A race that has produced for America the only folklore and folk songs of native growth, a race which has grown the most original and unique assemblage of fable and myth to be found on the continent, a race which has suggested and inspired almost the only distinctive American note which could chain the attention and charm the ear of the outside world﻿—has as yet found no mouthpiece of its own to unify and perpetuate its wondrous whisperings﻿—no painter-poet to distil in the alembic of his own imagination the gorgeous dyes, the luxuriant juices of this rich and tropical vegetation. It was the glory of Chaucer that he justified the English language to itself﻿—that he took the homely and hitherto despised Saxon elements and ideas, and lovingly wove them into an artistic product which even Norman conceit and uppishness might be glad to acknowledge and imitate. The only man who is doing the same for Negro folklore is one not to the manner born. Joel Chandler Harris has made himself rich and famous by simply standing around among the black railroad hands and cotton pickers of the South and compiling the simple and dramatic dialogues which fall from their lips. What I hope to see before I die is a black man honestly and appreciatively portraying both the Negro as he is, and the white man, occasionally, as seen from the Negro’s standpoint.

===Chinese===
事实是，唯有身心的双重自由感，方能助人怀着欣赏与鼓舞去追求美。一只鸟，只要笼丝还在每一次心跳处扎它、夹它，就唱不出最饱满、最欢畅的歌声；它的音调，只会变成愤怒与绝望的尖利而锥心的抗议。黑人也如此——甚至在他肉体的解放给了他言语之后——那处处硌人的环境仍驱策他，走向雄辩与辞章的烈火，而不是诗歌或传奇的温煦与暖辉。可悲啊，而这是实情。一个为美利坚贡献了本土仅有的民间传说与民歌的种族，一个繁育出这块大陆上最具原创性、最独一无二的寓言与神话之总汇的种族，一个启发并造就了几乎唯一能锁住外界耳目、令世界倾耳之美国音调的种族——至今还没有自己的喉舌，来统一并流传它神妙的低语——还没有一位画家兼诗人，来在他自己想象的蒸馏瓶里，蒸馏这热带般丰茂的植被的华彩与浓汁。乔叟（Chaucer）的荣耀，在于他向英语自身证明了英语——他拾起家常而久遭轻贱的撒克逊元素与撒克逊观念，用爱心把它们织成一件艺术品，连诺曼人的自负与傲慢也乐于承认、乐于摹仿。而今为黑人民间传说做着同一件事的唯一一人，却并非生得其土。乔尔·钱德勒·哈里斯（Joel Chandler Harris），不过是在南方的铁路工人与采棉人中间站一站，把他们唇间落下的简单而富于戏剧性的对白编纂成册，便已因此致富成名。我在有生之年希望看到的，是一个黑人，诚实而怀赏识地描画黑人本来的样子，并且偶尔，也描画从黑人的立足点上望见的白人。

===Original===
There is an old proverb “The devil is always painted *black*﻿—by white painters.” And what is needed, perhaps, to reverse the picture of the lordly man slaying the lion, is for the lion to turn painter.

===Chinese===
有一句古谚：「魔鬼总是被画成*黑的*——执笔的是白人画师。」而要颠倒这幅威严的主人搏杀雄狮的图画，所需要的，或许正是让狮子自己拿起画笔。

===Original===
Then too we need the calm clear judgment of ourselves and of others born of a disenchantment similar to that of a little girl I know in the South, who was once being laboriously held up over the shoulders of a surging throng to catch her first glimpse of a real live president. “Why Nunny,” she cried half reproachfully, as she strained her little neck to see﻿—“*It’s nuffin but a man!*”

===Chinese===
我们还需要一种对己对人的冷静清明的判断，它源自一种幻梦的初醒，好比南方一个我熟识的小女孩。她有一回被人费力地举过攒动人群的肩头，想第一眼望一望活生生的总统。「哎呀南妮，」她伸长小脖子张望时，半带嗔怪地喊道——「*没啥，不过是个人罢了！*」

===Original===
When we have been sized up and written down by others, we need not feel that the last word is said and the oracles sealed. “It’s nuffin but a man.” And there are many gifts the giftie may gie us, far better than seeing ourselves as others see us﻿—and one is that of Bion’s maxim “*Know Thyself*.” Keep true to your own ideals. Be not ashamed of what is homely and your own. Speak out and speak honestly. Be true to yourself and to the message God and Nature meant you to deliver. The young David cannot fight in Saul’s unwieldy armor. Let him simply therefore gird his loins, take up his own parable and tell this would-be great American nation “*A chile’s amang ye takin’ notes*;” and when men act the part of cowards or wild beasts, this great silent but open-eyed constituency has a standard by which they are being tried. Know thyself, and know those around at their true weight of solid intrinsic manhood without being dazzled by the fact that littleness of soul is often gilded with wealth, power and intellect. There can be no nobility but that of soul, and no catalogue of adventitious circumstances can wipe out the stain or palliate the meanness of inflicting one ruthless, cruel wrong. ’Tis not only safer, but nobler, grander, diviner,

===Chinese===
当我们被他人品评已定、论断已下，我们不必觉得这就是最后的判词、神谕已封。「没啥，不过是个人罢了。」彭斯所祈的那份天赋所能赐我们的恩物很多，远胜于「如他人之眼看见我们自己」——其中之一，便是比昂（Bion）的箴言「*认识你自己*（Know Thyself）」。忠于你自己的理想。不要以家常己有之物为耻。开口说话，诚实地说。忠于你自己，忠于上帝与自然命你传递的那个音信。年幼的大卫穿不惯扫罗那笨重的铠甲。就让他束起腰来，提起自己的预言，对这个自命伟大的美利坚民族说一声：「*有个小子正在你们中间记着笔记呢*（A chile's amang ye takin' notes）；」而当有人扮演懦夫或野兽的角色时，这个伟大的、沉默而睁着眼的选民群体，自有一杆衡量他们的标尺。认识你自己，也按其坚实内在的人格分量认识你周围的人，不要因灵魂的渺小往往镀着金钱、权势与智识的金光而目眩。除了灵魂的高贵，别无高贵可言；外在境遇的任何花名册，都抹不掉那污点，也减轻不了施加一桩无情残忍之冤的卑劣。这样做不仅更安稳，而且更高贵、更宏伟、更神圣——

===Original===
> “To be that which we destroy
 Than, by destruction, dwell in doubtful joy.”

===Chinese===
> 「宁做那被我们毁灭的人，
>  也胜过凭着毁灭，栖身于疑惧的欢愉。」（莎士比亚《麦克白》，麦克白夫人语）

===Original===
With this platform to stand on we can with clear eye weigh what is written and estimate what is done and ourselves paint what is true with the calm spirit of those who know their cause is right and who believe there is a God who judgeth the nations.

===Chinese===
站在这个立足点上，我们便能以澄明之眼衡量所写的文字、估量所做的事情，并以自知其事业正当、且信有一位审判列邦的上帝之人的沉静心怀，亲自画下真实。

### What Are We Worth? / 我们的价值何在？

===Original===
I once heard Henry Ward Beecher make this remark: “Were Africa and the Africans to sink tomorrow, how much poorer would the world be? A little less gold and ivory, a little less coffee, a considerable ripple, perhaps, where the Atlantic and Indian Oceans would come together﻿—that is all; not a poem, not an invention, not a piece of art would be missed from the world.”

===Chinese===
我曾听亨利·沃德·比彻（Henry Ward Beecher）说过这样一句话：「倘若明天非洲和非洲人沉入海底，世界会穷多少？不过是少一点黄金和象牙，少一点咖啡，或许在大西洋与印度洋相合之处泛起一圈可观的涟漪——如此而已；世上不会少一首诗、少一项发明、少一件艺术品。」

===Original===
This is not a flattering statement; but then we do not want flattery if seeing ourselves as others see us is to help us in fulfilling the higher order, “know thyself.” The world is often called cold and hard. I don’t know much about that; but of one thing I am sure, it is intensely practical. Waves of sentiment or prejudice may blur its old eyes for a little while but you are sure to have your bill presented first or last with the inexorable “How much owest thou?” What have you produced, what consumed? What is your real value in the world’s economy? What do you give to the world over and above what you have cost? What would be missed had you never lived? What are you worth? What of actual value would go down with you if you were sunk into the ocean or buried by an earthquake tomorrow? Show up your cash account and your balance sheet. In the final reckoning do you belong on the debit or the credit side of the account? according to a fair and square, an impartial and practical reckoning. It is by this standard that society estimates individuals; and by this standard finally and inevitably the world will measure and judge nations and races.

===Chinese===
这番话并不动听；然而，倘若「以别人眼中的我看自己」有助于我们践行「认识你自己」（know thyself）这条更高的谕令，我们便不想要动听的谀辞。世人常说这个世界冷酷无情。这一点我不甚了然；但有一件事我确信不疑：它极其讲求实际。情感或偏见的波澜，或许会一时模糊它昏花的老眼，但或迟或早，你必定会收到它呈来的账单，附着那句铁面无私的「你欠多少？」（How much owest thou?）你生产了什么，消费了什么？在世界的大经济中，你的真正价值几何？除去你的耗费，你给世界多贡献了什么？倘若你从未活过，会有什么被人怀念？你价值几何？倘若明天你沉入大洋或被地震埋葬，什么实际的价值会随你一同湮灭？亮出你的现金账目和资产负债表吧。在最终的清算里，按一场公平正直、不偏不倚、切合实际的结算，你究竟记在这本账的借方还是贷方？社会正是依这个标准估量个人；而依这个标准，世界最终也必将衡量并裁判各个民族与种族。

===Original===
It may not be unprofitable then for us to address ourselves to the task of casting up our account and carefully overhauling our books. It may be well to remember at the outset that the operation is purely a mathematical one and allows no room for sentiment. The good housewife’s pet chicken which she took when first hatched, fed from her own hand and fondled on her bosom as lovingly as if it were a babe, is worth no more (for all the affection and care lavished on it) when sold in the shambles: and that never-to-be-forgotten black hen that stole into the parlor, flew upon the mantel looking for a nest among those handsome curios, smashed the Sèvres vases and picked the buds from the lovely tea rose﻿—so exasperatingly that the good woman could never again endure the sight of her﻿—this ill-fated bird is worth no less. There are sections of this country in which the very name of the Negro, even in homeopathic doses, stirs up such a storm of feeling that men fairly grow wild and are unfit to discuss the simplest principles of life and conduct where the colored man is concerned; and you would think it necessary for the Ethiopian actually to change his skin before there can be any harmonious living or lucid thinking: there are a few nooks and crannies, on the other hand, in another quarter of the same country, in which that name embodies an idealized theory and a benevolent sentiment; and the black man (the blacker the better) is the petted nursling, the haloed idea, the foregone conclusion. In these Arcadias, it is as good capital as pushing selfishness and aspiring mediocrity need ask, to be advertised as one of the oppressed race and probably born a slave.

===Chinese===
如此说来，着手盘算我们的账目、仔细稽核我们的账簿，于我们未必无益。开宗明义便须记住：这道工序纯粹是数学的，容不得半点感情。善良的主妇那只心爱的小鸡——刚破壳便抱来，亲手喂养，搂在怀里爱抚，宛如疼一个婴孩——待到送进肉档出售时（尽管倾注了那么多疼爱与照料），身价并不因之增加分毫；而那只令人永难忘怀的黑母鸡，溜进客厅，飞上壁炉架，想在那些漂亮古玩中间寻个窝，砸碎了塞夫勒（Sèvres）瓷瓶，又把娇艳的香水月季的花苞啄了个精光——气得那位善良的主妇从此再见不得它——这只倒霉的鸟儿，身价也并不因之减少分毫。这个国家的某些地区，单是「黑人」这个名目，哪怕只消顺势疗法剂量的一星半点，也足以掀起这样一场情感风暴，使人简直发狂，一涉及有色人便连最简单的生活与行为准则也讨论不成：你会以为非等古实人果真换了皮肤，才谈得上和谐的共处与清明的思想；另一方面，在同一个国家的另一隅，也有几处角落旮旯，这个名字在那里凝结成一套理想化的理论与一副仁善的情肠；黑人（越黑越好）是受人宠爱的婴孩、罩着光轮的观念、不证自明的结论。在这些阿卡迪亚（Arcadia）式的世外桃源里，只要标榜自己是受压迫种族的一员、多半生来为奴，便是一份足令急进的自私与攀高的平庸都无可再求的上好资本。

===Original===
But after all sentiment, whether adverse or favorable, is ephemeral. Ever shifting and unreliable, it can never be counted in estimating values. The sentiments of youth are outgrown in age, and we like today what we despised or were indifferent to yesterday. Nine-tenths of the miscalled color prejudice or race prejudice in this country is mere sentiment governed by the association of ideas. It is not color prejudice at all. The color of a man’s face per se has no more to do with his worthiness and companionableness than the color of his eyes or the shades of his hair. You admire the one or think the other more beautiful to rest the gaze upon. But everyone with brains knows and must admit that he must look deeper than this for the man. Mrs. Livermore once said in my hearing: “It is not that the Negro is black; Spaniards, Portuguese, East Indians, enter our parlors, sup at our tables, and, if they have a sufficiently long bank account, they may marry our daughters: but the Negro is weak﻿—and we don’t like weakness.”

===Chinese===
然而，无论厌憎还是偏爱，情感终究朝生暮死。它变幻不定、不可倚恃，估算价值时永远不能计入。青年时的情感到老境便被超越；我们今天喜爱的，正是昨天所鄙夷或漠然的东西。这个国家被误称为肤色偏见或种族偏见的东西，十之八九不过是受观念联想支配的情感。那根本算不得肤色偏见。一个人面孔的颜色本身，与其人之值得敬重、宜于交往与否，关系并不比他眼睛的颜色或头发的深浅更大。你欣赏这一种，或觉得那一种更宜于入眼。但凡有脑筋的人都知道、也必须承认：要找这个人，他必须看得比这更深。利弗莫尔夫人（Mary A. Livermore）有一次当着我的面说：「问题不在于黑人是黑的；西班牙人、葡萄牙人、东印度人照样进我们的客厅、上我们的餐桌，只要他们的银行账户够长，还可以娶我们的女儿：可黑人是软弱的——而我们不喜欢软弱。」

===Original===
Now this dislike it is useless to inveigh against and folly to rail at. We share it ourselves and often carry it to a more unjustifiable extent. For as a rule the narrower the mind and the more circumscribed the experience, the greater will be the exaggeration of accidents over substance, and of circumstance over soul. It does no good to argue with the poor seasick wretch who, even on land after the voyage, is nauseated by the sight of clear spring water. In vain you show the unreason of the feeling. This, you explain, is a different time, a different place, a different stage of progress in the circulation of waters. That was salt, this is fresh, and so on. You might as well be presenting syllogisms to Aetna. “Yes, my dear Fellow,” he cries, “You talk admirably; but you don’t know how I feel. You don’t know how sick I was on that nasty ship!” And so your rhetoric cannot annihilate the association of ideas. He feels; *you know*. But he will outgrow his feeling﻿—and you are content to wait.

===Chinese===
对这种厌恶，痛加抨击无济于事，破口大骂更是愚妄。我们自己同样怀有它，而且往往推到更没有道理的地步。因为大抵心智越狭、阅历越陋，便越是把偶然夸大过本质、把境遇抬高过灵魂。同那个晕船的可怜虫争辩没有用处：即便航程已毕、人已登岸，他一见清冽的泉水仍要作呕。你向他指出这种感受之无理，也是徒然。你解释说：此一时也，彼一时也；此一地也，彼一地也；水的循环已进入另一个阶段。那是咸的，这是淡的，云云。你简直是在向埃特纳火山（Aetna）陈列三段论。「是啊，亲爱的伙计，」他嚷道，「你说得头头是道；可你不知道我是什么感受。你不知道我在那条破船上晕得有多厉害！」于是你的雄辩终究消灭不了观念的联想。他在感受；*你知道*。但他终会长大、脱出这份感受——而你也甘愿等待。

===Original===
Just as impervious to reason is the man who is dominated by the sentiment of race prejudice. You can only consign him to the fatherly hand of Time; and pray that your own mental sight be not thus obscured and your judgment warped in your endeavors to be just and true.

===Chinese===
受种族偏见情感支配的人，同样不为道理所动。你只能把他交托到时间老父的手里；并祈求你自己的心眼，在力求公正与真实之际，不致同样遭翳、你的判断不致同样扭曲。

===Original===
Sentiment and cant, then, both being ruled out, let us try to study our subject as the world finally reckons it﻿—not certain crevices and crannies of the earth, but the cool, practical, businesslike world. What are we worth? not in Georgia nor in Massachusetts; not to our brothers and sisters and cousins and aunts, every one of whom would unhesitatingly declare us worth a great gold-lump; nor to the exasperated neighbor over the way who would be just as ready, perhaps, to write us down a most unmitigated nuisance. But what do we represent to the world? What is our market value. Are we a positive and additive quantity or a negative factor in the world’s elements. What have we cost and what do we come to?

===Chinese===
于是，情感与空论既已双双排除，就让我们按世界最终结算的方式来研究我们的题目——不是按地球这一处那一处的罅隙角落，而是按这个冷静、实际、公事公办的世界。我们价值几何？不是在佐治亚，也不是在马萨诸塞；不是对我们的兄弟姐妹姑舅姨婶——他们每一位都会毫不迟疑地宣称我们值一大块金锭；也不是对马路对面那位怒不可遏的邻人——他或许同样乐于把我们记作一个不折不扣的祸害。而是要问：对世界，我们代表着什么？我们的市价几何？在世界诸元素中，我们是一个正的、可加的量，还是一个负的因子？我们耗费了几何，又折合几许？

===Original===
The calculation may be made in the same way and on the same principle that we would estimate the value of any commodity on the market. Men are not very unlike watches. We might estimate first the cost of material﻿—is it gold or silver or alloy, solid or plated, jewelled or sham paste. Settle the relative value of your raw material, and next you want to calculate how much this value has been enhanced by labor the delicacy and fineness, the honesty and thoroughness of the workmanship; then the utility and beauty of the product and its adaptability to the end and purpose of its manufacture; and lastly is there a demand in the market for such an article. Does it meet a want, *will it go* and *go right*? Is it durable and reliable. How often do you have to wind it before it runs down, how often repair it. Does it keep good time and require but little watching and looking after. And there is no radical difference, after all, between the world’s way of estimating men and our usual way of valuing watches. In both the fundamental item is the question of material, and then the refining and enhancement of that material through labor, and so on through the list.

===Chinese===
这笔账，可以照我们估算市面上任何一件商品之价值的方式与原则来计算。人与表相去不远。我们可以先估材料的成本——是金是银还是合金，是实心是镀层，是镶了宝石还是冒牌料子。定下原材料的相对价值之后，接下来要计算这份价值经由劳动提升了多少——做工的精微与细致、诚实与彻底；再估产品的效用与美观，及其是否合于制造的用途与目的；最后，市面可有对这样一件货品的需求。它是否满足一种需要，*它走得动*，而且*走得准*？它经久可靠么？多久不上弦便会停摆，多久须修一次？它计时准不准，要不要人时时看护照料？说到底，世界估量人的方式，与我们通常估价表的方式，并无根本的不同。两者之中，首要的项目都是材料问题，其次是劳动对材料的精炼与增值，如此逐项推演下去。

===Original===
What then can we say for our raw material?

===Chinese===
那么，关于我们的原材料，我们能说什么呢？

===Original===
Again I must preface an apology for anything unpalatable in our menu. I promised, you remember, to leave out the sentiment﻿—you may stir it in afterwards, mixing thoroughly according to taste. We must discuss facts, candidly and bluntly, without rhetoric or cant if we would have a clear light on our problem.

===Chinese===
这里我得再为菜单上可能难以下咽的东西预致歉意。你总记得，我答应过把感情撇出去——回头你可以再把它搅进来，随口味拌匀。若要让我们的问题得一个清明的亮光，我们就必须坦率而直截地讨论事实，不假辞藻，不说空话。

===Original===
Now whatever notions we may indulge on the theory of evolution and the laws of atavism or heredity, all concede that no individual character receives its raw material newly created and independent of the rock from whence it was hewn. No life is bound up within the period of its conscious existence. No personality dates its origin from its birthday. The elements that are twisted into the cord did not begin their formation when first the tiny thread became visible in the great warp and filling of humanity. When first we saw the light many of the threads undoubtedly were spun and the color and fineness of the weft determined. The materials that go to make the man, the probabilities of his character and activities, the conditions and circumstances of his growth, and his quantum of resistance and mastery are the resultant of forces which have been accumulating and gathering momentum for generations. So that, as one tersely expresses it, in order to reform a man, you must begin with his great grandmother.

===Chinese===
如今，无论我们对进化论、对隔代遗传或遗传的法则作何想法，人人都承认：没有哪一个人的品格，其原材料是凭空新造、可以离弃它被凿出来的那块山岩的。没有哪一重生命，会被封在自己有意识地存在的这段岁月之内。没有哪一个人格，是从它的生日才开始起算的。搓成绳股的纤维，并不等到那根细线初次显现于人类巨大的经纬之中才开始成形。我们初见天光之时，许多线缕无疑早已纺就，纬纱的颜色与粗细也早已注定。构成此人的材料、他品格与活动的可能性、他生长的条件与境遇、他的抵抗力与掌控力，都是种种力量的合力，而这些力量已经积聚增益了世世代代。所以，正如有人言简意赅地说过的：要改造一个人，你必须从他的曾祖母着手。

===Original===
A few years ago a certain social scientist was struck by a remarkable coincidence in the name of a number of convicts in the State prison of New York. There were found thirty-five or forty men, of the same name with but slight modification in the spelling, all convicted of crimes similar in character. Looking into the matter, he traced them every one back to one woman of inferior character who had come from England in one of the first colonial ships. *And that woman had been a convict and charged with pretty nearly the same crime.*

===Chinese===
几年前，某位社会科学家为纽约州立监狱中一批囚犯姓氏的惊人巧合所触动。他发现三十五或四十个男人，姓氏相同、仅拼法上略有出入，全都因性质相似的罪行定罪。追查之下，他把他们一一追溯到一个品行卑下的女人身上——她乘最早的殖民船之一来自英格兰。*而那个女人本身便是个罪犯，所犯的罪名与这批人几乎如出一辙。*

===Original===
Rightly to estimate our material, then, it is necessary to go back of the twenty or thirty years during which we have been in possession, and find out the nature of the soil in which it has been forming and growing.

===Chinese===
因此，要正确估量我们的材料，就必须回溯到我们持有它的这二三十年之前，查明它得以孕育生长的那片土壤的性质。

===Original===
There is or used to be in England a system of entail by which a lot of land was fixed to a family and its posterity forever, passing always on the death of the father to his eldest son. A man may misuse or abuse, he may impoverish, mortgage, sterilize, eliminate every element of value﻿—but he can never sell. He may cut down every tree, burn every fence and house, abstract by careless tillage, or by no tillage, every nutritive element from the soil, encumber it to two or three times its value and destroy forever its beauty and fertility﻿—but he can never rid himself of it. That land with all its encumbrances and liabilities, its barrenness and squalidness, its poverty and its degradation is inexorably, inevitably, inalienably his; and like a shattered and debased personality it haunts him wherever he goes. An heir coming into an estate is thus often poorer than if he had no inheritance. He is chained to a lifelong possession of debt, toil, responsibility, often disgrace. Happier were it for him if he could begin life with nothing﻿—an isolated but free man with no capital but his possibilities, with no past and no pedigree. And so it often is with men. These bodies of ours often come to us mortgaged to their full value by the extravagance, self-indulgence, sensuality of some ancestor. Some man, generations back, has encumbered his estate for strong drink, his descendants coming into that estate have the mortgage to pay off, principal and interest. Another cut down the fences of character by debauchery and vice﻿—and these have to ward off attacks of the enemy without bulwarks or embattlements. They have burnt their houses of purity and integrity, have rendered the soil poor and unproductive by extravagance and folly﻿—and the children have to shiver amid the storms of passion and feed on husks till they can build for themselves a shelter and fertilize their farms. Not very valuable estates, you will say. Well, no﻿—nothing to boast of, perhaps. But an energetic heir can often pay off some of the liabilities and leave the estate to his children less involved than when he received it. At least he can arrest the work of destruction and see to it that no further encumbrances are added through his folly and mismanagement.

===Chinese===
英格兰有一种（或者说曾经有一种）限定继承制：一片土地被永远固定给一个家族及其后裔，父亲一死便总是传给长子。一个人可以滥用、糟蹋，可以使它贫瘠、抵押、荒芜，耗尽其中每一种价值要素——但他永远不能出售。他可以砍光每一棵树，烧掉每一道围篱和每一座房屋，以粗放的耕作或索性全不耕作，把土壤的养分攫取殆尽，使它负上两三倍于其价值的债累，永远毁掉它的秀美与丰饶——但他永远摆脱不掉它。那片土地，连同它全部的债累与责任、荒瘠与腌臜、贫困与堕落，铁面无私地、无可逃遁地、不可让渡地属于他；又如一个破碎堕落的人格，他走到哪里，它便跟到哪里。这样，继承一份产业的人往往比没有继承反倒更穷。他被锁进一份终身的占有：债务、劳苦、责任，往往还有耻辱。他若能白手起家倒更幸福——一个孤零却自由的人，除了自身的可能性之外身无长物，没有过去，也没有家谱。人也往往如此。我们这副身体来到我们手中时，常常已被某位祖先的奢靡、放纵与耽于情欲抵押到了足值。某个人隔了几代，为烈饮而抵押了祖产，子孙承产时便须连本带利偿清那笔抵押。另一位以放浪与邪恶拆毁了品格的围篱——这些人便只得在没有堡垒与壁垒的情况下抵御仇敌的攻击。他们烧掉了纯洁与正直的房舍，以奢靡与愚行把土壤弄得贫瘠不毛——儿女们便只得在激情的风暴里瑟缩，拿豆荚充饥，直到能为自己筑起一个庇护所、为自己的田地施肥。你会说，这不是什么值钱的产业。唉，确实——或许没什么可夸耀的。但一个精力充沛的继承人，往往能偿清一部分负债，把产业交到儿女手中时，比他接手时不那么债台高筑。至少他可以遏止那场破坏，留心别再因自己的愚蠢与管理不善添上新债。

===Original===
In estimating the value of our material, therefore, it is plain that we must look into the deeds of our estates and ferret out their history. The task is an individual one, as likewise its application. Certainly the original timber as it came from the African forests was good enough. No race of heathen are more noted for honesty and chastity than are the tribes of Africa. For one of their women to violate the laws of purity is a crime punishable with death; and so strictly honest are they, it is said, that they are wont to leave their commodities at the place of exchange and go about their business. The buyer coming up takes what he wishes to purchase and leaves its equivalent in barter or money. A returned missionary tells the story that certain European traders, when at a loss as to the safe keeping of their wares, were told by a native chief, “Oh just lay them down there. *They are perfectly safe, there are no Christians here.*”

===Chinese===
因此，在估算我们材料的价值时，显然必须查验我们产业的契据，把它们的来历查个水落石出。这项工作因人而异，其结论的适用亦然。当然，当年从非洲森林运来的原始木料本身是够好的。没有哪个异教种族比非洲各部族更以诚实与贞洁著称。他们的妇女若有败坏贞法者，便是死罪难逃；而他们之诚实，据说诚实到惯于把货物留在交易之处，各自去忙各的营生。买主走来，取走欲购之物，留下等价的实物或钱款。一位归国的传教士讲过一桩事：几个欧洲商人拿不准货物存放何处才稳妥，一位土著酋长告诉他们：「哦，就搁在那儿吧。*它们保管万无一失，这里没有基督徒。*」

===Original===
Whatever may be said of its beauty, then, the black side of the stream with us is pretty pure, and has no cause to blush for its honesty and integrity. From the nature of the case the infusions of white blood that have come in many instances to the black race in this country are not the best that race afforded. And if anything further is needed to account for racial irregularities﻿—the warping and shrinking, the knotting and cracking of the sturdy old timber, the two hundred and fifty years of training here are quite sufficient to explain all. I have often thought, since coming in closer contact with the Puritan element in America, what a different planing and shaping this timber might have received under their hands!

===Chinese===
那么，且不论其美丑，我们这里这条溪流的黑色一侧是相当纯净的，论诚实与正直，它无须脸红。就事理之常而言，流入这个国家黑色种族的白色血统，在许多实例中都并非那个种族所能拿得出的最好的东西。倘若还须为种族的种种失范另寻解释——结实老木料的翘曲与收缩、结节与开裂——那么此地二百五十年的陶冶已尽足以说明一切。自从来到美国、与清教因素有了较近的接触，我常常想：这批木料若经他们的手，本会得到一番多么不同的刨削与规整！

===Original===
As I compare the Puritan’s sound, substantial, sanctified common sense with the Feudal froth and foam of the South; the Puritan’s liberal, democratic, ethical and at the same time calculating, economical, stick-to-ative and go-ahead-ative spirit﻿—with the free and easy lavishness, the aristocratic notions of caste and class distinctions, the pliable consciences and unbending social bars amid which I was reared;﻿—I have wished that it might have been ordered that as my race had to serve a term of bondage it might have been under the discipline of the successors of Cromwell and Milton, rather than under the training and example of the luxurious cavaliers. There is no doubt that the past two hundred and fifty years of working up the material we now inherit, has depreciated rather than enhanced its value. We find in it the foolish ideas of aristocracy founded on anything else than a moral claim; we find the contempt for manual labor and the horror of horny palms, the love of lavish expenditure and costly display, and﻿—alas, that we must own it﻿—the laxness of morals and easygoing consciences inherited and imitated from the old English gentry of the reigns of Charles and Anne. But to know our faults is one step toward correcting them, and there are, I trust, no flaws in this first element of value, *material*, which may not be planed and scraped and sandpapered out by diligent and strenuous effort. One thing is certain, the flaws that are simply ingrained in the timber are not our responsibility. A man is to be praised primarily not for having inherited fine tools and faultless materials but for making the most of the stuff he has, and doing his best in spite of disadvantages and poor material. The individual is responsible, not for what he has not, but for what he has; and the vital part for us after all depends on the use we make of our material.

===Chinese===
当我把清教徒健全、扎实、圣洁的常识，与南方的封建浮沫浪花相对照；把清教徒那种自由、民主、讲伦理，同时又精打细算、克勤克俭、既「锲而不—舍」又「一往直—前」（stick-to-ative and go-ahead-ative）的精神，与我自幼生长其中的那种散漫豪奢的作风、贵族式的种姓与等级观念、柔顺的良心与僵硬的社会栅栏相对照——我便不禁想：倘若我的种族命定要服一段奴役之刑，但愿它是在克伦威尔（Cromwell）与弥尔顿的后继者的管教之下，而不是在骄奢的骑士们的训育与示范之下。毫无疑问，过去二百五十年对我们如今承继的这批材料的加工，贬损而非增益了它的价值。我们在其中发现：把贵族政治安放在道德资格以外任何东西之上的妄念；对体力劳动的轻蔑、对掌上老茧的畏怖；对挥霍与豪奢的嗜好；还有——唉，不得不承认——从查理与安妮两朝的英国旧乡绅那里承袭并仿效来的疏纵的道德与苟且的良心。然而，知过便是改过的第一步；而且我相信，在价值的第一要素——*材料*——之中，没有任何瑕疵是勤勉刻苦的努力所刨不掉、刮不掉、砂不掉的。有一点是确凿的：单纯嵌在木纹里的瑕疵，责任不在我们。一个人首先值得称许的，不是继承了精良的工具与无瑕的材料，而是把手上那点东西用到极致、在逆境与劣材之中竭尽所能。个人所负的责任，不在于他所没有的，而在于他所有的；而归根结底，我们的要害端在于我们如何使用自己的材料。

===Original===
Many a passable article has by diligent workmanship been made even from inferior material. And this brings us to our second item of value﻿—Labor.

===Chinese===
许多还过得去的物件，靠勤勉的做工，即便从劣等材料也能做出来。这便把我们引到价值的第二项——劳动。

===Original===
This is a most important item. It would seem sometimes that it is labor that creates all value. A gold mine is worth no more than common clay till it is worked. The simple element of labor bestowed on iron, the cheapest and commonest of metals, multiplies its value four hundred thousand times, making it worth sixty-five times its weight in gold, e.g.:

===Chinese===
这是至关重要的一项。有时简直让人觉得：创造一切价值的正是劳动。金矿不经开采，身价并不高于寻常黏土。单是施加于铁——最贱最凡的金属——上的劳动这一项，就使它的身价增长四十万倍，令它按重量计值黄金的六十五倍，例如：

===Original===
A pound of good iron is worth about
4 cts.
A pound of inch screws
$1.00
A pound of steel wire from
$3.00 to $7.00
A pound of sewing needles
$14.00
A pound of fish hooks from
$20.00 to $50.00
A pound of jewel screws for watches
$3,500.00
A pound of hair springs for watches
$16,000.00
While a pound of fine gold in standard coin is worth only about
$248.00

===Chinese===
一磅好铁约值
4 美分
一磅英寸螺丝
1.00 美元
一磅钢丝自
3.00 至 7.00 美元
一磅缝衣针
14.00 美元
一磅鱼钩自
20.00 至 50.00 美元
一磅手表宝石螺钉
3,500.00 美元
一磅手表游丝
16,000.00 美元
而一磅标准铸币的纯金只约值
248.00 美元

===Original===
Now it is the same fundamental material in the hair springs valued at $16,000.00 which was sold in the rough at 4 cts. per pound. It is labor that has thus enhanced its value. Now let us see if there is a parallel rise of value in the material of which men are made.

===Chinese===
如今，值 16,000.00 美元的那些游丝，与粗料时按每磅 4 美分出售的，本是同一种基础材料。是劳动抬升了它的价值。现在让我们看看，构成人的材料是否也有平行的增值。

===Original===
No animal, the scientists tell us, is in infancy so utterly helpless, so completely destitute of the means of independent existence, so entirely worthless in itself as the world estimates values, as is man. The chick just out of the shell can pick up its own food and run away from approaching danger. Touch a snapping turtle just a moment after its birth, and it will bite at you. Cut off its head and it will still bite. Break open the egg of the young and the vivacious little creature will, even in the embryo, try to fight for its rights and maintain its independence. But the human babe can for weeks and months, do nothing but cry and feed and fear. It is a constant drain on the capital of its parents, both physically and mentally. It is to be fed, and worked for, and sheltered and protected. It cannot even defend itself against a draft of wind.

===Chinese===
科学家们告诉我们，没有哪种动物在襁褓期像人这样全然无助、这样彻底缺乏独立生存的手段、这样——按世界的价值估量——本身一无是处。刚出壳的雏鸡便能自己啄食，便能逃避迫近的危险。鳄龟刚生下来，你一碰，它便咬你；斩下它的头，它仍旧咬；破开龟卵，那只活泼的小东西甚至在胚胎状态便要为自己的权利而搏斗、维护自己的独立。而人类的婴孩，一连几个星期、几个月，只会哭、只会吃、只会怕。它是父母资本的一笔持续的耗损，体力脑力两皆如此。它须人喂养，须人为之操劳，须人庇护、须人护卫。它连一阵穿堂风都抵御不住。

===Original===
What is it worth? Unsentimentally and honestly﻿—it is worth just as much as a leak is worth to a ship, or what the mistletoe is worth to the oak. He is a parasite, a thief, a destroyer of values. He thrives at another’s expense, and filches from that other every atom of his own existence. The infatuated mother, it is true, would not sell him, she will tell you, for his weight in gold; but that is sentiment﻿—not business. Besides, there is no danger of her having the chance to make such a bargain. No one will ever tempt her with any such offer. The world knows too well what an outlay of time and money and labor must be made before he is worth even his weight in ashes. His present worth no one would accept even as a gift﻿—and it is only the prospect of future development of worth that could induce anyone, save that mother, to take up the burden. What an expenditure of toil and care, of heart power and brain power, what planning, what working, what feeding, what enriching, what sowing and sinking of values before one can tell whether the harvest is worth the output. Yet, how gladly does the mother pour out her strength and vitality, her energy, her life that the little bankrupt may store up capital for its own use. How anxiously does she hang over the lumpish little organism to catch the first awakening of a soul. And when the chubby little hands begin to swing consciously before the snapping eyes, and the great toe is caught and tugged towards the open mouth, when the little pink fists for the first time linger caressingly on her cheek and breast, and the wide open eyes say distinctly “I know you, I love you,”﻿—how she strains him to her bosom as her whole soul goes out to this newly found intelligence in the impassioned cry of Carlyle: “*Whence﻿—and Oh Heavens, whither!*”

===Chinese===
它值多少呢？撇开感情、照实说——它的身价，恰如船底的漏洞之于一条船，又如槲寄生之于橡树。他是一个寄生者、一个窃贼、一个价值的毁坏者。他靠别人的耗费滋长，从别人身上窃取自己存在的每一原子。不错，那位痴心的母亲会说，给她等身的黄金她也不卖；可那是感情——不是生意。况且也绝无此虞，谁也不会拿这样的价钱来诱她成交。世界太清楚：须得垫上多少光阴、金钱与劳力，他才勉强值上他等重的灰烬。他眼下的价值，白送也没人肯要——除了那位母亲，唯有对未来价值发育的预期，才能诱人担起这副重担。须得付出多少操劳与照料、多少心力与脑力，多少筹划、多少劳作、多少喂养、多少培壅，多少价值的播撒与投入，才等得到判断这场收成是否抵得上支出！然而，母亲又是何等甘愿倾出自己的气力与生机、她的精力、她的生命，好让这小小的破产者为自己攒起资本。她又是何等焦灼地俯向那团混沌未凿的小生命，捕捉一个灵魂最初的觉醒。而当胖乎乎的小手开始在忽闪的眼睛前有意识地挥动，大脚趾被抓住、拽向张开的小嘴，当粉红的小拳头第一次依恋地停驻在她的脸颊与胸膛，当睁大的眼睛分明说出「我认得你，我爱你」——她怎样把他紧紧搂进怀里，整个灵魂扑向这新发现的智慧，发出卡莱尔（Carlyle）那句炽热的呼喊：「*从何处来——啊，苍天，往何处去！*」

===Original===
> “How poor, how rich, how abject, how august,
 

 How complicate, how wonderful is man!”

===Chinese===
> 「人何其贫乏，何其富有，何其卑贱，何其尊荣，
>  何其繁复，又何其奇妙的是人！」

===Original===
It is labor, development, training, careful, patient, painful, diligent toil that must span the gulf between this vegetating life germ (now worth nothing but toil and care and trouble, and living purely at the expense of another)﻿—and that future consummation in which “the elements are so mixed that Nature can stand up and say to all the world, ‘*This is a man*.’ ”

===Chinese===
必须由劳动、发育、训练——审慎的、耐心的、苦痛的、勤勉的操劳——来跨越这道鸿沟：一端是这株只值操劳、照料与麻烦、全然仰人鼻息的植物性的生命胚芽；另一端是那样一种未来的圆满——「各种元素调配得如此匀称，以致造化能站起来对全世界说：『*这是一个人*』」。

===Original===
It is a heavy investment, requires a large outlay of money on long time and large risk, no end of labor, skill, pains. Education is the word that covers it all﻿—the working up of this raw material and fitting it into the world’s work to supply the world’s need﻿—the manufacture of men and women for the markets of the world. But there is no other labor which so creates value. The value of the well developed man has been enhanced far more by the labor bestowed than is the iron in the watch springs. The value of the raw material was far below zero to begin with; but this “quintessence of dust” has become, *through labor*, “the beauty of the world, the paragon of animals﻿—noble in reason and infinite in faculty!”

===Chinese===
这是一笔沉重的投资，须作长年期、高风险的大宗垫款，须投入无穷的劳动、技艺与心血。「教育」一词涵盖其全部——把这件原材料加工出来，安放进世界的工作，以供给世界的需要——为世界的市场制造男人和女人。但没有任何别的劳动像它这样创造价值。一个发育充分的人，其价值因所加劳动而提升的幅度，远远超过表中的游丝之于铁。原材料的起点本远低于零；然而这抔「泥土的精华」，*经由劳动*，竟成了「宇宙的精华、万物的灵长——理性何其高贵，机能何其无穷」！

===Original===
What a piece of work, indeed!

===Chinese===
的的确确，人是多么了不起的一件作品！

===Original===
Education, then, is the safest and richest investment possible to man. It pays the largest dividends and gives the grandest possible product to the world﻿—a man. The demand is always greater than the supply﻿—and the world pays well for what it prizes.

===Chinese===
教育，便是人类所能做的最稳妥、最丰厚的投资。它派息最钜，并给世界以所能产出的最宏伟的产品——一个人。需求永远大于供给——而世界总是肯为它所珍重的东西付高价。

===Original===
Now what sort of workmanship are we putting on our raw material. What are we doing for education? The man-factories among our people make, I think, a fairly good showing. Figures are encouraging things to deal with, and too they represent something tangible in casting up our accounts. There are now 25,530 colored schools in the United States with 1,353,352 pupils; the colored people hold in landed property for churches and schools $25,000,000. 2,500,000 colored children have learned to read and most of these to write also. 22,956 colored men and women are teaching in these schools. There are sixty-six academies and high schools and one hundred and fifty schools for advanced education taught by colored teachers, together with seven colleges administered by colored presidents and faculties. There are now one thousand college bred Negro ministers in the country, 250 lawyers, 749 physicians; while, according to Dr. Rankin, there are 247 colored students preparing themselves in the universities of Europe.

===Chinese===
如今，我们在自己的原材料上做着什么样的工？我们在教育上做了什么？我们民族中间这些「人的制造厂」，我想颇有可观的成绩。数字是鼓舞人心的东西，在盘账时也代表某种确凿之物。合众国现有 25,530 所有色人种学校、1,353,352 名学生；有色人种为教堂与学校持有的地产达 25,000,000 美元。2,500,000 名有色儿童学会了阅读，其中多数还学会了书写。22,956 名有色男女在这些学校里任教。现有六十六所中学与高中、一百五十所由有色教师执教的高等教育学校，另有七所由有色校长与教员管理的学院。本国现有受过大学教育的一千名黑人牧师、250 名律师、749 名医师；而据兰金博士（Dr. Rankin）说，欧洲各大学里尚有 247 名有色学生在深造。

===Original===
The African Methodists alone, representing the unassisted effort of the colored people for self-development, have founded thirty-eight institutes and colleges, with landed property valued at $502,650, and 134 teachers supported entirely by the self denying effort of the colored people themselves.

===Chinese===
单是非洲裔卫理公会（African Methodists）——代表着有色人种不求外助、自我发展的努力——就创办了三十八所学院与学校，地产价值 502,650 美元，并有 134 名教师全然靠有色人种克己的努力来供养。

===Original===
This looks like an attempt, to say the least, to do the best we can with our material. One feels there has not been much shirking here; the workmanship may be crude sometimes, when measured by more finished standards﻿—but they have done what they could; in their poverty and inexperience, through self denial and perseverance, they are struggling upward toward the light.

===Chinese===
这至少像是一番尽力用好手中材料的尝试。人们感到这里没有什么偷工减料；以更精成品的标准衡量，做工有时未免粗陋——但他们已竭尽所能；在贫困与生疏之中，靠着克己与坚持，他们正挣扎着向光明攀升。

===Original===
There is another item to be taken into account in estimating the value of a product, to which we must give just a thought in passing, *i.e.*, the necessary waste of material in the making.

===Chinese===
估算一件产品的价值，还有一项须在此顺带一顾、必须计入的因素，即*制造过程中材料的必要损耗*。

===Original===
The Sultan of Turkey once sent to China to procure a facsimile of some elegant plates he had had, all of which were now broken but one and that, unfortunately, was cracked. He sent this one as a pattern and requested that the set be renewed exactly like the former ones. He was surprised on receiving the plates to note the fabulous sum charged for them﻿—but the Celestial explained that the cost was greatly increased by having *to put in the crack*﻿—so many had been lost in the making.

===Chinese===
土耳其苏丹有一次遣人赴中国，仿制他先前用过的一套雅致盘碟；那套盘子如今尽碎，只剩一只——不幸的是，偏偏带着一道裂纹。他便把这只寄去做样，要求照旧样复制一套。收到盘子时，见索价之昂令他愕然——而那位天朝工匠解释说，造价之所以大增，是因为*必须做出那道裂纹*——制作过程中报废了太多。

===Original===
The anecdote is not my own, but it suggests a thought that may be useful to us and I borrow it for that purpose. They tell us that the waste of material is greater in making colored men and women than in the case of others﻿—that a larger percentage of our children die under twenty-one years of age, especially in large cities, and that a larger number who reach that age and beyond, are to be classed among the world’s invalids and paupers. According to the census of 1880 the average death rate throughout the country was, among the whites 14.74 per 1,000; among colored 17.28 per 1,000: the highest among whites being in New Mexico, 22.04, lowest in Arizona, 7.91 per 1,000. Among colored, the mortality ranges from 35.25 in the District of Columbia where it is the highest, to 1.89 in Arizona, the lowest.

===Chinese===
这则轶事非我所杜撰，但它提示的一个想法或于我们有益，故而借来一用。人们告诉我们：制造有色男女，比制造旁人耗损的材料更多——我们不满二十一岁夭折的孩子比例更高，在大城市尤甚；而活过这个年纪的人当中，归入世上病弱者与赤贫者之列的也更多。据 1880 年人口普查，全国平均死亡率为：白人每千人 14.74；有色人种每千人 17.28：白人最高为新墨西哥的 22.04，最低为亚利桑那的每千人 7.91。有色人种的死亡率，则以哥伦比亚特区为最高，达 35.25，以亚利桑那的 1.89 为最低。

===Original===
For 1889 the relative death-rate of the two races in the District of Columbia was: whites, 15.96 per 1,000; colored, 30.48, about double. In 1888 they stood 18+ to 30+; in 1886 and ’87, about 17 to 31; in ’85 and ’86, 17 to 32. Especially noticeable is the difference in the mortality of children. This is simply alarming. The report for 1889 shows that out of the 5,152 deaths occurring in the District of Columbia during that year, 634 were white infants under one year old, while 834, an excess of 200, within the same limits were colored. Yet the white population of the District outnumbers the colored two to one. The Health Commissioner, in his report for that year, says: “This material difference in mortality may be charged to a great extent to the massing of colored people in alleys and unhealthy parts of the city and to their unsanitary surroundings: while there is no doubt that a very large proportion of these children die in consequence of being fed improper and unhealthy food, especially cheap and badly prepared condensed milk, and cow’s milk which has been allowed to stand to the point of acidity after having been kept in vessels badly or unskillfully cleaned.” And he adds, “if the general statistics of infant mortality seem astounding to the public, the cause can most frequently be found in the reprehensible custom of committing little impoverished waifs to hired nurses and foul feeding bottles rather than allow them the food that nature has provided.”

===Chinese===
1889 年，哥伦比亚特区两个人种的相对死亡率为：白人每千人 15.96；有色人种 30.48，约莫一倍。1888 年为 18 对 30 上下；1886、1887 两年约为 17 对 31；1885、1886 年为 17 对 32。尤其触目的是儿童死亡率的悬殊，简直令人惊心。1889 年的报告显示：该年哥伦比亚特区死亡 5,152 人，其中一岁以下白人婴孩 634 名，而同一年龄段的有色婴孩为 834 名，多出 200 名——而特区的白人人口是有色的两倍。卫生专员在那年的报告中说：「死亡率上的这种重大差异，很大程度上须归咎于有色人大批挤居在巷陌与城中不卫生的地段，归咎于他们周围的不洁环境；同时毫无疑问，这些孩子中有很大一部分死于喂养不当、有碍健康的食物，尤其是廉价而调制恶劣的炼乳，以及盛放在清洗不善或清洗拙劣的器皿中、任其放到发酸的牛奶。」他又补充道：「如果婴儿死亡的总体统计令公众骇异，其原因最常见的便是那种应受谴责的积习：宁可把瘦弱的弃婴交给雇佣乳母和污秽的奶瓶，也不肯给他们大自然所预备的食物。」

===Original===
Now all this unquestionably represents a most wanton and flagrant *waste* of valuable material. By sapping out the possibilities of a healthy and vigorous existence it is deliberately and flagitiously breeding and multiplying paupers, criminals, idiots, drunkards, imbeciles and lunatics to infest and tax the commonwealth. The number spoiled in the making necessarily adds to the cost of those who survive. It is like the Sultan’s cracked dinner-plates. It is no use to go into hysterics and explode in Ciceronian phillippics against life insurance companies for refusing to insure or charging a higher premium for colored policies. With them it is simply a question of dollars and cents. What are you worth? What are your chances, and what does it cost to take your risks in the aggregate? If thirty-five colored persons out of every thousand are, from any cause whatever, lost in the making, the remaining nine hundred and sixty-five will have to share the loss among them. This is an unavoidable law. No man can dissociate himself from his kind. The colored gentleman who keeps his horses, fares sumptuously, and lives in luxury is made to feel the death gasps of every squalid denizen of the alley and poorhouse. It is God’s own precaution to temper our self-seeking by binding our sympathies and interests indissolubly with the helpless and the wretched.

===Chinese===
凡此种种，无可置疑地代表着对贵重材料最暴殄天物的*挥霍*。它榨干健康而旺盛的生存之可能，实际是在蓄意地、惨无人道地繁衍并增多赤贫者、罪犯、白痴、醉汉、低能者与疯人，来侵扰并加重共和国的负担。制造中报废的数目，必然摊入幸存者的成本。这正是苏丹那批带裂纹的餐盘。对着人寿保险公司拒保有色的保单、或对之索取更高保费而歇斯底里、大发西塞罗「反腓力辞」式的痛斥，是无济于事的。在它们那里，这不过是几元几角的问题。你值多少？你的机会几何？承你的风险总共要付出几许？倘若每一千个有色人中有三十五个——无论因何缘故——在制造中报废，余下的九百六十五人便须分摊这笔亏损。这是一条无可逃遁的铁律。没有人能与其同类相割离。那位养着骏马、食必粱肉、起居豪奢的有色绅士，每一回巷陌与贫民院里凄惨的魂灵咽气，他都要跟着感到抽搐。这是上帝自己的预防之策：把我们的同情与利益，同那些无助者与不幸者牢不可破地拴在一处，以节制我们的自利。

===Original===
What our men of means need to do, then, is to devote their money, their enlightened interest, their careful attention to the improvement of sanitation among the poor. Let some of those who can command real estate in healthful localities build sweet and clean and wholesome tenements *on streets* and rent them at reasonable rates to the worthy poor who are at present forced into association with the vileness and foulness of alleys and filthy courts by the unfeeling discrimination of white dealers. Let some colored capitalists buy up a few of those immense estates in the South, divide them into single farms with neat, cheery, well-ventilated, healthsome cottages to be rented to the colored tenants who are toiling all these weary years in the one-room log hut, like their own cheerless mules﻿—just to fodder themselves.

===Chinese===
那么，我们的有产者所当做的，便是把他们的金钱、他们开明的关切、他们细致的注意，投向贫苦阶层卫生状况的改善。但愿若干能在卫生地段支配不动产的人，*临街*建起洁净甘美、有益健康的住宅，以合理的租金租给那些值得敬重的穷人——他们如今正因白人房东冷酷无情的歧视，被迫与巷陌污秽院落里的龌龊为伍。但愿若干有色资本家买下南方几处大庄园中的几座，分割成一户户农庄，配上整洁、开朗、通风、卫生的村舍，租给那些经年累月蜗居在一间原木小屋里、像他们自己那头没有欢颜的骡子一样——只求喂饱自己——的有色佃农。

===Original===
In cities, low priced houses on streets are almost uniformly kept for the white poor. I know of numerous houses in Washington the rent of which is no dearer than colored people are paying in alleys﻿—but the advertisement says, “not rented to colored people.” If the presence of a colored tenant in a neighborhood causes property to depreciate, it may be a question of sentiment﻿—it must be a question of business. The former it is superfluous to inveigh against or even to take cognizance of. It is possibly subject to enlightenment, and probably a sickness not unto death. But the practical reason underlying it is directly our concern and should command our energetic consideration. It is largely a question of what are we worth﻿—and as such, subject to our immediate responsibility and amendment. If improvement is possible, if it is in our power to render ourselves *valuable* to a community or neighborhood, it should be the work of the earnest and able men and women among us, the moral physicians and reformers, to devise and apply a remedy. Sure it is that the burden rests on all till the deliverance comes. The richest and most highly favored cannot afford to be indifferent or to rest quietly complacent.

===Chinese===
在城市里，临街的低价房屋几乎一律留给白人贫民。我知道华盛顿有不少房屋，租金并不比有色人眼下在巷陌里付出的更贵——可招租广告上写着：「不租给有色人。」倘若有色租客的出现使一处地段的地价跌落，这或许是感情问题——那就必是生意问题。前者，痛诋它是多此一举，甚至理会它也是多余；它或许尚可开化，多半也还是一场不至于死的病。但潜藏其下的那个实际的缘由，却直接干系着我们，理当引起我们旺盛的思虑。这在很大程度上正是「我们值多少」的问题——既然如此，便直接系于我们的责任与改进。倘若改进是可能的，倘若我们有能力使自己对于一个社区、一个地段*有价值*，那么设法并施用一项疗方，就应当是我们中间诚挚而有才干的男女、那些道德的医师与改革者的事业。可以肯定，在解脱到来之前，这副担子压在所有人身上；最富足、最蒙眷顾的人，也担待不起漠然自安。

===Original===
In rural districts, the relative mortality of colored people is not so excessive, still the poverty and destitution, the apparent dearth of accumulation notwithstanding ceaseless drudging toil is something phenomenal in labor statistics. I confess I have felt little enthusiasm for the labor riots which seem epidemic at the North. Carnegie’s men at Homestead, for instance, were among the best paid workmen in the country, receiving many of them $240 per month, living luxuriously, dictating their own terms as to who should work with them, how many hours, and what special labor they will perform. Their employers are forced to hire so many and such men﻿—for these laboring despots insist on an exact division of labor, no one must be called on to work outside his specialty. Then they must share profits, but be excused from all concern in losses﻿—a patent adjustable sliding scale for wages which slides up beautifully, but never down! If the Northern laboring man has not become a tyrant, I would like to know what tyranny is.

===Chinese===
在乡间地区，有色人的相对死亡率并不这般骇人，但那贫困与匮乏、那终岁胼手胝足而积攒依然荡然的景象，在劳动统计上是骇人听闻的。我承认，我对北方那些仿佛瘟疫般蔓延的罢工风潮提不起多少热忱。譬如卡内基（Carnegie）霍姆斯特德（Homestead）的工人，属于全国工薪最优厚者之列，许多人月入 240 美元，生活豪奢，对谁同他们一起做工、做几个钟头、做哪种活，都要自定条件。雇主被迫雇下这么多、这样的工人——因为这些劳动的暴君坚持精确的分工，任何人不得被召去做他本行之外的活。他们还须分享利润，却免于分担亏损——工资上的一具专利可调滑动秤，往上调时何其美妙，往下则寸步不滑！如果北方的劳工还没有变成暴君，那我倒要请教：暴君究竟是什么。

===Original===
But I wonder how many know that there are throughout the Southland able bodied, hard working men, toiling year in and year out, from sunrise to dusk, for fifty cents per day, out of which they must feed and shelter and clothe themselves and their families! That they often have to take their wage in tickets convertible into meat, meal and molasses at the village grocery, owned by the same ubiquitous employer! That there are tenants holding leases on farms who toil sixteen hours to the day and work every chick and child in their possession, not sparing even the drudging wife﻿—to find at the end of the harvesting season and the squaring up of accounts that their accumulations have been like gathering water in a sieve.

===Chinese===
可是有几个人知道：在南方全境，有多少壮健勤勉的汉子，年复一年、日出而作、日入而息，一天的工钱只有五十美分，还须以此养活、安顿、衣被他自己和他的全家！他们常常只能领到票券作工资，只能在那家同样无处不在的雇主所开的乡村杂货铺里，兑成咸肉、玉米面和糖蜜！有多少握着租约的佃农，一天做满十六个钟头，把家里的大小孩儿统统赶去上工，连操劳的妻子也不放过——到收割完毕、账目轧平，才发现一年的积攒犹如以筛子汲水！

===Original===
Do you ask the cause of their persistent poverty? It is not found in the explanation often vouchsafed by the white landlord﻿—that the Negro is indolent, improvident and vicious. Taking them man for man and dollar for dollar, I think you will find the Negro, in ninety-nine cases out of a hundred, not a whit behind the Anglo-Saxon of equal chances. It is a fact which every candid man who rides through the rural districts in the South will admit, that in progressive aspirations and industry the Negro is ahead of the white man of his chances. Indeed it would not be hard to show that the white man *of his chances* does not exist. The “Crackers” and “poor-whites” were never slaves, were never oppressed or discriminated against. Their time, their earnings, their activities have always been at their own disposal; and pauperism in their case can be attributed to nothing but stagnation﻿—moral, mental, and physical immobility: while in the case of the Negro, poverty can at least be partially accounted for by the hard conditions of life and labor﻿—the past oppression and continued repression which form the vital air in which the Negro lives and moves and has his being.

===Chinese===
你若问他们世代贫困的缘由？它不在白人地主惯常给出的那种解释里——说黑人懒惰、没有远虑、品行不端。一个对一个、一元对一元地掂量，我想你会发现，一百例中有九十九例，黑人不比机会均等的盎格鲁-撒克逊人差一丝一毫。凡骑马走过南方乡野的坦白人都得承认这样一个事实：就向上的志气与勤奋而论，黑人超过与他同等机会的白人。的确，不难证明：*与他同等机会的*白人根本就不存在。「克拉克佬」（Crackers）与「穷白人」（poor-whites）从未为奴，从未受压迫或歧视。他们的光阴、他们的进项、他们的营生，从来都由自己支配；他们的赤贫只能归因于停滞——道德、心智与体魄的一潭死水；而黑人的贫困，至少有一部分可以归因于生活与劳作的严酷条件——那往日的压迫与延续至今的压制，正是黑人生活、动作、存留于其中的生命之气。

===Original===
One often hears in the North an earnest plea from some lecturer for “our working girls” (of course this means white working girls). And recently I listened to one who went into pious agonies at the thought of the future mothers of Americans having to stand all day at shop counters; and then advertised with applause a philanthropic firm who were giving their girls a trip to Europe for rest and recreation! I am always glad to hear of the establishment of reading rooms and social entertainments to brighten the lot of any women who are toiling for bread﻿—whether they are white women or black women. But how many have ever given a thought to the pinched and downtrodden colored women bending over washtubs and ironing boards﻿—with children to feed and house rent to pay, wood to buy, soap and starch to furnish﻿—lugging home weekly great baskets of clothes for families who pay them for a month’s laundrying barely enough to purchase a substantial pair of shoes!

===Chinese===
