在北方，人们常听到某位讲演员为「我们的做工姑娘」热切陈词（这自然只指白人做工姑娘）。前不久我便听过一位：一想到未来的美国母亲们竟须整日立在柜台之后，她便痛心疾首；随后又博得一片掌声地夸赞起某家慈善商行——他们让自己的姑娘们去欧洲休养游乐！凡为照亮任何靠双手挣饭吃的妇女——无论白人妇女还是黑人妇女——的命运而设的阅览室与联谊消遣，我一概乐闻其成。可是有几个人曾想到过那些憔悴困顿的有色妇女——俯身在洗衣盆与烫衣板前——要喂孩子，要付房租，要买柴火，要置办肥皂与浆粉——每周拖着一大筐一大筐的衣裳回家，而那些人家付给她整月浆洗的工钱，勉强够买一双结实的鞋！

===Original===
Will you call it narrowness and selfishness, then, that I find it impossible to catch the fire of sympathy and enthusiasm for most of these labor movements at the North?

===Chinese===
那么，当我发现自己无论如何也燃不起同情与热忱、去追随北方这些劳工运动的大多数时，你会把这唤作偏狭与自私么？

===Original===
I hear these foreigners, who would boycott an employer if he hired a colored workman, complain of wrong and oppression, of low wages and long hours, clamoring for eight-hour systems and insisting on their right to have sixteen of the twenty-four hours for rest and self-culture, for recreation and social intercourse with families and friends﻿—ah, come with me, I feel like saying, I can show you workingmen’s wrong and workingmen’s toil which, could it speak, would send up a wail that might be heard from the Potomac to the Rio Grande; and *should it unite and act*, would shake this country from Carolina to California.

===Chinese===
我听见这些外国人——他们的规矩是：雇主若雇一个有色工人，便群起抵制——抱怨冤屈与压迫、低薪与长时，鼓噪八小时工作制，坚称二十四小时中应有十六小时归他们休息与自修、消遣娱乐、与家人朋友往来——啊，随我来，我真想对他们说，我可以指给你们看什么叫做工人的冤屈、做工人的苦楚；这冤苦倘能开口，一声哀号便可以从波托马克河传到格兰德河；*倘若它联合起来、行动起来*，足以把这个国家从卡罗来纳震动到加利福尼亚。

===Original===
But no man careth for their souls. The labor interests of the colored man in this country are as yet dumb and limp. The unorganized mass has found neither tongue nor nerve. In the free and liberal North, thanks to the amalgamated associations and labor unions of immigrant laborers, who cannot even speak English﻿—the colored man is relegated to the occupations of waiter and barber, unless he has a taste for school teaching or politics. A body of men who still need an interpreter to communicate with their employer, will threaten to cut the nerve and paralyze the progress of an industry that gives work to an American-born citizen, or one which takes measures to instruct any apprentice not supported by the labor monopoly. A skilled mechanic, a friend of mine, secured a job in one of our cities and was seen by union men at work on his house. He was immediately ordered in murderous English to take down his scaffolding and leave the town. Refusing to do so, before night he was attacked by a force that overwhelmed him and he was obliged to leave. Such crushing opposition is not alone against colored persons. These amalgamated and other unions hold and are determined to continue holding an impenetrable monopoly on the labor market, assuming supreme censorship as regards the knowledge and practice of their trade.

===Chinese===
可是没有人在乎他们的灵魂。这个国家有色人的劳动利益至今喑哑而瘫软。这一盘散沙既未找到舌头，也未找到筋骨。在自由宽宏的北方，托那些移民劳工的联合组织（amalgamated associations）与工会之福——他们连英语都还说不利索——有色人被打发去当侍者、理发匠，除非他偏巧爱好教书或政治。一群还得靠翻译才能与雇主通话的人，却扬言要斩断命脉、瘫痪一门给美国本土公民提供工作的产业，或阻挠对任何不经这一劳动垄断集团供养的学徒传授技艺。我的一位朋友，一名熟练技工，在本国某城找到一份活计，被工会中人在他干活的那所房子里撞见。他们立刻用杀气腾腾的英语勒令他拆下脚手架、滚出城去。他拒不从命，入夜之前便遭到一股压倒性人力的袭击，只得离开。这样碾轧式的敌意并非只施于有色人。这些组织及其他工会把持着、并决意继续把持劳动市场上针插不进、水泼不进的垄断，对行业知识与行业操作自居最高审查之权。

===Original===
In the South, on the other hand, where the colored man virtually holds the labor market, he is too uncertain and unorganized to demand anything like a fair share of the products of his toil. And yet the man who thinks, must see that our labor interests lie at the foundation of our material prosperity. The growth of the colored man in this country must for a long time yet be estimated on his value and productiveness as a laborer. In adding up the account the aggregate of the great toiling mass largely overbalances the few who have acquired means and leisure. The nation judges us as workingmen, and poor indeed is that man or race of men who are compelled to toil all the weary years ministering to no higher want than that of bread. To feed is not the chief function of this material that has fallen to our care to be developed and perfected. It is an enormous waste of values to harness the whole man in the narrow furrow, plowing for bread. There are other hungerings in man besides the eternal all-subduing hungering of his despotic stomach. There is the hunger of the eye for beauty, the hunger of the ear for concords, the hungering of the mind for development and growth, of the soul for communion and love, for a higher, richer, fuller living﻿—a more abundant life! And every man owes it to himself to *let nothing in him starve* for lack of the proper food. “What is man,” says Shakespeare, “if his chief good and market of his time be but to sleep and feed!” Yet such slavery as that is the settled lot of four-fifths the laboring men of the Southland. This, I contend, is an enormous, a profligate waste of the richest possibilities and the divinest aptitudes. And we owe it to humanity, we owe it preeminently to those of our own household, to enlarge and enrich, so far as in us lies, the opportunity and grasp of every soul we can emancipate. Surely there is no greater boon we can bestow on our fellow-man in this life, none that could more truly command his deepest gratitude and love, than to disclose to his soul its possibilities and mend its opportunities﻿—to place its rootlets in the generous loam, turn its leaves towards the gracious dews and warm sunlight of heaven and let it grow, let it mature in foliage, flower and fruit for **God and the race**! Philanthropy will devise means﻿—an object is not far to seek.

===Chinese===
而在南方，有色人实际上执掌着劳动市场，却又散漫无组织，无力为自己讨到劳动产品的一个像样的份额。然而但凡动脑的人必定看到：我们的劳动利益，正是我们物质繁荣的根基。这个国家有色人的成长，在今后很长一个时期内，仍须以其作为劳动者的价值与产出来估量。盘账之时，劳苦大众的总数远压过那少数攒下资财与闲暇的人。这个民族是以劳动者的身份评判我们的；而不得不终岁劳碌、所伺候的无非果腹一事者，无论其人其族，都穷得可怜。喂养并不是交到我们手中待发育、待完善的这具材料的首要职能。把整个人套进窄窄的犁沟里犁饭吃，是对价值的一大挥霍。在那专横的肠胃之外，人身上还有别的饥饿：眼睛之于美，耳朵之于和声，心智之于发育成长，灵魂之于相通与相爱，之于一种更高、更富、更圆满的生活——一种更丰盛的生命！每个人都对自己负有一份义务：*不让自己身上的任何一部分*因缺少应得的食物而挨饿。莎士比亚说：「人若毕生之大利无非吃与睡，那还算什么人！」然而这样的奴役，正是南方五分之四劳动者的定命。我主张：这是对最丰厚的可能性、最神圣的天资的一种骇人的、挥霍无度的浪费。我们欠人类一笔债，尤其是欠我们自己家里的人一笔债：但凡力所能及，便去扩充并丰饶每一个我们能够解放的灵魂的机会与襟抱。的确，我们在此生能赐给同胞的恩惠，再没有比这更大、也再没有比这更能真正赢得他最深的感戴与热爱的了：向他的灵魂揭示它的可能，修补它的机会——把它的须根安放进宽厚的沃土，让它的叶片朝向天恩的甘露与暖阳，任它生长，任它在枝叶、花朵与果实中长成，为**上帝，为这个种族**！慈善自会想出办法——目标并不难寻。

===Original===
Closely akin to the value that may be said to have been wasted through the inclemency and barrenness of circumstance, through the sickness, sin and death that wait on poverty and squalor, a large item of worth has undoubtedly been destroyed by mistaken and unscientific manufacture﻿—foolhardy educators rashly attempting to put in some theoretically desirable *crack*﻿—the classical crack, or the professional crack, or the artistic-aesthetic-accomplishments crack﻿—into material better fitted for household pottery and common everyday stone and iron ware. I want nothing I may say to be construed into an attack on classical training or on art development and culture. I believe in allowing every longing of the human soul to attain its utmost reach and grasp. But the effort must be a fizzle which seeks to hammer souls into preconstructed molds and grooves which they have never longed for and cannot be made to take comfort in. The power of appreciation is the measure of an individual’s aptitudes; and if a boy hates Greek and Latin and spends all his time whittling out steamboats, it is rather foolish to try to force him into the classics. There may be a locomotive in him, but there is certainly no foreshadowing evidence of either the teacher or preacher. It is a waste of forces to strain his incompetence, and smother his proficiencies. If his hand is far more cunning and clever than his brain, see what he can best do, and give him a chance according to his fitness; try him at a trade.

===Chinese===
与那因境遇之严酷荒瘠、因随贫困腌臜而至的疾病、罪恶与死亡而虚掷的价值紧相毗邻的，是另一大宗价值的毁弃——它出自错误而不科学的制造：莽撞的教育者们，硬要在一些更宜制作家常陶器、寻常铁石器皿的材料上，敲进某条理论上看去可取的*裂纹*——或古典的裂纹，或专业的裂纹，或艺术—审美—才艺的裂纹。但愿我这里说的一切不被曲解为对古典训练或艺术陶冶的攻击。我相信应容让人灵魂中的每一种向往各极其致。但是，想把灵魂硬锤进它们从未向往过、也永不能安之若素的预制模槽，这样的努力注定是一场空。鉴赏力是个人才具的量尺；一个男孩若憎恶希腊文拉丁文、把全部光阴用来削刻轮船模型，那么强把他往古典里推便属愚蠢。他肚子里也许藏着一台火车头，却断乎没有半点当教师或当传道人的朕兆。勉强其不能而窒息其所能，是力量的浪费。倘若他的手远比他的脑灵巧，那就看看他最能做什么，依其禀赋给他一个机会：让他去学一门手艺。

===Original===
Industrial training has been hitherto neglected or despised among us, due, I think, as I have said elsewhere, to two causes: first, a mistaken estimate of labor arising from its association with slavery and from its having been despised by the only class in the South thought worthy of imitation; and secondly, the fact that the Negro’s ability to work had never been called in question, while his ability to learn Latin and construe Greek syntax needed to be proved to sneering critics. “Scale the heights!” was the cry. “Go to college, study Latin, preach, teach, orate, wear spectacles and a beaver!”

===Chinese===
实业训练在我们中间一向遭忽视或轻鄙，其原因，如我在别处说过的，我以为有二：其一，是对劳动的错误估价——它与奴役相联，又被南方唯一被认为值得效仿的阶层所轻鄙；其二，则是因为黑人的干活能力从未受人置疑，而他的读通拉丁文、解析希腊文法的能力，却亟须向冷嘲热讽的批评者证明。「攀登高峰！」当年是一片呼声。「上大学，读拉丁文，去布道，去教书，去演说，戴上眼镜，扣一顶海狸皮高帽！」

===Original===
Stung by such imputations as that of Calhoun that if a Negro could prove his ability to master the Greek subjunctive he might vindicate his title to manhood, the newly liberated race first shot forward along this line with an energy and success which astonished its most sanguine friends.

===Chinese===
卡尔霍恩（Calhoun）曾放言：黑人若能证明自己攻得下希腊语虚拟语气，方配为自己的人的资格辩护。新近解放的种族受此讥刺所激，首先便循着这条路猛进，那股劲头与成就，令最乐观的朋友也为之咋舌。

===Original===
This may not have been most wise. It certainly was quite natural; and the result is we find ourselves in almost as ludicrous a plight as the African in the story, who, after a sermon from his missionary pleading for the habiliments of civilization, complacently donned a Gladstone hat leaving the rest of his body in its primitive simplicity of attire. Like him we began at the wrong end. Wealth must pave the way for learning. Intellect, whether of races or individuals, cannot soar to the consummation of those sublime products which immortalize genius, while the general mind is assaulted and burdened with “what shall we eat, what shall we drink, and wherewithal shall we be clothed.” Work must first create wealth, and wealth leisure, before the untrammeled intellect of the Negro, or any other race, can truly vindicate its capabilities. Something has been done intellectually we all know. That one black man has written a Greek grammar is enough to answer Calhoun’s sneer; but it is leisure, the natural outgrowth of work and wealth, which must furnish room, opportunity, possibility for the highest endeavor and most brilliant achievement. Labor must be the solid foundation stone﻿—the sine qua non of our material value; and the only effective preparation for success in this, as it seems to me, lies in the establishment of industrial and technical schools for teaching our colored youth trades. This necessity is obvious for several reasons. First, a colored child, in most cases, can secure a trade in no other way. We had master mechanics while the Negro was a chattel, and the ingenuity of brain and hand served to enrich the coffers of his owner. But today skilled labor is steadily drifting into the hands of white workmen﻿—mostly foreigners. Here it is cornered. The white engineer holds a tight monopoly both of the labor market and of the science of his craft. Nothing would induce him to take a colored apprentice or even to work beside a colored workman. Unless then trades are to fall among the lost arts for us as a people, they must be engrafted on those benevolent institutions for Negro training established throughout the land. The youth must be taught to use his trigonometry in surveying his own and his neighbor’s farm; to employ his geology and chemistry in finding out the nature of the soil, the constituents drafted from it by each year’s crop and the best way to meet the demand by the use of suitable renewers; to apply his mechanics and physics to the construction and handling of machinery﻿—to the intelligent management of iron works and water works and steam works and electric works. One mind in a family or in a town may show a penchant for art, for literature, for the learned professions, or more bookish lore. You will know it when it is there. No need to probe for it. It is a light that cannot be hid under a bushel﻿—and I would try to enable that mind to go the full length of its desires. Let it follow its bent and develop its talent as far as possible: and the whole community might well be glad to contribute its labor and money for the sustenance and cultivation of this brain. Just as earth gives its raw material, its carbons, hydrogen, and oxygen, for the tree which is to elaborate them into foliage, flower and fruit, so the baser elements, bread and money furnished the true brain worker come back to us with compound interest in the rich thought, the invention, the poem, the painting, the statue. Only let us recognize our assignment and not squander our portion in over fond experiments. James Russell Lowell says, “As we cannot make a silk purse out of a sow’s ear, no more can we perform the opposite experiment without having a fine lot of spoiled silk on our hands.”

===Chinese===
这未必是最明智的一步，却无疑是最自然的一步；其结果便是，我们发觉自己陷入了几乎同故事里那个非洲人一样滑稽的境地：传教士布过一场道，恳求他穿上文明的衣装，他便心满意足地戴上一顶格莱斯顿帽（Gladstone hat），让身体其余部分保持着服饰上太古的纯朴。同他一样，我们从头错了起。财富必须为学问开路。无论种族或个人，其心智都休想腾空飞抵那些使天才不朽的极致造诣，只要这颗心智还被「吃什么、喝什么、穿什么」围攻重压。必须先由劳动创造财富，由财富生闲暇，黑人或任何别个种族的心智，才能真正证明自己的能力。在心智上已有建树，这我们大家都知道。单是有一个黑人写成过一部希腊文文法，便足以回敬卡尔霍恩的冷笑；但是，唯有闲暇——工作与财富天然的产儿——才能为最高的奋勉、最辉煌的成就提供余地、机会与可能。劳动必须是我们物质价值坚实的奠基石——我们价值不可或缺的条件（sine qua non）；而在我看来，制胜于此的唯一有效的准备，便是建立实业与技术学校，教我们的有色青年学得手艺。此举之必要，理由昭然。其一，一个有色孩子，多半唯有由此一途方能学到一门手艺。当黑人还是一件动产时，我们自有熟练的工匠，而脑与手的巧思所增益的，是往他主人的钱柜里填金。可如今，熟练劳动正不断流入白人工人之手——多为外邦人。在这里它被把持独占。白人工程师对劳动市场与他的行业技艺，都握着死紧的垄断。任凭什么也不能诱他收一个有色学徒，甚至不肯与有色工人并肩做工。那么，除非手艺注定要沦为我们这个民族的绝艺，就必须把它们嫁接到遍布各地的那些黑人训育慈善机构上去。必须教会青年用他的三角学去测量自己与邻人的田庄；用他的地质学与化学去查明土壤的性质、查明每季作物从土中汲取的成分、以及用适宜的肥料补足需求的上策；把他的机械学与物理学应用于机器的构造与操纵——应用于铁工厂、自来水厂、蒸汽厂与电力厂的聪明管理。一个家庭、一个镇子，或许有一颗头脑显露出对艺术、文学、学术性专业或更书本化的学问的癖好。真有，你自然认得出，无须去探问。那是藏不住斗底的光——我也必尽力让那颗头脑穷其欲之所至。由它循性之所近，尽可能展其才：而整个社区正该乐于献出劳力与金钱，来供养、培灌这颗头脑。恰如大地献出它的原材料——碳、氢、氧——交给那株将把它们化为枝叶花果的树，面包与金钱这些较粗的元素献给真正的脑力劳动者之后，也会以丰厚的复利回到我们手里：化为丰富的思想、发明、诗篇、绘画与雕像。只是须认清我们的本分，莫在过分痴爱的实验里挥霍了自己的一份。詹姆斯·拉塞尔·洛威尔（James Russell Lowell）说：「我们既不能把猪耳朵做成丝绸钱囊，也就做不成反向的实验——除非情愿到头来手里落下一大批糟蹋了的丝绸。」

===Original===
With most of us, however, the material, such as it is, has been already delivered. The working of it up is also well under way. The gold, the silver, the wood, the hay, the stubble, whatever there was at hand has all gone in. Now can the world use it? Is there a demand for it, does it perform the functions for which it was made, and is its usefulness greater than the cost of its production? Does it pay expenses and have anything over.

===Chinese===
然而就我们多数人而言，材料既已如此交到我们手里，把它加工出来的工序也已全面铺开。金子、银子、木头、干草、秸秆，手边有什么都下了料。如今，世界用得上它么？它有需求么？它行其当行之用么？它的用处大于生产它的成本么？它付得清开销而尚有盈余么？

===Original===
The world in putting these crucial questions to men and women, or to races and nations, classifies them under two heads﻿—as consumers or producers. The man who consumes as much as he produces is simply nil. It is no matter to the world economically speaking whether he is in it or out of it. He is merely one more to count in taking the census. The man who consumes more than he produces is a destroyer of the world’s wealth and should be estimated precisely as the housekeeper estimates moths and mice. These are the world’s parasites, the shirks, the lazy lubbers who hang around rum shops and enter into mutual relationships with lamp posts to bear each the other’s burdens, moralizing all the while (wondrous moralists and orators they often are!) and insisting that the world owes them a living! To be sure the world owes them nothing of the kind. The world would consider it a happy riddance from bad rubbish if they would pay up their debt and move over to Mars. Every day they live their unproductive bodies sink and destroy a regular portion of the world’s values. At the very lowest estimate, a boy who has reached the age of twenty, has already burned up between three and four thousand dollars of the world’s possessions. This is on the very closest and most economical count; I charge him nothing for fuel or lights, allowing him to have warmed by fires that would have burned for others and estimating the cost simply of what he has eaten and worn, *i.e.* the amount which he has actually sunk of the world’s wealth. I put his board at the moderate sum of ten dollars per month, and charge him the phenomenally small amount of thirty dollars a year for clothing and incidentals. This in twenty years gives him a debt of three thousand dollars, which no honest man should be willing to leave the world without settling. The world does not owe them a living then﻿—the world only waits for them to square up and change their residence. It is only they who produce more than they consume, that the world owes, or even acknowledges as having any practical value.

===Chinese===
世界把这些问题锋利地摆到男女、或种族与民族面前时，便把它们分作两类——消费者，或生产者。消费与生产相抵的人，于世界不过一个零。在经济上说来，他在不在这个世界上都无关痛痒，不过是人口普查时多数一个数。消费多于生产的人，是世界财富的毁坏者，其估量当与主妇估量蠹虫老鼠无异。他们是世界的寄生者、偷懒的懒汉，整天泡在酒馆门口，与路灯杆结成相互扶持之交、彼此分担重负，还一面发着道德议论（他们往往是了不起的道德家与演说家呢！），一口咬定世界欠他们一碗饭吃！世界当然不欠他们这碗饭。他们若肯把欠账结清、搬家去火星，世界只会额手称庆，谢天谢地送走这堆废物。他们活着的每一天，那不事生产的躯体都在坐耗、销毁世界价值中固定的一份。往最少里算，一个满二十岁的男孩，已经烧掉了世界上三千至四千美元的家当。这还是按顶紧、顶节俭的账算的：燃料灯火我一概不取，且算他烤的是本会为别人燃烧的火，只计他吃掉穿掉的那部分，即他实际从世界财富里沉没的数目。我按每月十美元这一不高不低的数目计他的食宿，衣着杂用一年只算三十美元这个出奇的小数。二十年下来，他便背上了三千美元的债——任何一个诚实的人，都不该不肯清账就离开这个世界。可见世界不欠他们一碗饭——世界只等着他们结清欠账、迁居他方。唯有那些生产多于消费的人，才是世界有所亏欠、甚至肯承认有任何实际价值的人。

===Original===
Now to which class do we belong? The question must in the first place be an individual one for every man of whatever race: Am I giving to the world an equivalent of what it has given and is giving me? Have I a margin on the outside of consumption for surplus production? We owe it to the world to give out at least as much as we have taken in, but if we aim to be accounted a positive value we must leave it a little richer than we found it. The boy who dies at twenty leaving three thousand dollars in bank to help another, has just paid expenses. If he lives longer it increases his debit and should be balanced by a corresponding increase on the credit side. The life that serves to develop another, the mother who toils to educate her boy, the father who invests his stored-up capital in education, giving to the world the energies and usefulness of his children trained into a well disciplined manhood and womanhood has paid his debt in the very richest coin﻿—a coin which is always legal tender, a priceless gift, the most precious payment we can make for what we have received. And we may be sure, if we can give no more than a symmetric life, an inspiring thought, a spark caught from a noble endeavor, its value will not be lost.

===Chinese===
那么，我们属于哪一类？这问题首先必须对每一个人——无论何种族——各别地提出：我给世界的，是否抵得上世界已给、正给我的？我在消费之外，可有富余的生产以供输出？我们欠世界一笔债：至少须给出与所取等量的东西；但若想被算作一个正的价值，我们就必须让世界因我们而比所见的更富一点。二十岁死去、在银行留下三千美元助人的男孩，不过刚刚付清开销。活得愈久，借方便愈增，贷方便应有相应的增加。那用于成全另一个生命的生命，那位辛劳供儿子读书的母亲，那位把积攒的资本投于教育的父亲——把儿女调教成训练有素的男子品格与女子品格，把他们的精力与效用献给世界——乃是按最贵重的钱币清偿了他的债：这种钱币永远是法定通货，是无价的赠礼，是我们所能为所受的一切作出的最珍贵的偿付。而且我们可以确信：哪怕我们给得出的不过是一种匀称的生命、一星启发人的思想、一粒从高尚的奋勉中掠来的火星，它的价值也不会落空。

===Original===
Previous to 1793 America was able to produce unlimited quantities of cotton, but unable to free the fibre from the seeds. Eli Whitney came to the rescue of the strangled industry and perfected a machine which did the work needed. The deliverance which he wrought was complete. The following year America’s exports of cotton to England were increased from not one pound in previous years to 1,600,000 pounds. He gave dollars.

===Chinese===
1793 年以前，美国能生产无限量的棉花，却无法把棉纤维从棉籽上剥离。伊莱·惠特尼（Eli Whitney）拯救了这门被扼得透不过气的产业，完善了一台机器，做成了所需的工序。他所成就的解救是彻底的。第二年，美国输往英格兰的棉花，从以往年头的磅两皆无，增至 1,600,000 磅。他献上的是美元。

===Original===
Just before the battle of Quebec Wolf repeated and enjoyed Gray’s “Elegy” saying he valued that gem more highly than the capture of the city before which he was encamped. The next day the city was taken and Wolf was laid to rest. But the world is in debt to both the poet and the soldier﻿—a boundless debt, to the one for an eternal thought-gem, to the other for immortal heroism and devoted patriotism.

===Chinese===
魁北克城下决战前夕，沃尔夫（Wolfe）将军反复吟诵、深深味赏着格雷的《墓园挽歌》，说这颗思想之珠，比他兵锋所指的那座城池更可珍贵。次日城破，沃尔夫也长眠不起。但世界对诗人与军人同负着债——一笔无涯的债：对一位，欠一颗永恒的思想之珠；对另一位，欠不朽的英勇与舍身的爱国。

===Original===
Once there lived among men One whom sorrowing millions for centuries since have joyed to call friend﻿—One whose “come unto me ye that are heavy laden” has given solace and comfort to myriads of the human race. *He gave a life.*

===Chinese===
曾有一人在世人中间活着——千百年来，忧伤的万千民众乐于称他为朋友——他那一声「凡劳苦担重担的人，可以到我这里来」，给人类中的无数人带来了慰藉与安舒。*他献上的是一条生命。*

===Original===
We must as individuals compare our cost with what we are able to give. The worth of a race or a nation can be but the aggregate worth of its men and women. While we need not indulge in offensive boasting, it may not be out of place in a land where there is some adverse criticism and not a little unreasonable prejudice, quietly to take account of stock and see if we really represent a value in this great American commonwealth. The average American is never too prejudiced, I think, to have a keen appreciation for the utilities; and he is certainly not behind the rest of the world in his clear perception of the purchasing power of a dollar. Beginning here, then, I find that, exclusive of the billions of wealth *given* by them to enrich another race prior to the passage of the Thirteenth Amendment, the colored people of America today hold in their own right $264,000,000 of taxable property; and this is over and above the $50,000,000 which collapsed in the Freedman’s Savings Bank when that gigantic iniquity paralyzed the hope and shocked the faith of an inexperienced and unfinancial people.

===Chinese===
我们必须以个人的身份，比对我们所费于人与我们所能给予。一个种族或民族的价值，无非是其男女价值之总和。我们无须逞意气之争锋，然而在这样一个不无逆耳的批评、也不乏无理的偏见的国度里，安安静静地盘一盘货、看一看我们在这个伟大的美利坚共同体中究竟是否代表一份价值，或许不算不合时宜。我想，普通的美国人再心存偏见，也从不吝于敏锐地欣赏「有用」二字；而在看清一美元的购买力这件事上，他也绝不落后于世界上任何人。那么，就从这里开始：且不算宪法第十三修正案通过之前他们*奉献*给另一个种族、使之富足的那亿万资财，今日美国的有色人种，以自己的名分持有应税财产 264,000,000 美元；这还不算在那场巨大的不义中化为乌有的 50,000,000 美元——自由民储蓄银行（Freedman's Savings Bank）的倒闭，曾令一个毫无理财经验的民族希望瘫痪、信念震骇。

===Original===
One would like to be able to give reliable statistics of the agricultural and mechanical products of the colored laborer, but so far I have not been able to obtain them. It is a modest estimate, I am sure, to ascribe fully two-thirds of the 6,940,000 bales of cotton produced in 1888 to Negro cultivation. The reports give estimates only in bulk as to the products of a state or county. Our efficient and capable census enumerators never draw the color line on labor products. You have no trouble in turning to the page that shows exactly what percentage of colored people are illiterate, or just how many have been condemned by the courts; no use taking the trouble to specify whether it was for the larceny of a ginger cake, or for robbing a bank of a cool half million and skipping off to Canada: it’s all crime of course, and crime statistics and illiteracy statistics must be accurately detailed﻿—and colored.

===Chinese===
我很想提供有色劳动者农产品与制成品的可靠统计，但迄今未能觅得。把 1888 年所产 6,940,000 包棉花的三分之二悉归于黑人的耕耘，我相信这还是一个谦抑的估计。各项报告只按州县笼统开列产品估计。我们干练得力的普查员，在劳动产品上是从不画肤色线的。翻到标明有色人中文盲准确百分比的那一页，或已被法院定罪者的确切数目，你毫不费事；至于是为了一块姜饼行窃，还是卷走银行整整五十万、逃之加拿大，则不必劳神细究：反正都是犯罪，犯罪统计与文盲统计自须详确开列——而且一律「有色」。

===Original===
Similar commendable handling meets the colored producer from the managers of our Big American Show at Chicago which we are all so nervously anxious shall put the best foot foremost in bowing to the crowned heads and the gracious lords and ladies from over the waters. To allow any invention or mechanism, art or farm product to be accredited a black man would be drawing the color line! And our immaculate American could never be guilty of anything so vile as drawing a color line!!!

===Chinese===
我们那场将在芝加哥上演的「美国大秀」——我们个个提心吊胆，唯恐它不能在各国君王与海那边来的温文勋贵面前，先亮出最体面的一脚——其主办诸公对待有色生产者的手法，同样堪嘉。让任何一件发明、机械、艺术品或农产品记在一个黑人名下，那便是画肤色线了！而我们一尘不染的美国同胞，是断断做不出画肤色线这等下作事的！！！

===Original===
I am unable to say accurately, then, just how many bales of cotton, pounds of tobacco, barrels of molasses and bushels of corn and wheat are given to the world through Negro industry. The same difficulty is met in securing authentic information concerning their inventions and patents. The records of the Patent Office at Washington do not show whether a patentee is white or colored. And all inventions and original suggestions made by a colored man before emancipation were necessarily accredited to some white individual, a slave not being able to take the oath administered to the applicant for a patent. Prof. Wright, however, by simply collecting through personal inquiry the number of colored patentees which could be remembered and identified by examiners and attorneys practicing before the Patent Office authorities, published upwards of fifty in the *A.M.E. Review* for April, 1886. Doubtless this number was far within the truth, and many new patents have been taken out since his count was made. Almost daily in my walk I pass an ordinary looking black man, who, I am told, is considering an offer of $30,000 for his patent rights on a corn planter, which, by the way, has been chosen as part of the Ohio exhibit for the Columbian Exposition. He has secured as many as half a dozen patents within a few years and is carrying around a “new machine” in his head every day.

===Chinese===
于是，通过黑人劳作究竟交给了世界多少包棉花、多少磅烟草、多少桶糖蜜、多少蒲式耳的玉米与小麦，我无法确言。要取得他们发明与专利的可靠情报，同样困难。华盛顿专利局的档案并不载明专利权人是白人还是有色。而解放以前有色人所作的一切发明与创见，都必然记在某个白人名下——奴隶无法履行专利申请人所须作的宣誓。不过，赖特教授（Prof. Wright）仅凭私人询访，把专利局审查员与出庭代理人所能忆及、所能指认的有色专利权人凑拢来，已在 1886 年 4 月号的《非洲卫理公会评论》（*A.M.E. Review*）上发表了五十余人。此数无疑远低于实情，自他点数以来，又已有许多新专利注册。我散步时几乎天天路过一个相貌平平的黑人；人们告诉我，有人正出价 30,000 美元购买他一项玉米播种机的专利权——这项发明还恰好入选了哥伦布博览会的俄亥俄州展品。几年之内他已拿下足足半打专利，而且每天脑子里还转着一台「新机器」。

===Original===
Granville Wood, of Cincinnati, has given valuable returns to the world as an electrician; and there is no estimating the money in the outright gift of this people through unremunerated toil. The Negro does not always show a margin over and above consumption; but this does not necessarily in his case prove that he is not a producer. During the agitations for adverse legislation against the Chinese, the charge was alleged that they spent nothing in the country. They hoarded their earnings, lived on nothing, and finally returned to China to live in luxury and to circulate the wealth amassed in this country. A similar complaint can never be lodged against the Negro. Poor fellow, he generally lives pretty well up to his income. He labors for little and spends it all. He has never yet gained the full consent of his mind to “take his gruel a little thinner” till his little pile has grown a bit. He does not like to seem short. And had he the wage of a thousand a year his bigheartedness would immediately put him under the painful necessity of having it do the entertainment of five thousand. He must eat, and is miserable if he can’t dress; and seems on the whole internally fitted every way to the style and pattern of a millionaire, rather than to the plain, plodding, stingy old path of common sense and economy. This is a flaw in the *material* of the creature. The grain just naturally runs that way. If our basal question of economics were put to him: “*What do you give﻿—are you adding something every year to the world’s stored up capital?*” His ingenuous answer would be, as the ghost of a smile flits across his mobile lips﻿—“Yea, Lord; I give back *all*. I am even now living on the prospects of next year’s income. I give my labor at accommodation rates, and forthwith reconvert my wages into the general circulation. Funds, somehow, don’t seem to stick to me. I have no talents, or smaller coins either, hid in a napkin.” It will be well for him to learn, however, that it is not what we make but what we save that constitutes wealth. The hod-carrier who toils for $1.50 a day, spending the dollar and laying up the half, is richer than the congressman with an annual income of $5,000 and annual duns of $8,000. What he most urgently needs to learn is systematic saving. He works hard enough generally﻿—but does not seem able to retrench expenses﻿—to cut off the luxuries which people of greater income and larger foresight, seeing to be costly and unnecessary would deny themselves. He wants to set to work vigorously to widen the margin outside the expenditures. He cannot be too deeply impressed with the fact that tobacco and liquors﻿—even leaving out their moral aspects﻿—are too costly to be indulged in by any who are not living on the interest of capital ready in store. A man living on his earnings should eschew luxuries, if he wishes to produce wealth. But when those luxuries deteriorate manhood, they impoverish and destroy the most precious commodity we can offer the world.

===Chinese===
辛辛那提的格兰维尔·伍德（Granville Wood）作为电机技师，已向世界交出可观的回报；而这一民族以无偿劳作白白奉送的东西，其金钱价值更无从估量。黑人的支销并非总是低于收入、留有盈余；但这在他的情形未必便证明他不是生产者。排华恶法鼓噪之时，曾有人指控他们在这个国家分文不花：他们把挣来的钱窖藏起来，近乎不吃不喝，末了回中国享福，把在美国敛得的财富拿到那边去流通。类似的指控却永远安不到黑人头上。可怜的人，他过日子照例把进项花得精光。他挣得少，又花个罄尽。他至今还没能完全说服自己的心，「把稀粥再熬稀一点」，好等那一小堆积蓄长得厚些。他不喜欢显得寒酸。哪怕给他一千美元的年薪，他那副宽宏的心肠也会立刻害他陷入窘境：非拿它撑起五千美元的排场不可。他得吃，穿不上体面衣裳便苦恼；通身上下，他天生处处合乎百万富翁的气派款式，倒不合乎常识与节俭那条朴素的、埋头苦干的老路。这是造物*材料*里的一处瑕疵。木纹天生就朝那边长。倘若把他请到我们经济学的根基之问前：「*你给出什么——你是否每年都给世界的积蓄增添一点什么？*」他嘴角掠过一丝笑影，那副老实的答话会是：「是的，主啊；我把*全部*都还回去了。我这会儿过日子，靠的还是明年进项的指望。我的力气按通融的价钱出手，工钱一转手又流回总流通里去了。钱这东西，粘不住我。我没有把银子——连小钱也没有——包在手巾里藏着。」不过他最好还是学一学：构成财富的不是我们挣了多少，而是我们省下多少。日挣 1.50 美元的搬砖工，花掉一美元、存下五角，便比年薪 5,000 美元、年欠 8,000 美元的国会议员更富。他最亟须学会的，是有计划的储蓄。他干活照例够卖力——只是似乎不会节流——不会砍掉那些收入更丰、眼光更长的人明知其昂贵而甘愿戒绝的奢侈。他须得发狠做工，把开销之外的那道边际拓宽。这个道理他体会得再深也不为过：烟草与酒——纵然撇开其道德一面——对任何不是靠存量资本的利息过活的人，都贵得消受不起。靠劳动收入生活的人，想创造财富就应戒绝奢侈品。而这些奢侈品一旦贬损男子气概，毁掉的便是我们所能奉献给世界的最贵重的商品。

===Original===
For after all, the highest gifts are not measurable in dollars and cents. Beyond and above the class who run an account with the world and merely manage honestly to pay *in kind* for what they receive, there is a noble army﻿—the Shakespeares and Miltons, the Newtons, Galileos and Darwins﻿—Watts, Morse, Howe, Lincoln, Garrison, John Brown﻿—a part of the world’s roll of honor﻿—whose price of board and keep dwindles into nothingness when compared with what the world owes them; men who have taken of the world’s bread and paid for it in immortal thoughts, invaluable inventions, new facilities, heroic deeds of loving self-sacrifice; men who dignify the world for their having lived in it and to whom the world will ever bow in grateful worship as its heroes and benefactors. It may not be ours to stamp our genius in enduring characters﻿—but we can give what we are *at its best*.

===Chinese===
毕竟，最高的馈赠原不是以几元几角计量的。在那与世界记着账、老老实实仅以实物偿付其所受的一类人之上，还有一支高贵的大军——莎士比亚们与弥尔顿们，牛顿们、伽利略们与达尔文们——瓦特、莫尔斯、豪、林肯、加里森、约翰·布朗——世界荣誉名册上的一页——他们的膳宿之费，与世界所欠他们的相比，渺不足数；他们取了世界的面包，偿付以不朽的思想、无价的发明、新的便利，以及舍身之爱的英雄行迹；他们因曾活在这世上而使世界生辉，世界也将永远向他们俯首，感戴地奉为自己的英雄与恩主。以不朽的字符钤盖我们的天才，或许非我们所能——但我们能够献出*臻于至善*的自己。

===Original===
Visiting the slave market in Boston one day in 1761, Mrs. John Wheatley was attracted by the modest demeanor and intelligent countenance of a delicate looking black girl just from the slave ship. She was quite nude save for a piece of coarse carpet she had tied about her loins, and the only picture she could give of her native home was that she remembered her mother in the early morning every day pouring out water before the rising sun. The benevolent Mrs. Wheatley expended some labor in polishing up this crude gem, and in 1773 the gifted Phillis gave to the world a small octavo volume of one hundred and twenty precious pages, published in London and dedicated to the Countess of Huntingdon. In 1776, for some lines she had sent him, she received from the greatest American the following tribute dated at Cambridge:

===Chinese===
1761 年的一天，约翰·惠特利夫人（Mrs. John Wheatley）造访波士顿的奴隶市场，被一个刚下贩奴船、模样纤弱的黑女孩那端庄的举止与聪慧的面容所吸引。她几乎赤身露体，只在腰间缠了一片粗毡；她所能给出的故土图景，只有这么一点：她记得每天清早，母亲向着初升的太阳泼水。仁慈的惠特利夫人费了些功夫打磨这块璞玉，1773 年，才华横溢的菲莉丝向世界献出了一册一百二十页的小开本珍集，在伦敦出版，题献给亨廷顿伯爵夫人（Countess of Huntingdon）。1776 年，她因寄去几行诗，收到了那位最伟大的美国人自剑桥寄来的如下礼赞：

===Original===
> Miss Phillis:﻿—﻿ ﻿… I thank you most sincerely for your polite notice of me in the elegant lines you enclosed; and however undeserving I may be of such encomium and panegyric, the style and manner exhibit a striking proof of your poetical talents; in honor of which and as a tribute justly due to you, I would have published the poem had I not been apprehensive that, while I only meant to give the world this new instance of your genius, I might have incurred the imputation of vanity. This and nothing else determined me not to give it place in the public prints. If you should ever come to Cambridge or near headquarters, I shall be happy to see a person so favored by the Muses, and to whom nature has been so liberal and beneficent in her dispensations. I am, with great respect,
> Your obedient humble servant,
> George Washington.

===Chinese===
> 菲莉丝小姐：——……您在惠寄的隽雅诗行中对我的雅意垂注，我谨致最诚挚的谢意；无论这般的赞颂与褒扬于我何其不称，其风骨与笔致已为您的诗才出示了显豁的明证；为表敬意，也为献上理应归于您的礼敬，我本欲将此诗刊布问世，只是唯恐我本意虽仅在向世人再添一例您的天纵之才，却难免招致自炫之讥。唯此一端，别无他故，使我不曾让它在印刷品上占得一席之地。您若来日莅临剑桥或行辕左近，我必欣然迎见这样一位深蒙缪斯眷顾、天赋如此宽厚丰盈之人。我怀着深深的敬意，是您恭顺而卑微的仆人，
> 乔治·华盛顿（George Washington）。

===Original===
That girl paid her debts *in song*.

===Chinese===
那个女孩子以*歌声*偿清了她的债务。

===Original===
In South Carolina there are two brothers, colored men, who own and conduct one of the most extensive and successful farms in this country for floriculture. Their system of irrigating and fertilizing is the most scientific in the state, and by their original and improved methods of grafting and cultivating they have produced a new and rich variety of the rose called *Loiseaux*, from their name. Their roses are famous throughout Europe and are specially prized by the French for striking and marvellous beauty. The Loiseaux brothers send out the incense of their grateful returns to the world in the *sweet fragrance of roses*.

===Chinese===
南卡罗来纳有兄弟二人，有色人，拥有并经营着本国有数的一座最宏大、最兴旺的花卉农场。他们的灌溉与施肥体系是全州最科学的；凭着自己独创而改良的嫁接栽培之法，他们育出了一种丰艳的玫瑰新种，即以他们的姓氏命名的*卢瓦索*（Loiseaux）。他们的玫瑰名满欧洲，尤以惊心动魄之美为法国人所宝爱。卢瓦索兄弟向世界呈上他们感恩回礼的馨香——*在玫瑰的甜香里*。

===Original===
Some years ago a poor and lowly orphan girl stood with strange emotions before a statue of Benjamin Franklin in Boston. Her bosom heaved and her eyes filled as she whispered between her clenched teeth, “Oh, how I would like to make a stone man?” Wm. Lloyd Garrison became her providence and enlarged her opportunity; *she paid for it* in giving to the world the *Madonna with the Christ and Adoring Angels*, now in the collection of the Marquis of Bute. From her studio in Rome Edmonia Lewis, the colored sculptress, continues to increase the debt of the world to her by her graceful thoughts in the chaste marble.

===Chinese===
数年前，一个贫苦卑贱的孤女，怀着异样的心绪伫立在波士顿一座本杰明·富兰克林（Benjamin Franklin）雕像之前。她胸脯起伏，双眼噙泪，从咬紧的齿缝间低语：「啊，我多么想也造出一个石头人！」威廉·劳埃德·加里森（Wm. Lloyd Garrison）成了她的天意，拓展了她的机会；*她偿付了它*——把《圣母与圣婴及朝拜的天使》献给了世界，今藏布特侯爵（Marquis of Bute）的收藏。在她的罗马工作室里，有色女雕塑家埃德蒙妮娅·刘易斯（Edmonia Lewis）仍在以贞洁大理石中的隽美构思，继续加深世界对她的亏欠。

===Original===
On May 27, 1863, a mixed body of troops in blue stood eagerly expectant before a rebel stronghold. On the extreme right of the line, a post of honor and of danger, were stationed the Negro troops, the first and third regiments of the Louisiana Native Guards. On going into action, says an eye witness, they were 1,080 strong, and formed into four lines, Lieut.-Colonel Bassett, 1st Louisiana, forming the first line, and Lieut.-Colonel Henry Finnegas the second. Before any impression had been made upon the earth works of the enemy, and in full face of the batteries belching forth their sixty-two pounders, the order to charge was given﻿—and the black regiment rushed forward to encounter grape, canister, shell and musketry, having no artillery but two small howitzers﻿—which seemed mere popguns to their adversaries﻿—and with no reserve whatever. The terrible fire from the rebel guns upon the unprotected masses mowed them down like grass. Colonel Bassett being driven back, Colonel Finnegas took his place, and his men being similarly cut to pieces, Bassett reformed and recommenced. And thus these brave fellows went on from 7 o’clock in the morning till 3:30 p.m., under the most hideous carnage that men ever had to withstand. During this time they rallied and were ordered to make six distinct charges, losing thirty-seven killed, one hundred and fifty-five wounded, and one hundred and sixteen missing, “the majority, if not all of these,” adds a correspondent of the New York Times, who was an eye witness of the fight, “being in all probability now lying dead on the gory field without the rights of sepulture! *for when, by flag of truce our forces in other directions were permitted to reclaim their dead, the benefit, through some neglect, was not extended to these black regiments.*”

===Chinese===
1863 年 5 月 27 日，一支身着蓝装的混合部队，正热切而紧张地列阵于一处叛军堡垒之前。在全线的最右翼——一个荣誉与危险并重的哨位——驻扎着黑人部队：路易斯安那本土卫队（Louisiana Native Guards）第一与第三团。据一位目击者说，投入战斗时他们共 1,080 人，编为四线：路易斯安那第一团的中校巴塞特（Bassett）率第一线，亨利·芬尼加斯（Henry Finnegas）中校率第二线。敌军工事尚未受到任何挫动，而在那一片喷吐着 62 磅炮弹的炮垒的枪林正面，冲锋令下——黑人团队奋然前扑，迎着葡萄弹、筒弹、榴弹与排枪，除两门在敌人看来不过玩具铳的小榴弹炮外再无炮火，且无一兵预备队。叛军炮火对着这些毫无掩蔽的人群倾泻，把他们像割草一般放倒。巴塞特中校被击退，芬尼加斯中校顶了上去；他的部队同样被剁得七零八落，巴塞特重整旗鼓，再度冲锋。这些勇敢的人们就这样从清晨 7 点坚持到下午 3 点 30 分，顶着人所曾经受过的最骇人的屠戮。其间他们六次奉命冲锋，阵亡三十七人，负伤一百五十五人，失踪一百一十六人。「这些人中的多数——即便不是全部，」一位曾目击这场战斗的《纽约时报》记者补写道，「十有八九此刻正陈尸于血染的沙场，不得掩埋之礼！*因为当我军凭休战旗在他处获准领回阵亡将士时，此项恩惠，因某项疏漏，并未惠及这些黑人团队。*」

===Original===
“The deeds of heroism,” he continues, “performed by these colored men were such as the proudest white men might emulate. Their colors are torn to pieces by shot, and literally bespattered by blood and brains. The color-sergeant of the 1st La. on being mortally wounded, hugged the colors to his breast when a struggle ensued between the two color-corporals on each side of him as to who should bear the sacred standard﻿—and during this generous contention one of the corporals was wounded. One black lieutenant mounted the enemy’s works three or four times, and in one charge the assaulting party came within fifty paces of them. If only ordinarily supported by artillery and reserve, no one can convince us that they would not have opened a passage through the enemy’s works. Captain Callioux, of the 1st La., a man so black that he prided himself on his blackness, died the death of a hero leading on his men in the thickest of the fight. One poor wounded fellow came along with his arm shattered by a shell, jauntily swinging it with the other, as he said to a friend of mine: ‘Massa, guess I can fight no more.’ I was with one of the captains looking after the wounded, when we met one limping along toward the front. Being asked where he was going, he said, ‘I been shot in de leg, cap’n, an’ dey wants me to go to de hospital﻿—but I reckon I c’n gib ’em some mo’ yit.’ ”

===Chinese===
「这些有色人建立的英雄功勋，」他继续写道，「足以令最骄傲的白人起而效法。他们的军旗被炮火撕成碎片，实实在在溅满了血与脑浆。第一路易斯安那团的旗手中弹垂危之际，把军旗紧抱在胸前；他两侧的两名护旗下士随即争了起来——争谁该接掌这面神圣的旗帜——在这场高尚的相争中，又有一名下士负了伤。一名黑人中尉三番五次跃上敌人的工事，有一次冲锋，突击队逼近到距敌仅五十步。只要炮兵与预备队给予寻常的支援，谁也无法使我们相信他们打不开一条通入敌人工事的通道。第一路易斯安那团的卡利乌上尉（Captain Callioux），黑得以自己的黑为傲，在战事最炽处率队冲锋，死得像个英雄。一个可怜的伤号走过来，一条手臂被榴弹炸碎，另一只手还潇洒地甩着它，对我的一个朋友说：『老爷，估摸我打不动啦。』我正同一位上尉照看伤员，遇见一个一瘸一拐往前线去的。问他去哪儿，他说：『俺腿上挨了一枪，长官，他们叫俺上医院去——可俺寻思，俺还能再给他们来几下。』」

===Original===
Says Major-General Banks in the report from Headquarters of the Army of the Gulf, before Port Hudson, May 30, 1863, writing to Major-General Halleck, General-in-Chief at Washington: “The position occupied by the Negro troops was one of importance and called for the utmost steadiness and bravery in those to whom it was confided. It gives me pleasure to report that they answered every expectation. Their conduct was heroic. No troops could be more determined or more daring.”

===Chinese===
班克斯少将（Major-General Banks）在 1863 年 5 月 30 日于波特哈德森（Port Hudson）城前的海湾军团总部，致函华盛顿的陆军总司令哈勒克少将（Major-General Halleck），报告中写道：「黑人部队据守的位置关系重大，受命者须有超等的沉着与英勇。我乐于报告：他们不负所望。其行为堪称英勇。没有哪支部队能比他们更坚决、更无畏。」

===Original===
> “ ‘Charge!’ Trump and drum awoke,
 

 Onward the bondmen broke;
 

 Bayonet and sabre-stroke
 

 Vainly opposed their rush.
 

 Through the wild battle’s crush,
 

 With but one thought aflush,
 

 Driving their lords like chaff,
 

 In the guns’ mouths they laugh;
 

 Or at the slippery brands
 

 Leaping with open hands,
 

 Down they bear man and horse,
 

 Down in their awful course;
 

 Trampling with bloody heel
 

 Over the crashing steel,
 

 All their eyes forward bent,
 

 Rushed the black regiment.
> ‘Freedom!’ their battle-cry﻿—
 

 ‘Freedom! or leave to die!’
 

 Ah! and they meant the word,
 

 Not as with us ’tis heard,
 

 Not a mere party-shout:
 

 *They gave their spirits out*.
 

 Trusted the end to God,
 

 And on the gory sod
 

 Rolled in triumphant blood!”

===Chinese===
> 「『冲啊！』军号战鼓齐惊，
>  被奴的人们向前突进；
>  刺刀劈砍，马刀挥临，
>  挡不住他们的冲奔。
>  穿过鏖战的乱阵，
>  胸中一念如焚，
>  把他们的主子驱如糠秕，
>  他们在炮口里纵声笑吟；
>  或向滑腻的刀锋，
>  张开双手纵身，
>  人与马一齐扑倒，
>  倒在他们可怖的途程；
>  以血淋淋的脚跟，
>  踏过轧碎的钢铁，
>  所有的眼睛向前凝望——
>  黑人军团滚滚向前。
> 『自由！』是他们的战叫——
> 『自由！』不然就许我战死！
>  啊！这个字他们是当真的，
>  不像在我们口中那样轻飘，
>  不是一句党派的呼号：
>  他们交出了自己的灵魂。
>  把结局信托给上帝，
>  在血污的草地上，
>  翻滚于胜利的血泊！」

===Original===
And thus they paid *their debt*. “They gave﻿—*their spirits out*!”

===Chinese===
他们就这样偿清了*他们的债务*。「他们交出——*交出了自己的灵魂*！」

===Original===
In the heart of what is known as the “Black Belt” of Alabama and within easy reach of the great cotton plantations of Georgia, Mississippi, and Florida, a devoted young colored man ten years ago started a school with about thirty Negro children assembled in a comical looking shanty at Tuskegee. His devotion was contagious and his work grew; an abandoned farm of 100 acres was secured and that gradually grew to 640 acres, largely woodland, on which a busy and prosperous school is located; and besides a supply farm was added, of heavy rich land, 800 acres, from which grain and sugarcane are main products. Since 1881, 2,947 students have been taught here, of whom 102 have graduated, while 200 more have received enough training to fit them to do good work as teachers, intelligent farmers, and mechanics. The latest enrollment shows girls, 247; boys, 264. Of the 102 graduates, 70 percent are teachers, ministers and farmers. They usually combine teaching and farming. Three are printers (learned the trades at school), one is a tinner, one a blacksmith, one a wheelwright, three are merchants, three are carpenters, others in the professions or filling miscellaneous positions.

===Chinese===
在所谓亚拉巴马「黑土带」（Black Belt）的心脏地带，距佐治亚、密西西比与佛罗里达的大棉花庄园皆近在咫尺之处，一位矢志奉献的青年有色人十年前在塔斯基吉（Tuskegee）创办了一所学校，约三十名黑人孩童聚在一间样子滑稽的棚屋里开课。他的奉献感人相传，事业日益壮大；先置得一座 100 英亩的废弃农场，渐次扩充到 640 英亩（大半为林地），一所兴旺忙碌的学校便坐落其上；此外又添置了一处 800 英亩膏腴沃地的供应农场，谷物与甘蔗为其主要出产。自 1881 年以来，共有 2,947 名学生在此受教，其中 102 人毕业，另有 200 人所受训练足以胜任教师、明达的农人与工匠之职。最近一次注册显示：女生 247 名，男生 264 名。102 名毕业生中，70% 为教师、牧师与农人，他们多半教书务农兼顾。三人做印刷工（手艺是在学校学的），一人做白铁匠，一人做铁匠，一人做轮匠，三人经商，三人做木工，余者操专业或任杂职。

===Original===
That man is paying his debt by giving to this country *living, working, consecrated men and women*!

===Chinese===
那个人正在偿付他的债务：他把*活的、干活的、献了身的男女*献给了这个国家！

===Original===
Now each can give something. It may not be a poem, or marble bust, or fragrant flower even; it may not be ours to place our lives on the altar of country as a loving sacrifice, or even to devote our living activities so extensively as B. T. Washington to supplying the world’s need for strong and willing helpers. But we can at least *give ourselves*. Each can be *one* of those strong willing helpers﻿—even though nature has denied him the talent of endlessly multiplying his force. And nothing less can honorably cancel our debt. Each is under a most sacred obligation not to squander the material committed to him, not to sap its strength in folly and vice, and to see at the least that he delivers a product worthy the labor and cost which have been expended on him. A sound manhood, a true womanhood is a fruit which the lowliest can grow. And it is a commodity of which the supply never exceeds the demand. There is no danger of the market being glutted. The world will always want *men*. The worth of one is infinite. To this value all other values are merely relative. Our money, our schools, our governments, our free institutions, our systems of religion and forms of creeds are all first and last to be judged by this standard: what sort of men and women do they grow? How are men and women being shaped and molded by this system of training, under this or that form of government, by this or that standard of moral action? You propose a new theory of education; *what sort of men does it turn out?* Does your system make boys and girls superficial and mechanical? Is it a producing of average percentages or a rounding out of manhood﻿—a sound, thorough, and practical development﻿—or a scramble for standing and marks?

===Chinese===
如今，人人都拿得出一点什么。它未必是一首诗，未必是一尊大理石半身像，甚至未必是一朵芳香的花；把生命作为爱的祭牲安放在祖国的祭坛上，或许也轮不到我们；像布克·T·华盛顿（B. T. Washington）那样，把自己在世的精力如此广博地献给世界对强壮而甘愿的助力之需，或许也非我们所能。但我们至少能够*献出我们自己*。每个人都能够做那强壮而甘愿的助力中之*一员*——哪怕造化没有赐他无穷放大自身力量的才具。而唯有如此，才算体面地清偿了我们的债务。每个人都负着一桩至圣的义务：不糟蹋交到他手里的材料，不在愚妄与邪恶里蛀空它的气力，至少要确保他交出的产品，配得上已在他身上耗去的劳力与成本。健全的男子品格、纯真的女子品格，是一种最低微者也栽得出的果实。而这是一宗供应永不逾于需求的商品，市场绝无壅塞之虞。世界永远要*人*。一个人的价值是无穷的：一切别的价值，对此价值而言都只是相对的。我们的金钱、我们的学校、我们的政府、我们的自由制度、我们的宗教体系与信条形式，归根结底都要受这一标准的检验：它们种出的是什么样的男人和女人？男女正在被这种训练体系、这种那种政体、这条那条道德行为的准绳，怎样地塑造与浇铸？你提出一套新的教育理论；*它造出的是什么样的人？*你的制度是否把男孩女孩弄得肤浅而机械？它是在制造平均的百分数，还是在成全完整的男子品格——健全、扎实而切于实用的发展——抑或只是一场名次与分数的抢夺？

===Original===
We have a notion here in America that our political institutions﻿—the possibilities of a liberal and progressive democracy, founded on universal suffrage and in some hoped-for, providential way *compelling* universal education and devotion﻿—our peculiar American attainments are richly worth all they have cost in blood and anguish. But our form of government, divinely ordered as we dream it to be, must be brought to the bar to be tested by this standard. It is nothing worth of itself﻿—independently of whether it furnishes a good atmosphere in which to cultivate men. Is it developing a self-respecting freedom, a sound manliness on the part of *the individual*﻿—or does it put into the power of the wealthy few the opportunity and the temptation to corrupt the many? If our vaunted “*rule of the people*” does not breed nobler men and women than monarchies have done﻿—it must and will inevitably give place to something better.

===Chinese===
我们美国人有这样一种信念：我们的政治制度——一种建立在普选之上、并以某种可望的天意之道*强令*普遍的教育与虔敬的自由进步民主之可能——我们这份独特的美国业绩，以其所耗的血泪而言是足值的。然而我们梦想中奉若神明的这一政体，也必须带到这架天平前受审。它本身并无价值——全看它是否提供一种宜于培植人的氛围。它是在培育*个人*自尊的自由、健全的刚毅，还是在把腐蚀多数的机会与诱惑，交到豪富的少数人手里？倘若我们自诩的「*民治*」造不出比君主国更高贵的男女——它就必定、也必然要让位于更好的东西。

===Original===
I care not for the theoretical symmetry and impregnable logic of your moral code, I care not for the hoary respectability and traditional mysticisms of your theological institutions, I care not for the beauty and solemnity of your rituals and religious ceremonies, I care not even for the reasonableness and unimpeachable fairness of your social ethics﻿—if it does not turn out better, nobler, truer men and women﻿—if it does not add to the world’s stock of valuable souls﻿—if it does not give us a sounder, healthier, more reliable product from this great factory of *men*﻿—I will have none of it. I shall not try to test your logic, but weigh your results﻿—and that test is the *measure of the stature of the fullness of a man*. You need not formulate and establish the credibility and authenticity of Christian Evidences, when you can demonstrate and prove the present value of **Christian men**. And this test for systems of belief, for schools of thought, and for theories of conduct, is also the ultimate and inevitable test of nations, of races and of individuals. What sort of men do you turn out? *How* are you supplying the great demands of the world’s market? What is your true value? This, we may be sure, will be the final test by which the colored man in America will one day be judged in the cool, calm, unimpassioned, unprejudiced second thought of the American people.

===Chinese===
你的道德法典在理论上如何对称、逻辑如何无懈可击，我不在意；你的神学建制如何年高德劭、传统如何玄奥，我不在意；你的仪文与宗教典礼如何华美庄严，我不在意；甚至你的社会伦理如何合乎情理、如何公正得无可指摘，我也不在意——倘若它造不出更好、更高贵、更真实的男女——倘若它不曾给世界的珍贵灵魂之库存添上一分——倘若它不能从*人*的这座大工厂里给我们一个更健全、更康强、更可靠的产品——我便不要它。我不试你的逻辑，只称你的结果——而那个检验，便是*一个人丰盈身量的尺度*。你无须去为基督宗教证据的可信与确实铺陈论证，只要你拿得出**基督徒式的人**的现时价值。这一对信仰体系、思想流派与行为理论的检验，同样也是民族、种族与个人的终极而不可避免的检验。你造出的是什么样的人？你*怎样*应答世界市场的巨大需求？你的真正价值几何？我们可以确信：终有一日，美国人民在冷静、平和、不动声色、不抱成见的第二度思考里，将以这一检验来裁判美洲的有色人。

===Original===
Let us then quietly commend ourselves to this higher court﻿—this final tribunal. Short sighted idiosyncracies are but transient phenomena. It is futile to combat them, and unphilosophical to be depressed by them. To allow such things to overwhelm us, or even to absorb undue thought, is an admission of weakness. As sure as time *is﻿—these mists will clear away*. And the world﻿—our world, will surely and unerringly see us as we are. Our only care need be the intrinsic worth of our contributions. If we represent the ignorance and poverty, the vice and destructiveness, the vagabondism and parasitism in the world’s economy, no amount of philanthropy and benevolent sentiment can win for us esteem: and if we contribute a positive value in those things the world prizes, no amount of negrophobia can ultimately prevent its recognition. And our great “problem” after all is to be solved not by brooding over it, and orating about it, but by *living into it*.

===Chinese===
那么，让我们安静地把我们自己托付给这座更高的法庭——这终审的裁判所。目光短浅的癖性不过是浮云过眼。与之搏斗是徒劳，为之沮丧是不智。容这类东西压倒我们，甚至容它们吞没我们过量的心思，便是自认软弱。时间既在——*这些迷雾终将散尽*。而这个世界——我们的世界，必将确实无误地看清我们的本相。我们唯一须挂心的，只是我们贡献的内在价值。倘若在世界的大经济中，我们代表的是愚昧与贫困、邪恶与毁败、游惰与寄生，那么多少慈善、多少仁善的情肠也换不来敬重；倘若我们在世界所珍重之事上贡献了正的价值，那么多少厌黑症（negrophobia）也终究挡不住它的被承认。而我们那个大大的「问题」，归根结底不是靠对之冥想、对之演说所能解决，而是要靠*活进它里面去*。

### The Gain from a Belief / 信念的收益

===Original===
A solitary figure stands in the marketplace, watching as from some lonely tower the busy throng that hurry past him. A strange contrast his cold, intellectual eye to the eager, strained, hungry faces that surge by in their never ending quest of wealth, fame, glory, bread.

===Chinese===
市场里立着一个孤独的身影，仿佛从某座孤塔上眺望那群匆匆从他身旁涌过的忙碌人潮。他那只冷静而理智的眼睛，与一张张热切、紧绷、饥渴的面孔恰成奇特的对照——那些面孔在永不休止的寻求中翻涌而过，寻求着财富、名声、荣耀、面包。

===Original===
Mark his pallid cheek and haggard brow, and the fitful gleam of those restless eyes like two lone campfires on a deserted plain.

===Chinese===
且看他苍白的双颊与憔悴的额头，看他那双不安的眼睛里忽明忽灭的微光，宛如荒原上两堆孤零零的营火。

===Original===
Why does that smile, half cynical, half sad, flit across his countenance as he contemplates these mighty heartthrobs of human passions and woes, human hopes and human fears? Is it pity﻿—is it contempt﻿—is it hate for this struggling, working, believing humanity which curls those lips and settles upon that hitherto indifferent brow?

===Chinese===
当他凝望人类激情与苦难、希望与恐惧那一次次剧烈的心跳时，为什么一丝半含讥诮、半含哀愁的笑意会掠过他的面容？是怜悯——是轻蔑——还是对这个挣扎着、劳作着、笃信着的人类的憎恨，扭曲了那两片嘴唇，落上了那素来冷漠的眉头？

===Original===
Who is he?

===Chinese===
他是谁？

===Original===
Earth’s skepticism looking on at the protean antics of earth’s enthusiasms. Speculative unbelief, curiously and sneeringly watching the humdrum, commonplace, bread-and-butter toil of unspeculative belief. Lofty, unimpassioned agnosticism, *that thinks*﻿—face to face with hobbling, blundering, unscientific faith, *that works*.

===Chinese===
他是尘世的怀疑主义，冷眼旁观尘世种种热忱千变万化的表演。他是思辨的不信，好奇而带讥地注视着不事思辨的信仰那平庸单调、谋衣谋食的操劳。他是高蹈而不动感情的不可知论，*它思想*——与它面对面的，是那跛行的、笨拙的、不合科学的信仰，*它行动*。

===Original===
Dare we approach?

===Chinese===
我们敢上前么？

===Original===
“Sir: I perceive you are not drawn into the whirlpool of hurrying desires that sweep over earth’s restless sons. Your philosophy, I presume, lifts you above the toils and anxieties the ambitions and aspirations of the common herd. Pardon me, but do you not feel called to devote those superior powers of yours to the uplifting of your less favored brethren? May not you pour the oil of human kindness and love on these troubled waters? May not your wisdom shape and direct the channel of this tortuous stream, building up here, and clearing out there, till this torrent become once more a smiling river, reflecting Heaven’s pure love in its silvery bosom, and again this fruitful valley blossom with righteousness and peace? Does not your soul burn within you as you look on this seething mass of struggling, starving, sinning souls? Are you not inspired to lift up despairing, sinking, grovelling man﻿—to wipe the grime and tears from his marred countenance, and bid him Look aloft and be strong, Repent and be saved, Trust God and live!”

===Chinese===
「先生：我看出您并未被卷入那席卷尘世不安之子的欲望漩涡。我猜想，您的哲学使您超然于芸芸众生的操劳与焦虑、雄心与抱负之外。恕我冒昧：您可曾觉得受着感召，要把您那些优越的才能奉献出来，扶助您那些福分较薄的同胞？您可愿把人类仁慈与爱的膏油浇在这动荡的水面上？您可愿以您的智慧疏浚、导引这条九曲回环的水道——此处筑起一道堤，彼处清开一段淤——直待这股湍流重新变成一条含笑的江河，在它银色的胸怀里映出上天纯净的爱，这丰饶的溪谷重又开遍正义与和平之花？当您望着这一大片翻腾挣扎、饥寒交迫、身陷罪孽的灵魂，您心中可曾燃烧？您可曾受到感召，要扶起那绝望的、沉沦的、匍匐在地的人——拭去他污损面容上的尘垢与泪水，吩咐他昂首仰望而刚强，悔改而得救，信上帝而得生！」

===Original===
Ah! the coldness of the look he turned on me! Methought ’twould freeze my soul. “Poor fool!” it seemed to say; and yet I could not but think I discovered a trace of sadness as he replied:﻿—

===Chinese===
啊！他转向我的那一瞥何其冰冷！我几乎以为它会冻彻我的灵魂。那眼神仿佛在说：「可怜的傻子！」然而当他答话时，我仍不能不觉得其中透出一缕悲凉：——

===Original===
“What is man?﻿—A curiously fashioned clock; a locomotive, capable of sensations;﻿—a perfected brute. Man is a plant that grows and thinks; the form and place of his growth and the product of his thought are as little dependent on his will or effort as are the bark, leaves, and fruit of a tree on its choice. Food, soil, climate﻿—these make up the man﻿—the whole man, his life, his soul (if he have one). Man’s so-called moral sense is a mere dance of molecules; his spiritual nature, a pious invention. Remorse is a blunder, repentance is vain, self-improvement or reformation an impossibility. The laws of matter determine the laws of intellect, and these shape man’s nature and destiny and are as inevitable and uncontrollable as are the laws of gravitation and chemical affinity. You would-be reformers know not the stupendous nonsense you are talking. Man is as little responsible for vice or crime as for fever or an earthquake. Those in whom the cerebrum shows a particular formation, will make their holidays in gambling, betting, drinking, horse-racing﻿—their more serious pursuits in stealing, ravening, murdering. They are not immoral any more than a tiger is immoral; they are simply *un*moral. They need to be restrained, probably, as pests of society, or submitted to treatment as lunatics. Their fellows in whom the white and gray matter of the brain cells are a little differently correlated, will in their merry moods sing psalms and make it their habitual activity to reach out after the Unknown in various ways, trying to satisfy the vague and restless longings of what they call their souls by punishing themselves and pampering the poor. I have neither blame nor praise. Each class simply believe and do as they must. And as for God﻿—science finds him not. If there be a God﻿—He is unknown and unknowable. The finite mind of man cannot conceive the Infinite and Eternal. And if such a being exists, he cannot be concerned about the miserable wretches of earth. Searching after him is vain. Man has simply projected his own personality into space and worshipped it as a God﻿—a person﻿—himself. My utmost knowledge is limited to a series of sensations within, aware of itself; and a possibility of sensations without, both governed by unbending laws within the limits of experience and a reasonable distance beyond.”

===Chinese===
「人是什么？——一只构造精巧的钟；一台能有感觉的机车；——一头完善化了的畜生。人是一株会生长、会思想的植物；他生长的形态与处所、他思想的产物，之不系于他的意志或努力，恰如树木的皮、叶、果实之不系于树的选择。食物、土壤、气候——这些造成了人——造成了整个的人，他的生命，他的灵魂（倘若他有灵魂的话）。人所谓的道德感，不过是分子的一场舞蹈；他的灵性，不过是一项虔敬的发明。悔恨是失策，忏悔是枉然，自我改进或改造断无可能。物质的法则决定理智的法则，而后者又塑造人的天性与命运，并且如引力律与化学亲和律一样，无可规避，无从驾驭。你们这班自命的改革者，全然不知自己说的是何等天大的胡话。人为他的恶行或罪行所负的责任，并不比为一场热病或一次地震所负的更多。凡大脑显示出某种特定构造的人，会把假日消磨在赌博、押注、酗酒、赛马上——把更正经些的营生用在偷窃、劫掠、杀戮上。他们并不比老虎更不道德；他们根本就是*非*道德的。他们大概需要被当作社会祸害加以约束，或当作疯子交付治疗。他们的同类——那些脑细胞中白质与灰质的关联方式稍有不同的人——兴之所至便会唱起圣诗，并把以种种方式探求那「不可知者」当作惯常的功课，试图以苦待己身、娇养穷人来安抚他们所谓灵魂里那模糊而不宁的渴望。我既无责备，也无褒扬。每一类人只是信其所必信、行其所必行。至于上帝——科学找不到他。倘若真有一位上帝——他是未知的，也是不可知的。人有限的心智无法构想那无限与永恒。而倘若这样的存在果真存在，他也不会把心思放在尘世这些可怜的苦人身上。寻觅他是徒劳。人不过是把自己的人格投射到太空之中，再把这人格当作神来敬拜——当作一个人——他自己。我的全部知识，不外乎内部一连串自知的感受，加上外部种种感受的可能性；两者皆由不容变通的法则支配，其效力止于经验的界限之内、以及合理外推所及的不远之处。」

===Original===
“And beyond that Beyond” I ask breathlessly﻿—“beyond that Beyond?”

===Chinese===
「那么，在那『彼界』之外呢？」我屏息问道——「在那『彼界』之外呢？」

===Original===
I am sure I detected just then a tremor as of a chill running through that fragile frame; and the eye, at first thoughtful and coldly scornful only, is now unmistakably shaded with sadness. “Beyond that Beyond?” he repeated slowly﻿—beyond that Beyond, *if* there be such﻿—*spaces of darkness and eternal silence!*

===Chinese===
我确信，就在那一刻，我察觉一阵寒颤般的战栗掠过他那孱弱的身躯；那双起初只是沉思而冷冷含讥的眼睛，此刻已分明蒙上一层悲哀。「在那『彼界』之外？」他缓缓地重复道——在那彼界之外，*倘若*真有那样的所在——*是黑暗与永恒沉默的茫茫空域！*

===Original===
Whether this prolonged throb of consciousness exist after its external possibilities have been dissolved﻿—I cannot tell. That is to me﻿—a horrible plunge﻿—*in the dark!* I stand at the confluence of two eternities and three immensities. I see, with Pascal, only infinities in all directions which envelop me like an atom﻿—like a shadow which endures for a moment and﻿—will never return! All that I know is that I must die, but what I know the very least of is that very death﻿—which I can not avoid! *The eternal silence* of these infinite paces maddens me!

===Chinese===
这绵延的意识搏动，在其外部的种种可能消解之后是否还会存在——我说不出。那于我——是一次骇人的纵身一跃——*跃进黑暗！*我站在两个永恒与三重无垠的交汇之处。我与帕斯卡尔（Pascal）一样，只见四面八方的无穷，它们裹挟着我，如裹一粒微尘——如裹一道延续片刻、而且——永不再来的影子！我所知道的一切，只是我必须死；而我所知最少的，恰恰是那场死——那场我无从逃避的死！这些无限空域的*永恒沉默*，使我发狂！

===Original===
Sick at heart, I turn away and ask myself what is this system which, in the words of Richter, makes the universe an automaton, and man’s future﻿—a coffin! Is this the cold region to which thought, as it moves in its orbit, has brought us in the nineteenth century? Is this the germ of the “Philosophy of the future”﻿—the exponent of our “advanced ideas,” the “new light” of which our age so uproariously boasts? Nay rather is not this *monstruum horrendum* of our day but a renewal of the empiricism and skepticism of the days of Voltaire? Here was undoubtedly the nucleus of the cloud no bigger than a man’s hand, which went on increasing in bulk and blackness till it seemed destined to enshroud earth and heaven in the gloom of hell.

===Chinese===
我心中作呕，转过身去，自问这是一个什么体系——用里克特（Richter）的话说，它把宇宙变成一台自动机，把人的前程——变成一口棺材！思想循着自身的轨道运行，在十九世纪竟把我们带到了这样一片寒凉的地带么？这就是那「未来哲学」的胚芽——我们「先进思想」的代言者、我们这个时代喧腾自夸的「新光」么？不，毋宁说，我们今天这个 *monstruum horrendum*（骇人的怪物），岂不分明只是伏尔泰（Voltaire）时代那经验主义与怀疑主义的死灰复燃？无疑，这里正是一片不过如人手大小的云的云核，它不断增大、加深，直到仿佛注定要把地和天一并裹进地狱的幽暗。

===Original===
David Hume, who, though seventeen years younger than Voltaire, died in 1776 just two years before the great French skeptic, taught skepticism in England on purely metaphysical grounds. Hume knew little or nothing about natural science; but held that what we call mind consists merely of successive perceptions, and that we can have no knowledge of anything but phenomena. His system afterwards passes through France, is borrowed and filtered through the brain of a half crazy French schoolmaster, Auguste Comte, who thus becomes the founder of the Comtist school of Positivism or Nescience or Agnosticism as it is variously called. The adherents of his school admit neither revelation, nor a God, nor the immortality of the soul. Comte held, among other things, that two hours a day should be spent in the worship of Collective Humanity to be symbolized by some of the *sexe aimant*. On general principles it is not quite clear which is the *sexe aimant*. But as Comte proceeds to mention one’s wife, mother, and daughter as fitting objects of religious adoration because they represent the present, past and future of Humanity﻿—one is left to infer that he considered the female the *loving sex* and the ones to be worshipped; though he does not set forth who were to be objects of woman’s own adoring worship. In this ecclesiastical system which Prof. Huxley wittily denominates Romanism minus Christianity, Comte made himself High Pontiff, and his inamorata, the widow of a galley slave, was chief saint. This man was founder of the system which the agnostic prefers to the teachings of Jesus! However, had this been all, the positivist would have been as harmless as any other lunatic. But he goes a step farther and sets up his system as the philosophy of *natural science*, originating in and proved by pure observation and investigation of physical phenomena; and scoffs at as presumptuous and unwarrantable all facts that cannot be discerned through the senses. In this last position he is followed by John Stuart Mill, Herbert Spencer, G. H. Lewes, and a noble army of physicists, naturalists, physiologists, and geologists. Says one: “We have no knowledge of anything but phenomena, and the essential nature of phenomena and their ultimate causes are unknown and inscrutable to us.” Says another: “All phenomena without exception are governed by invariable laws with which no volitions natural or supernatural interfere.” And another: “Final causes are unknown to us and the search after them is fruitless, a mere chase of a favorite will-o-the-wisp. We know nothing about any supposed purposes for which organs ‘were made.’ Birds fly because they have wings, a true naturalist will never say﻿—he can never know they have wings *in order that* they may fly.”

===Chinese===
大卫·休谟（David Hume）虽比伏尔泰年轻十七岁，却于 1776 年、恰在那位伟大的法国怀疑论者之前两年辞世；他在英国教授怀疑主义，所持纯粹是形而上学的根据。休谟对自然科学所知甚少，甚至可以说一无所知；但他主张，我们所谓的心智不过是一连串前后相继的知觉，而且我们对任何事物的认识都只能止于现象。他的体系随后取道法国，被一位半疯的法国小学教员——奥古斯特·孔德（Auguste Comte）——的头脑借用、滤取；此人于是成了孔德学派的创始人——这一学派时而被称为实证主义，时而被称为无知论（Nescience）、不可知论（Agnosticism），名目不一。这一学派的信奉者既不承认启示，也不承认上帝，也不承认灵魂不朽。孔德所持的主张之一，是每天应当花两个小时礼拜「集体人类」，并由几位 *sexe aimant*（有爱的性别）者来作它的象征。照一般道理，究竟哪一性才算 *sexe aimant*（有爱的性别），并不十分清楚。但既然孔德进而举出人的妻子、母亲、女儿，作为宗教崇敬的合宜对象——因为她们分别代表人类的现在、过去与未来——人们便只能推断，他把女性当成了「有爱的性别」、当成了应受崇拜者；只是他没有说明，女性自己那一份虔敬的崇拜又当以谁为对象。在这套被赫胥黎教授（Prof. Huxley）风趣地命名为「罗马公教减去基督教」的教会体系里，孔德自立为最高大祭司，而他的意中人——一名苦役舰划桨奴的遗孀——则做了首席圣徒。就是这样一个人，创立了不可知论者宁取之而舍耶稣教导的那套体系！然而，倘若仅止于此，这位实证主义者原也不过同任何别的疯子一样无害。但他更进一步，把自己的体系立为*自然科学*的哲学，宣称它源起于、并证明于对物理现象的纯粹观察与探究；凡不能经由感官察知的事实，一概被他讥为僭妄而无据。在这最后一个立场上，约翰·斯图亚特·密尔（John Stuart Mill）、赫伯特·斯宾塞（Herbert Spencer）、G. H. 刘易斯（G. H. Lewes），以及一支由物理学家、博物学家、生理学家与地质学家组成的堂皇大军，都步了他的后尘。其中一位说：「除了现象，我们对任何事物都无所知；现象的本质及其究极原因，于我们是未知的、无从洞彻的。」另一位说：「一切现象，无分例外，皆受恒定不变的法则支配，无论自然的还是超自然的意志，皆无从干预。」又一位说：「目的因非我们所能识，对它的追寻徒劳无功，不过是追猎一只心爱的鬼火（will-o-the-wisp）。关于器官是『为了』何种假定的目的『被造』，我们一无所知。『鸟因为有翅膀而飞』——一位真正的博物学家绝不会这样说——他绝不可能知道，鸟有翅膀*是为了*它们能飞。」

===Original===
And Mr. Ingersoll, the American exponent of positivism, in his “Why I Am an Agnostic,” winds up a glittering succession of epigrammatic inconsistencies with these words: “Let us be honest with ourselves. In the presence of countless mysteries, standing beneath the boundless heaven sown thick with constellations, knowing that each grain of sand, each leaf, each blade of grass, asks of every mind the answerless question; knowing that the simplest thing defies solution; feeling that we deal with the superficial and the relative and that we are forever eluded by the real, the absolute﻿—let us admit the limitations of our minds, and let us have the courage and the candor to say: we do not know.”

===Chinese===
而实证主义在美国的代言者英格索尔先生（Mr. Ingersoll），在他的《我为什么是个不可知论者》（Why I Am an Agnostic）里，用下面这番话为一连串闪烁生辉、警句迭出的自相矛盾收了尾：「让我们对自己诚实。面对数不清的奥秘，站在缀满星座的无边天穹之下，知道每一粒沙、每一片叶、每一茎草，都在向每一颗心灵提出无从作答的问题；知道最简单的事物也拒不就范于解答；觉得我们打交道的只是肤浅的与相对的东西，而那真实的、绝对的东西永远从我们手边溜走——那就让我们承认自己心智的局限，拿出勇气与坦诚来说：我们不知道。」

===Original===
It is no part of my purpose to enter into argument against the agnostics. Had I the wish, I lack the ability. It is enough for me to know that they have been met by foemen worthy their steel and that they are by no means invincible.

===Chinese===
同不可知论者展开论战，本不在我的目的之内。纵有此愿，亦乏此才。我知道他们已遭遇过堪与交锋的敌手、绝非不可战胜，这就尽够了。

===Original===
“The average man,” says Mr. Ingersoll, “does not reason﻿—he feels.” And surely ’twere presumption for an average woman to attempt more. For my part I am content to “feel.” The brave Switzer who sees the awful avalanche stealing down the mountainside threatening death and destruction to all he holds dear, hardly needs any very correct ratiocination on the mechanical and chemical properties of ice. He *feels* there is danger nigh and there is just time for him to sound the tocsin of alarm and shout to his dear ones “fly!”

===Chinese===
「寻常人，」英格索尔先生说，「不推理——他感受。」一个寻常女子若想更进一层，想必更属僭妄。至于我，甘愿止于「感受」。那位勇敢的瑞士山民，眼见骇人的雪崩悄悄滑下山坡，威胁着他所挚爱的一切生死存亡，实在用不着先对冰的力学与化学性质做一番多么精确的推论。他*感受*到危险临头，而时间刚够他敲响警钟，向亲人们喊一声「快逃！」

===Original===
For me it is enough to know that by this system God and Love are shut out; prayer becomes a mummery; the human will but fixed evolutions of law; the precepts and sanctions of morality a lie; the sense of responsibility a disease. The desire for reformation and for propagating conviction is thus a fire consuming its tender. Agnosticism has nothing to impart. Its sermons are the exhortations of one who convinces you he stands on nothing and urges you to stand there too. If your creed is that nothing is sure, there is certainly no spur to proselytize. As in an icicle the agnostic abides alone. The vital principle is taken out of all endeavor for improving himself or bettering his fellows. All hope in the grand possibilities of life are blasted. The inspiration of beginning now a growth which is to mature in endless development through eternity is removed from our efforts at self-culture. The sublime conception of life as the seedtime of character for the growing of a congenial inner-self to be forever a constant conscious presence is changed into the base alternative conclusion, *Let us eat and drink for tomorrow we die*.

===Chinese===
于我，知道下列这些便尽够了：在这套体系之下，上帝与爱被关在门外；祈祷成了一场装模作样的哑剧；人的意志不过是法则的固定演进；道德的训诫与制裁成了一句谎言；责任感成了一种病。于是，改造的热望与传播信念的热望，便成了一场吞噬自家引柴的火。不可知论无可馈赠。它的布道，是先叫你相信他自己站在虚无之上、再劝你也站到那上面去的那种劝勉。倘若你的信条是「一切都不可靠」，那就断然没有劝人改宗的鞭策。不可知论者像一根冰柱，独自悬挂。一切求完善自己、求造福同胞的努力，都被抽去了生命的原理。对人生种种宏大可能的一切希望都凋萎了。从现在就开始一场生长、并将在无尽的开展中历经永恒而臻于成熟的灵感，被从我们自我修养的努力中抽走了。人生那庄严的构想——视人生为品格的播种期，借此培育一个意气相投的内在自我，使它成为一份恒常不灭、有意识的同在——被换成了那个卑下的备择结论：*让我们吃吃喝喝吧，因为明天要死了*。

===Original===
To my mind the essence of the poison is just here. As far as the metaphysical grounds for skepticism are concerned, they are as harmless to the masses as if they were entombed in Greek or Hebrew. Many of the terms, it is true, are often committed to memory and paraded pretty much in the spirit of the college sophomore who affects gold-bowed spectacles and stooping shoulders﻿—it is scholarly, you know. But the real reasons for and against agnosticism rest on psychological and scientific facts too abstruse for the laity to appreciate. There is much subtle sophistry in the oracular utterances of a popular speaker like Mr. Ingersoll, which catch the fancy and charm the imagination of the many. His brilliant blasphemies like the winged seed of the thistle are borne on the slightest breath of wind and find lodgment in the shallowest of soils; while the refutation of them, undertaken in a serious and logical vein is often too conclusive to convince: that is, it is too different in kind to reach the same class of minds that have been inoculated with the poison germs.

===Chinese===
在我看来，这毒的精髓恰在于此。至于怀疑主义的形而上学根据，它们于大众原是无害的，其无害的程度，一如被封存在希腊文或希伯来文之中。诚然，其中许多术语常被人记诵在心、炫示于口，那心态同大学二年级学生装模作样戴上金框眼镜、弓起肩膀的做派相去无几——你知道，那才显得有学问。但赞成与反对不可知论的真正理由，都建立在过于玄奥、非门外汉所能领会的心理学与科学事实之上。像英格索尔先生这样一位广受欢迎的演说家，他那神谕式的言谈里藏着许多精微的诡辩，正投众人之所好、摄众人之想象。他那些才华横溢的渎神之语，好比蓟草带翅的种子，乘着最轻微的一缕风便飘散开来，在最浅薄的土壤里也能落地生根；而对它们的驳斥，若以严肃而合于逻辑的路数出之，却又往往因太过确凿反而无从服人：也就是说，它品类悬隔，够不着那同一类已被接种了毒菌的心智。

===Original===
My own object, however, is neither to argue nor to refute argument here. I want to utter just this one truth:﻿—The great, the fundamental need of any nation, any race, is for heroism, devotion, sacrifice; and there cannot be heroism, devotion, or sacrifice in a primarily skeptical spirit. A great man said of France, when she was being lacerated with the frantic stripes of her hysterical children﻿—*France needs a religion!* And the need of France during her trying Revolution is the need of every crisis and conflict in the evolution of nations and races. At such times most of all, do men need to be anchored to what they *feel* to be eternal verities. And nothing else at any time can propel men into those sublime efforts of altruism which constitute the moral heroes of humanity. The demand for heroism, devotion and sacrifice founded on such a faith is particularly urgent in a race at almost the embryonic stage of character-building. The Hour is *now*;﻿—where is the man? He must *believe* in the infinite possibilities of devoted self-sacrifice and in the eternal grandeur of a human idea heroically espoused. It is the enthusiasms, the faiths of the world that have heated the crucibles in which were formed its reformations and its impulses toward a higher growth. And I do not mean by faith the holding of correct views and unimpeachable opinions on mooted questions, merely; nor do I understand it to be the ability to forge cast-iron formulas and dub them **truth**. For while I do not deny that absolute and eternal truth *is*﻿—still truth must be infinite, and as incapable as infinite space, of being encompassed and confined by one age or nation, sect or country﻿—much less by one little creature’s finite brain.

===Chinese===
然而，我自己的目的，在此既不是立论，也不是驳论。我只想说出这样一条真理：——任何民族、任何种族那最重大、最根本的需要，就是对英雄气概、献身精神与牺牲的需要；而在一种以怀疑为本的精神里，不可能有英雄气概、献身与牺牲。当法兰西正被她那些歇斯底里的儿女疯狂鞭笞之时，一位伟人论到她说——*法兰西需要一种宗教！*法兰西在她严峻的革命岁月里的需要，正是各民族、各种族进化途中每一次危机与冲突的需要。正是在这样的时刻，人最需要把自己锚定在他*感到*是永恒真理的事物之上。而且无论何时，唯有这一点才能把人推入那些崇高的利他努力，正是这类努力，造就了人类的道德英雄。对于一个在品格塑造上几乎还处于胚胎阶段的种族，这种建立在信念之上的英雄气概、献身与牺牲的要求尤为紧迫。时机就是*现在*；——其人安在？他必须*相信*虔敬舍己的无穷可能，*相信*一个被英勇担当起来的人类观念那永恒的崇高。正是世间的种种热忱、种种信念，烧热了那些坩埚，而世间诸般革新、以及迈向更高生长的诸般冲力，正是在其中炼成的。我所说的信念，并非仅仅指在聚讼纷纭的问题上持守正确的见解与无可指摘的意见；我也不把它理解为锻造生铁般的公式、再冠之以**真理**之名的本事。因为尽管我不否认绝对的、永恒的真理*存在*——真理却必是无限的，正如无限的空间一样，不能被一个时代或一个民族、一个宗派或一个国家所包揽、所局限——更遑论被一个小小受造物的有限头脑。

===Original===
To me, faith means *treating the truth as true*. Jesus *believed* in the infinite possibilities of an individual soul. His faith was a triumphant realization of the eternal development of *the best* in man﻿—an optimistic vision of the human aptitude for endless expansion and perfectibility. This truth to him placed a sublime valuation on each individual sentiency﻿—a value magnified infinitely by reason of its immortal destiny. He could not lay hold of this truth and let pass an opportunity to lift men into nobler living and firmer building. He could not lay hold of this truth and allow his own benevolence to be narrowed and distorted by the trickeries of circumstance or the colorings of prejudice.

===Chinese===
在我看来，信念意味着*把真理当作真的来对待*。耶稣*相信*一个个体灵魂的无穷可能。他的信念，是对人身上*至善者*永恒开展的一次凯旋式的体认——是对人类禀赋那无尽扩展与可完善性的一幅乐观愿景。在他，这条真理赋予每一个个体的感受性以崇高的估值——这价值又因其不朽的归宿而被无限放大。他既握住这条真理，便不可能放过任何一个机会，把人提升到更高尚的生活、更坚定的建造。他既握住这条真理，便不可能容许自己的仁爱，被境遇的诡诈或偏见的着色所窄化、所扭曲。

===Original===
Life must be something more than dilettante speculation. And religion (ought to be if it isn’t) a great deal more than mere gratification of the instinct for worship linked with the straight-teaching of irreproachable credos. Religion must be *life made true*; and life is action, growth, development﻿—begun now and ending never. And a life made true cannot confine itself﻿—it must reach out and twine around every pulsing interest within reach of its uplifting tendrils. If then you *believe* that intemperance is a growing vice among a people within touch of your sympathies; if you see that, whereas the “Lord had shut them in,” so that from inheritance there are but few cases of alcoholized blood﻿—yet that there is danger of their becoming under their changed circumstances a generation of inebriates﻿—if you believe this, then this is your truth. Take up your parable and in earnestness and faith *give it out* by precept and by example.

===Chinese===
人生必定不止于浅尝者的玄思。而宗教（倘若如今不是，也理应是）更远远不止于满足崇拜的本能、再配上对无可指摘之信条的正统传授。宗教必须是*活得真切的人生*；而人生就是行动、生长、开展——始于当下，永无止境。一个活得真切的人生无法自囿——它必须伸出手去，以自己那些向上攀缘的卷须，缠住够得着的每一桩搏动的关切。那么，倘若你*相信*酗酒正在一个触得着你同情之弦的民族中间滋长为一种恶习；倘若你看到，虽说「主曾把他们关在方舟里头」，以致他们承袭的血里酒精中毒的病例寥寥无几——然而境况既改，他们却有变成一代醉徒的危险——倘若你相信这一点，那这便是你的真理。就请提起你的诗歌，凭着恳切与信心，以言教、以身教把它*传讲出来*。

===Original===
Do you *believe* that the God of history often chooses the weak things of earth to confound the mighty, and that the Negro race in America has a veritable destiny in His eternal purposes﻿—then don’t spend your time discussing the “Negro Problem” amid the clouds of your fine havanna, ensconced in your friend’s well-cushioned armchair and with your patent leather boot-tips elevated to the opposite mantel. Do those poor “cowards in the South” need a leader﻿—then get up and lead them! Let go your purse-strings and begin to *live* your creed. Or is it your modicum of truth that God hath made of one blood all nations of the earth; and that all interests which specialize and contract the broad, liberal, cosmopolitan idea of universal brotherhood and equality are narrow and pernicious, then treat that truth as true. Don’t inveigh against lines of longitude drawn by others when at the same time you are applying your genius to devising lines of latitude which are neither race lines, nor character lines, nor intelligence lines﻿—but certain social-appearance circlets assorting your “universal brotherhood” by shapes of noses and texture of hair. If you object to imaginary lines﻿—don’t draw them! Leave only the real lines of nature and character. And so whatever the vision, the revelation, the idea, vouchsafed *you*,

===Chinese===
你*相信*历史之上帝常常拣选世上软弱的，叫那强壮的羞愧，*相信*美国的黑人种族在他的永恒旨意中确有一份真实的命运——那你就不要把光阴消磨在谈说「黑人问题」上，一边吞吐着你那上等哈瓦那雪茄的烟云，一边安坐在朋友家中软垫厚厚的扶手椅里，漆皮的靴尖翘上对面的壁炉架。南方那些可怜的「懦夫」们需要一位领袖吗——那就起来，去带领他们！松开你的钱囊，开始*活出*你的信条。或者，你那一分真理是：上帝已从一本造出地上万族的人；而凡把普世手足与平等这一宽宏、自由、通达的观念专门化、窄化的种种利益，都是狭隘而有害的——那就把那条真理当作真的来对待。不要一面痛诋别人画下的经线，一面又施展你的天才去发明种种纬线——它们既非种族线，也非品格线，也非智力线——不过是些按鼻形与发质给你的「普世手足」分档归类的社交门面小圈圈。你既然反对想象的线——那就不要去画！只留下自然与品格的真实的线。这样，无论赐给*你*的是怎样的异象、怎样的启示、怎样的观念，

===Original===
> Think it truly and thy thoughts shall the soul’s famine feed.
 

 *Speak* it truly and each word of thine shall be a fruitful seed;
 

 *Live* it truly and thy life shall be a grand and holy creed!

===Chinese===
> 思想得真，你的思想必喂养灵魂的饥馑；
 

 *言说*得真，你的每句话必是结实累累的种子；
 

 *践行*得真，你的一生必是庄严而神圣的信条！

===Original===
Macaulay has left us in his masterly description of Ignatius Loyola a vivid picture of the power of a belief and its independence of material surroundings.

===Chinese===
麦考莱（Macaulay）在对罗耀拉（Ignatius Loyola）的精妙描写中，为我们留下了一幅生动的图景，绘出信念的力量，以及它不系于物质境遇的独立。

===Original===
“On the road from the Theatine convent in Venice might have been seen once a poor crippled Spaniard, wearily but as fast as his injured limbs can carry him making his way toward Rome. His face is pinched, his body shrunken, from long fast and vigil. He enters the City of the Caesars without money, without patrons, without influence! but there burns a light in his eye that recks not of despair. In a frequented portion of a busy street he stops and mounts a stone, and from this rude rostrum begins to address the passers by in barbarous Latin. Lo, there is contagion in the man! He has actually imparted of his spirit to that mottled audience! And now the same fire burns in a hundred eyes, that shone erewhile from his. Men become his willing slaves to do his bidding even unto the ends of the earth. With what courage, what zeal, what utter self-abnegation, with what blind devotion to their ends regardless of means do they preach, teach, write, act! Behind the thrones of kings, at the bedside of paupers, under every disguise in every land, mid pestilence and famine, in prisons oft, in perils by land and perils by sea, the Jesuit, undaunted, pursues his way.”

===Chinese===
「在威尼斯通往泰亚廷会（Theatine）修道院的路上，人们或许曾见到过一个贫穷的跛足西班牙人，疲惫不堪，却竭尽受伤肢体之所能，一路赶奔罗马。长期的斋戒与守夜，使他面颊凹陷、身躯枯瘦。他走进这座凯撒们的城，没有钱，没有奥援，没有权势！然而他眼中燃烧着一团不顾绝望的光。在一条热闹大街上人烟稠密的路段，他停下脚步，登上一块石头，从这个简陋的讲坛上，开始用粗陋的拉丁语向过往行人演说。看哪，这人身上竟有感染之力！他竟真的把自己的精神分给了那形形色色的听众！此刻，方才还只在他眼中闪耀的那团火，已在一百双眼睛里燃烧。人们甘愿做他的奴隶，听凭差遣，虽地极亦所不辞。他们传道、授课、著述、行事，怀着何等的勇气、何等的热忱、何等彻底的舍己，怀着何等只问目的、不择手段的盲目忠诚！在国王们的宝座之后，在贫民的病榻之旁，在每一片土地上以百般伪装出没，身处瘟疫与饥馑之中，屡屡身陷囹圄，陆上有危，海上有险——耶稣会士无所畏惧，一路前行。」

===Original===
Do you seek to know the secret charm of Ignatius Loyola, the hidden spring of the Jesuit’s courage and unfaltering purpose? It is these magic words, “*I believe.*” That is power. That is the stamping attribute in every impressive personality, that is the fire to the engine and the motor force in every battery. That is the live coal from the altar which at once unseals the lips of the dumb﻿—and that alone which makes a man a positive and not a negative quantity in the world’s arithmetic. With this potent talisman man no longer “abideth alone.” He cannot stand apart, a cold spectator of earth’s pulsing struggles. The flame must burst forth. The idea, the doctrine, the device for betterment must be imparted. “*I believe*,”﻿—this was strength and power to Paul, to Mohammed, to the Saxon Monk and the Spanish Zealot﻿—and they must be our strength if our lives are to be worth the living. They mean as much today as they did in the breast of Luther or of Loyola. Who cheats me of this robs me of both shield and spear. Without them I have no inspiration to better myself, no inclination to help another.

===Chinese===
你想知道罗耀拉的秘魅何在、耶稣会士的勇气与不挠意志那暗藏的发条是什么吗？就是这句有魔力的言词：「*我相信。*」那就是力量。那是每一种动人心魄的人格身上那最鲜明的印记，那是引擎之火、每一组电池里的动力。那是祭坛上取下的活炭，一触即启哑者之唇——也唯有它，使一个人在世界的大算术里成为一个正量，而非负量。有了这道灵验的护符，人便不再「独自存留」。他无法袖手旁观、冷眼做尘世悸动纷争的看客。火焰必得喷薄而出。观念、主义、改善的方略必得传递于人。「*我相信*」——这于保罗（Paul）、于穆罕默德（Mohammed）、于那位萨克森修士与那位西班牙狂热者，都曾是力量与权能——倘若我们的生命要活得有所值，它们也必得成为我们的力量。这两个字今天的分量，不减于它们当日在于路德（Luther）或罗耀拉胸中的分量。谁骗走了我这个，便是既夺走了我的盾、又夺走了我的矛。没有了它们，我便既无完善自我的灵感，亦无帮助他人的心愿。

===Original===
It is small service to humanity, it seems to me, to open men’s eyes to the fact that the world rests on nothing. Better the turtle of the myths, than a *perhaps*. If “fooled they must be, though wisest of the wise,” let us help to make them the fools of virtue. You may have learned that the pole star is twelve degrees from the pole and forbear to direct your course by it﻿—preferring your needle taken from earth and fashioned by man’s device. The slave brother, however, from the land of oppression once saw the celestial beacon and dreamed not that it ever deviated from due North. He *believed* that *somewhere* under its beckoning light, lay a far away country where a man’s a man. He sets out with his heavenly guide before his face﻿—would you tell him he is pursuing a wandering light? Is he the poorer for his ignorant hope? Are you the richer for your enlightened suspicion?

===Chinese===
在我看来，让人们睁开眼睛看见世界无所依凭这一事实，于人类实在算不上什么大的效劳。神话里那只驮着世界的乌龟，也胜过一个「*也许*」。倘若「任凭他们智高一世，也免不了受骗」，那就让我们帮他们做受美德之骗的傻瓜吧。你或许已经获知，北极星离天极尚差十二度，于是你不肯再凭它定向航程——宁可信赖你那从地里采出、由人的机巧造出的指针。然而，那位来自压迫之地的奴隶兄弟，曾望见那座上天的灯塔，做梦也不曾想到它会偏离正北。他*相信*，在它招引的光芒之下的*某处*，有一个远方的国度，在那里人总是人。他把上天的向导举在面前，踏上了路途——你要告诉他，他追逐的是一颗游移的光么？他因自己无知的希望而更贫穷了么？你因自己开明的怀疑而更富足了么？

===Original===
Yes, I believe there is existence beyond our present experience; that that existence is conscious and culturable; and that there is a noble work here and now in helping men to live *into* it.

===Chinese===
是的，我相信，在我们当下的经验之外另有存在；我相信那存在是有意识的、可经修养而成就的；我也相信，此时此地便有一项高贵的工作——帮助人们*活进*那存在里去。

===Original===
> “Not in Utopia﻿—subterraneous fields﻿—
 

 Or some secreted island, Heaven knows where!
 

 But in this very world, which is the world
 

 Of all of us﻿—the place where in the end
 

 We find our happiness, or not at all!”

===Chinese===
> 「不在乌托邦——不在地底的幽壤——
 

 不在什么隐秘的海岛，天知道在何处！
 

 就在这个世界里——这世界乃是我们
 

 大家共同的世界——在这里，我们终究
 

 寻得我们的幸福，否则便一无所得！」

===Original===
There are nations still in darkness to whom we owe a light. The world is to be moved one generation forward﻿—whether by us, by blind force, by fate, or by God! If thou believest, all things are possible; and *as* thou believest, so be it unto thee.

===Chinese===
世上仍有身居黑暗的民族，我们欠他们一盏灯。这个世界要被向前推进一代——无论靠我们，靠盲目的力量，靠命运，还是靠上帝！你若信，凡事都可能；并且，*照着*你所信的，给你成全。
